# Part 3 — The Namespace

**Decimal range** `3.x` · **62 sections** · **page budget 23pp** · [← master index](../README.md)

> The dimension namespace, reserved prefixes, the consent family, and the subject_kind catalogue.

---

## 3.1 `namespace` — The dimension namespace

A federation needs a shared vocabulary before it can have a shared judgment. The dimension namespace is that vocabulary: the union of what every sibling component commits to, each family registered once under one owning component. CEG does not author the namespace top-down. It owns its own slice ([CC 3.1.1](#3.1.1)) and consumes everyone else's, so that justice — fair access to capability — and integrity — auditable claims — rest on names every participant agrees on. The family count is **generated, not asserted**: the namespace generator extracts every leaf family from this Part into [`manifests/namespace_registry.json`](../manifests/namespace_registry.json), whose `_meta` carries the totals, the per-component breakdown and the hash of the source it parsed — read the count there, never from a number restated in prose. **Eight** owning components commit to their families in their own mission statements; **CIRISBench** ([CC 3.1.10](part_3_the_namespace.md)) is catalogued without one and is the ninth.

This section catalogs every prefix family, organized by owning component, each under the component that commits to the concept.

### 3.1.1 `registry` — CIRISRegistry — identity / build / license / partner

**Steward**: CIRISRegistry, folding into CIRISServer as the conferred registry slice (the catalogue row's `owning_repo` follows the fold, per [CC 3.1.7](part_3_the_namespace.md) R1).

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `licensure:{authority_id}` | License status for a key under a named authority (`authority_id` is the licence's **provenance** — [CC 2.4.1.2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)). Status vocabulary (open per [CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)); canonical: `issued` · `probation` · `restricted` · `suspended` · `revoked` · `lapsed` · `surrendered` · `reduced`. **`suspended` is reversible; `revoked` is terminal** — the one operational distinction a consumer MUST honour, and a status a consumer MUST NOT infer from the other. **More than one status MAY be live concurrently** (active-on-probation is two facts, not one blended state), so a consumer composes the set rather than reading a scalar. Co-stewarded with Verify for the CIRIS-issued licence ([CC 3.4.9](part_3_the_namespace.md)) — composition, **not** a reservation: the family is open-emitter. | signed | No |
| `duty:{kind}` | **An obligation attached to a permission** — the thing a permission-and-prohibition grammar cannot express, and the thing every widely-used content licence rests on (attribution, share-alike, carry-restrictions-downstream). `{kind}` open vocabulary per [CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md); canonical kinds follow ODRL 2.2: `compensate` · `attribute` · `inform` · `obtain_consent` · `ensure_exclusivity` · `delete` · `next_policy` · `anonymize` · `review_policy`. A duty **states** the obligation; its **discharge** is evidenced by the existing `commitment_fulfillment:{prior_contribution_id}` ([CC 3.1.9.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)) rather than a second family, and its **breach** routes to `hard_case:duty_unmet` ([CC 3.1.9.4](part_3_the_namespace.md)) — adjudication, never automatic `slashing:*`. Rides `scores`; no new structural primitive. | signed | No |
| `partner_role:{role}` | Partner status (`community` / `community_plus` / `professional_medical` / `professional_legal` / `professional_financial` / `professional_full` — lowercase per [CC 3.1.7](part_3_the_namespace.md) R3). | enumerated | No |
| `revocation:{entity_type}:{reason}` | Entity revocation (`agent` / `partner` / `license`). Immediate, non-rollbackable. | -1 only | No |
| `bond_posted:{currency}` | A bond posted for Sybil resistance (proof of bond); forfeited on revocation. | positive-only | No |
| `build:registered:{target}` | Build manifest registered against the directory (precondition for L4 attestation). | boolean-via-score | No |
| `multilateral_participation:{forum}:{kind}` | Depth of a partner's participation across federated bodies. `{forum}` = named federated body or compact; `{kind}` ∈ `membership` \| `voting` \| `proposal_filing` \| `observer_status`. | signed | No |
| `agent_files:{kind}:{platform_or_target}` | **Joint claim with [CC 3.1.9.1](#3.1.9.1) NodeCore.** Canonical-attester rule: attestations by the accord-conferred **steward keys** of `ciris-canonical` ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md); human-held, never the installs') constitute the CIRIS canonical default-trust state. Anti-tricking guarantee at `registry.ciris-services-1.ai/install` per [CC 4.4.3.7](part_4_composition_governance.md) trust-composition policy. Open Contribution channel; consumer policy composes via [CC 4.4.3.7](part_4_composition_governance.md) trust layers. | signed | No |
| `ownership:{relation}:{target_kind}:{version}` | The owner-binding made observable as a score. Canonical leaf `ownership:responsible_party:node:v1` — names the single live responsible steward of an owned `node`/`agent` key. Mirrors, never replaces, the [CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) single-owner `delegates_to(user → key, delegation_purpose: owner_binding)`: cardinality ≠ 1 is an error, not a set to reduce. Reclaim/seizure ride the [CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) recovery path. | boolean-via-score | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `device:label` | **The name a person gives one of their own devices** (not `self:device_label`: `self` is a cohort scope and names no family). A `scores` row signed by the owner about an identity occurrence the owner holds, at `cohort_scope: self`, the name in the envelope's `label`; a rename is a `supersedes` of the previous row and the newest wins. It is display text: it confers nothing, no gate reads it, and it never leaves the owner's self cohort. The `device:` stem is closed to this one leaf. | positive-only | **Yes:** owner-signed, about an occurrence the signer owns; `self` scope only |
| `takedown_notice:{basis}` | **A takedown notice as a row** (shape and bases at [CC 3.3.2](#3.3.2)): the `scores` row whose envelope is the `takedown_notice` payload, `subject_kind: takedown_notice`, so that every notice has an `attestation_id` a tombstone cites and a `reconsideration:{grounds}` targets ([CC 4.5.5](part_4_composition_governance.md)). One family across bases; `{basis}` is the **closed** `LegalBasis` wire string: `{basis}` ∈ `dmca512` \| `dsa_article16` \| `tvec_terrorist` \| `ncmec_csam` \| `gifct_cip` \| `community_standards` \| `perceptual_hash_csam` \| `osa_illegal_content` \| `avmsd_age_inappropriate` \| `court_order` \| `steward_purge`. A `steward_purge` notice is a **cosigned row** ([CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md)): `attesting_key_id` is the seat that proposes it — any seat, so no single holder's reachability is load-bearing — the other seats ride in `additional_scrubs[]` over the same canonical bytes, the quorum is evaluated over attester + cosigners under the root's `consensus_protocol`, and the family is derived from the verified signers and MUST resolve to the `claimant_key_id` root ([CC 3.4.7](#3.4.7)); the purge's signing payload is this row's canonical bytes. The `takedown_notice:` stem is closed to this family. | -1 only | **Yes:** duty-holder-signed under the [CC 4.5.5](part_4_composition_governance.md) enforced-admission rule for the legal bases; cosigned by the trust root for `steward_purge` |
| `community:listing` | **A public community's own discovery row** ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) `public`): a federation-scoped `scores` row by the founder carrying the room's name, description, `cohort_subkind`, admission policy, named moderator and `community_key_id`, ranked by NodeCore votes. Wire `community:listing:v1`. It is the community's row, not a member's `listed: public` flag, and it names no roster. A rename or policy change is a `supersedes`; closing the room is a `withdraws`. The `community:` stem is closed to this leaf. | positive-only | **Yes:** signed by the founder (or a current `moderate`-holder) of the community it names; one live row per community |
| `trust:{job}:{version}` | The trust-root conferral and acceptance edges, carried as `dimension` values on `delegates_to` rows — the wire type is unchanged, so 1+4 is untouched. Three canonical jobs, named apart so a reader never infers one from another: `trust:charter:v1` (root→root self-declaration — the capability ceiling of the trust domain), `trust:confers:v1` (root→subject grant), `trust:accepts:v1` (node→root acceptance — the one-row un-trust lever). Retracted by `withdraws`/`recants`, never by a negative score. Conferral planes, the acceptance-edge rule and the liveness banding: [CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md). | positive-only | **Yes — [CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `accord:invoke:constitutional:{halt_id}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); the kill-switch invocation, semantics and canonical bytes at [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) / [CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md). Lowercase per R3 (not `CONSTITUTIONAL`). | +1.0 only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `accord:invoke:notify:{notify_id}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); federation-wide accord-holder communication, [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | +1.0 only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `accord:invoke:drill:{drill_id}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); accord-holder exercise, [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | +1.0 only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `accord:lifecycle` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); the accord heartbeat, wire `accord:lifecycle:v1`, [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | +1.0 only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `accord:lifecycle:confirmed` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); a strict majority's confirmation of a one-holder `constitutional` halt within its fuse, [CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md) / [CC 4.2.1.3](part_4_composition_governance.md). | +1.0 only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `accord:lifecycle:active` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); resumption from a `constitutional` halt, [CC 4.2.1.3](part_4_composition_governance.md). | +1.0 only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `accord:human_dignity` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); the accord-holder verdict on an agent's human-dignity conduct, [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `accord:*` | **Reserved** — only `identity_type=accord_holder` may emit. **Closed**: the rows above are its whole leaf set. One of several reserved families in this table alone (`ownership:*`, `trust:*`), and more at [CC 3.4](part_3_the_namespace.md); the generated registry is the count of record. What is distinctive is the *keying*: this is the one reservation held by a **closed roster slot** rather than by a role conferred on the delegation plane, so no root can confer it. The roster occupying the slot is an instance parameter per the [CC 1](part_1_foundation.md) reading rule. | see [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) | **Yes — [CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |

### 3.1.2 `attestation` — CIRISVerify — attestation ladder, provenance, transparency

**Steward**: CIRISVerify. These prefixes serve integrity: each is a checkable claim about how far a build, key, or license has climbed the trust ladder.

| Prefix | Description | Polarity |
|---|---|---|
| `attestation:self_verify` | Running CIRISVerify binary attests itself against its function manifest. (Consumer-side ladder: corresponds to L1; see CC 4.4.3.6 Policy I.) | boolean-via-score |
| `attestation:hardware_rooted` | Hardware-rooted attestation (TPM 2.0 / Android Keystore / iOS Secure Enclave). (Ladder L2.) | boolean-via-score |
| `attestation:registry_consensus` | 2-of-3 multi-source registry consensus on key / build / license validity. (Ladder L3.) An `Indeterminate` consensus is no row, never a third point; a consumer resolves the absence fail-secure (RESTRICTED). | boolean-via-score |
| `attestation:license_validity` | License-validity claim (Registry-signed, Verify-verified). (Ladder L4.) | boolean-via-score |
| `attestation:agent_integrity` | Agent source-tree byte-equal against registered manifest. (Ladder L5.) | boolean-via-score |
| `provenance:slsa:{level}` | SLSA build provenance levels 1-3. Registry emits these on build registration; Verify consumes them as `AttestBundle.provenance.slsa_level`. | boolean-via-score |
| `provenance:build_manifest:{target}` | Per-target canonical-staged-runtime manifest hash equality. Each `BuildManifest` is hybrid-signed (Ed25519 + ML-DSA-65) by the per-primitive steward. | boolean-via-score |
| `provenance:build_manifest:{target}:locale:{lang_code}` | Per-locale signed sub-manifest within a target's manifest tree. Parent target manifest is Merkle root over per-locale leaves. A non-power-of-two leaf count is padded by repeating the last leaf to the next power of two — not RFC 6962's split, and not the per-level duplication of [CC 6.1.1](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md#6.1.1); the leaves and parents carry RFC 6962's `0x00` / `0x01` prefixes, and the list is one leaf per locale. Detection surface for locale-targeted attacks. Canonical-bytes spec at [CC 3.1.2.1](#3.1.2.1). | boolean-via-score |
| `provenance:skill_import:{source}` | Community-skill import provenance. `{source}` ∈ `registry:{registry_id}` \| `direct:{url}` \| `local:{path}`. Envelope: `{skill_manifest_sha256, signer_identity, import_timestamp, capability_declaration}`. Canonical-bytes spec at [CC 3.1.2.1](#3.1.2.1). | signed |
| `transparency_log:inclusion` | RFC 6962 inclusion proof for an audit leaf. | boolean-via-score |
| `transparency_log:consistency` | RFC 6962 consistency proof between two STHs. | boolean-via-score |
| `transparency_log:cosigned:{tree_size}` | Witness cosignature on an STH (substrate-conformance path; see [CC 5.3.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md) endpoints). Witness-emitted, verify-recognized — emitter rule (`identity_type="witness"`) at CC 3.4.9. | signed |
| `rollback_detected:{revision_field}` | Anti-rollback — decrease in revocation revision. | -1 only |
| `cert_validity:{authority}` | Validity of a certification authority's signature. A certification authority's validity self-attestation is a **producer claim** ([CC 4.2.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)); the trust root itself is the GenesisBundle — tamper-evident, and authentic only against the consumer's out-of-band anchor ([CC 5.3.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md), [CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) T5) — never a steward-key self-report. | boolean-via-score |
| `hardware_custody:{platform}` | Where the key's seed lives — the custody **mechanism**, not the certified class ([CC 4.2.2](part_4_composition_governance.md) `hardware_class` answers that, and is a JSON property, never a value of `{platform}`). `{platform}` ∈ `android_keystore` \| `android_strongbox` \| `ios_secure_enclave` \| `mac_os_secure_enclave` \| `tpm_discrete` \| `tpm_firmware` \| `intel_sgx` \| `software_only` \| `aws_cloud_hsm` \| `azure_hsm` \| `gcp_cloud_hsm` \| `yubi_hsm` \| `external_secure_element` — **closed**, the one enumeration every custody policy keys on; an emitter that spells a run-together form (`tpmdiscrete`) or a retired token (`tpm`, `android`, `software_fallback`) is refused, never folded. | boolean-via-score |
| `identity:canonical_binding:{canonical_hash}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); the claim that federation key K is the identity behind canonical hash H, semantics and admission consequence at [CC 3.3.14](part_3_the_namespace.md). Carries no version segment (`_meta.case_rule.version_segment.exempt`): the suffix is the hash. | positive-only |

#### Assurance ladders (registry rows; rules at CC 3.4.11 / CC 3.4.12)

Both ladders are attestation ladders in the sense of this section — a registered witness climbs a rung about a subject — and both are ruled in [CC 3.4](part_3_the_namespace.md) and catalogued here, as [CC 3.1.7](part_3_the_namespace.md) R1 requires of any gated family. The subject-signed `age_self_declared:` counterpart is catalogued here too: it is *not* witness-reserved, but it carries an admission rule at [CC 3.4.11](part_3_the_namespace.md), so R1 requires its row.

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `age_assurance:{level}:{band}:{version}` | Witness rung of the age ladder — `{level}` ∈ `provider` \| `government` (closed; `self` is refused here), `{band}` ∈ `minor` \| `adult` \| `under_13` \| `13_15` \| `16_17` (closed — the [CC 3.4.13](part_3_the_namespace.md) Q1 finer bands spelled as the substrate's `AgeBandFine`; `unknown` is a consumer's fail-secure *resolution*, never a wire token), canonical `{version}` = `v1`. There is no `self` level on this prefix: the self rung rides `age_self_declared:` below. A consumer unions both and the witness rung outranks the self rung. Full ladder + protective gate at [CC 3.4.11](part_3_the_namespace.md). | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.11](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `age_self_declared:band:{band}:{version}` | Subject's own age-band declaration — `{band}` ∈ `minor` \| `adult` \| `under_13` \| `13_15` \| `16_17` (closed — the [CC 3.4.13](part_3_the_namespace.md) Q1 finer bands spelled as the substrate's `AgeBandFine`; `unknown` is a consumer's fail-secure *resolution*, never a wire token), canonical `{version}` = `v1`. Carries **no** `{level}` token: its level is `self` by construction, and a level token on this prefix is malformed. The literal `band` segment marks the rung (`age_self_declared:band:adult:v1`); the two-segment `age_self_declared:{band}:{version}` is not a registered shape. Non-reserved but not open-sender: admitted only where the signer acts for the subject, per [CC 3.4.11](part_3_the_namespace.md). | signed | No |
| `capacity_assurance:{level}:{domain}:{band}:{version}` | Witness rung of the adult decision-capacity ladder ([CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md)): a registered qualified assessor attests a per-domain band; the subject cannot self-emit it and the proposed steward cannot be the attester. `{level}` ∈ `provider` \| `panel` \| `government` (closed — the two reversible-cause companions below are their own rows, never a `{level}`); `{band}` ∈ `capacitated` \| `incapacitated` (closed); `{domain}` is the open per-domain vocabulary. Aggregation across domains is forbidden — capacity is a vector, never collapsed to a scalar. | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `capacity_assurance:reversible_excluded:{domain}:{version}` | Reversible-cause exclusion companion ([CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md)): the assessor attests that delirium, infection-confusion, depression and polypharmacy are ruled out for the domain. A mandatory predicate on any continuing `incapacitated` rung; witness-reserved like its parent. | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `capacity_assurance:reversible_pending:{domain}:{version}` | Acute-window companion ([CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md)): exclusion in progress, not skipped; admissible for the T1 emergency-necessity tier only, and the binding MUST lapse unless it resolves to `reversible_excluded` within its `valid_until`. Witness-reserved. | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |

#### 3.1.2.1 `provenance` — Canonical-bytes contracts for provenance primitives

A provenance claim is only as trustworthy as its preimage is reproducible. The two contracts below pin the exact bytes a producer signs and a consumer recomputes, so a skill import or a per-locale manifest verifies identically across implementations.

#### The build-manifest Contribution (normative reading)

A build manifest is published as **one** claim: a `scores` Contribution on `provenance:build_manifest:{target}`, signed by the pipeline that built it, citing the manifest's bytes from `evidence_refs[]`. `agent_files:build:{target}` ([CC 3.1.9.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) is **a different claim by a different attester** — the canonical attester's statement that an agent may load that file set, composed under [CC 4.4.3.7](part_4_composition_governance.md) — and cites the same bytes; a pipeline does not emit it, and emitting both from one key would be two claims about one fact. The Contribution's `build` descriptor is the descriptor [CC 5.3.2.5](part_5_transport_substrate.md) binds: it carries the manifest blob's byte length, **`manifest_size`**, beside `manifest_hash`, so a fetcher checks size before hashing. Its `cohort_scope` is `federation`. The pipeline's **standing** is not a member the producer writes about itself: it is evaluated by the reader under [CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) T2, on the plane that conferred it and never an inferred one — either a live `trust:confers:v1` grant carrying `infra:attest` (a key root's delegation, or a keyless family root's quorum-scrubbed grant), or a **co-scrub of `infra:attest` onto the pipeline's own key record** by the root's conferring roster reaching its `consensus_protocol` — the ceremony plane, the same act that confers root identity, and for the shipped root the accord co-scrub by a strict majority of the standing accord roster that [CC 4.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md) enumerates. Either is resolved at use against a root the reader trusts, a reader MUST evaluate both planes before finding a pipeline without standing, and a read route returns the signed claim with the bytes so the reader can re-verify it ([CC 5.3.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md): nothing a serving install signs on its own account is a root). A node's statement that **it runs** a given build is not a second claim on this dimension and needs no new family: it is the build attestation the trust ladder already carries — `attestation:self_verify` for the verifier itself and `attestation:agent_integrity` for an agent's tree, above — signed by the running node about itself and citing the manifest's Contribution: `evidence_refs[]` carries the manifest **blob's** digest (the bytes a holder serves), and `references_attestation_id` carries the manifest Contribution's row id — the member cites a row on any attestation type, and takes composer meaning only on `supersedes` / `withdraws` / `recants`. A rebuild is a `supersedes` on the earlier Contribution, and a bad build is withdrawn by the pipeline that signed it.

#### `SkillImportManifest` canonical bytes (v2 — normative)

```
canonical_bytes = sha256( JCS( {
  "domain": "ciris.skill_import.v2", // domain separation; pinned literal
  "source": source_string,
  "skill_manifest_sha256": sha256_hex_lowercase, // per CC 2.6.3
  "signer_identity": signer_key_id, // per CC 2.6.3
  "import_timestamp": rfc3339_canonical, // per CC 2.6.2
  "capability_declaration": sorted_capability_array, // JSON array of capability strings, sorted
    // lexicographically by byte representation BEFORE canonicalization — JCS canonicalizes
    // arrays in place and does not reorder elements, so the sort is part of the signed preimage
  "valid_until": rfc3339_canonical // OPTIONAL — CC 2.6.1.1 omit rule: absent if unset
}))
```

Hybrid signature: Ed25519 over `canonical_bytes`; ML-DSA-65 over `canonical_bytes || ed25519_signature_bytes` (bound payload). All [CC 2.6.1.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md) determinism rules apply (hex per CC 2.6.3, timestamps per CC 2.6.2, omit-vs-materialize per CC 2.6.1.1).

#### Per-locale Merkle composition (v2 — normative)

```
leaf_hash[lang_code] = sha256(
  0x00 || // RFC 6962 leaf-domain prefix (binary, outside the JSON)
  JCS( {
    "domain": "ciris.locale_manifest.v2", // domain separation; pinned literal
    "target": target_string,
    "locale": lang_code,
    "files_root": files_merkle_root_hex_lowercase, // per CC 2.6.3
    "build_id": build_id,
    "signer_identity": signer_key_id // per CC 2.6.3
  }))

parent_hash(left, right) = sha256(
  0x01 || // RFC 6962 parent-domain prefix
  left || right)
```

Locale ordering: lexicographic by ISO 639-1 / BCP 47 byte representation; `"polyglot"` sorts last. Padding: repeat the last leaf to the next power of two (not RFC 6962's split, and not the per-level duplication of [CC 6.1.1](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md#6.1.1)); one leaf per locale, so no leaf repeats except by padding.

Producers MUST emit v2. The closed-vocabulary [CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md) accord-invocation encoding is intentionally distinct: its preimage is a discriminator + nonce + enum fields with no attacker-controlled free text, so the injection surface this JCS form closes is not reachable there, and genesis-critical bytes stay stable.

### 3.1.3 `persist` — CIRISPersist — substrate health

**Steward**: CIRISPersist. These dimensions are substrate-self-reports — emittable only by the running Persist instance, which is what makes them honest: the substrate cannot be made to lie about its own health by a third party.

`system:*` is one family, reserved to the substrate component ([CC 3.4.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)) and registered once, here: CIRISPersist emits on it with `identity_type="substrate_persist"` and CIRISEdge ([CC 3.1.4](part_3_the_namespace.md)) with `identity_type="substrate_edge"`.

Canonical leaves: `system:*`, `audit_chain:hash_continuity`, `corpus_health:n_eff_measurable`, `identity_continuity:relational_anchor`, `federation_directory:replication_lag`. Polarity: signed. Authors: see [CC 8.1](part_8_appendices.md) Persist leaf glossary for narrative-name → canonical-leaf mapping.

#### Settlement and ledgers (registry rows; semantics at [CC 3.3.10](part_3_the_namespace.md) and [CC 3.3.10.1](part_3_the_namespace.md))

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `settlement:*` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); the optional, privacy-scoped linkage of a federation action to its off-stack settlement — a bare `scores` against the paid Contribution, or the `settlement` subject_kind ceremony when an explicit receipt is wanted ([CC 3.3.10](part_3_the_namespace.md)). Open leaves (`settlement:paid:v1` canonical); `cohort_scope` is the producer's choice. CEG records that a settlement happened and what it paid for; the chain settles the value. | signed | No |
| `ledger:head:{unit}:{version}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); an in-grammar ledger's **head** — sequence, unit and the declared anchoring cadence ([CC 3.3.10.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) clauses 1–4). `{unit}` is the ledger's unit (`external`: an ISO 4217 code or a unit the ledger declares); wire `ledger:head:USD:v1`. | signed | **Yes:** the ledger's single owner (the steward-bound identity of its `ledger_id` triple) or a delegate under its declared serialization discipline |
| `ledger:checkpoint:{unit}:{version}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); a co-witnessed balance snapshot at a sequence number, superseding the detail below it ([CC 3.3.10.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) clause 5); the conservation fold is provable from the latest checkpoint forward. | signed | **Yes:** as `ledger:head` |
| `ledger:promotion:{version}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); promotion of a ledger to cohort visibility, carrying the compliance proof — chain validity from the latest witnessed checkpoint to the promoted head ([CC 3.3.10.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) clause 6). | signed | **Yes:** as `ledger:head` |

#### The planes as rows (registry rows; cosigned rows, [CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md))

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `group:roster` | A family's or community's **roster version**: the signed roster record at a version that [CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) T6 makes the lineage head, as a cosigned `scores` row — `attested_key_id` the group, envelope carrying the fold (seats, roles, `effective_at`s, bounds), `prev_head_digest` and `charter_digest`. Wire `group:roster:v1`. A holder's recovery version ([CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md)) is the one single-signer version. | positive-only | **Yes — cosigned under the group's `consensus_protocol`** (the founder quorum; the accord's roster-change threshold for the accord family); withdrawn only by a cosigned `withdraws` |
| `key:record` | **A key's claim of itself**: `attesting_key_id` = `attested_key_id` = the key, envelope carrying `pubkey_ed25519_base64`, `pubkey_ml_dsa_65_base64`, `identity_type` and roles. Wire `key:record:v1`. Admitted by **proof of possession** against the keys it carries — the one exception to resolving the attester through a held row. Rebind or rotation is `supersedes`; an anchor scrub, a root conferral or an accord co-scrub (`infra:attest`, conferred roles) is a cosignature under [CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md). | positive-only | **Yes — self only, by possession**; every other row is admitted only once its signer's `key:record` is held (retryable otherwise) |
| `identity:occurrence` | An identity's occurrence record ([CC 3.3.6](part_3_the_namespace.md)): device class, encryption pubkeys, transport binding. Wire `identity:occurrence:v1`. | positive-only | **Yes:** signed by the identity or a live occurrence of it (`signer_acts_for`) |
| `key:revocation` | A key-plane revocation ([CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)): subject `revoked_key_sha256_ed25519_raw`, `revoked_after` only ever lowering across versions. Wire `key:revocation:v1`. **Never withdrawn** (non-rollbackable, [CC 3.1.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)). | -1 only | **Yes:** the revoker's authority is judged without the subject's record; a `withdraws` naming it is refused |
| `identity:occurrence_revocation` | **The carrier of a framed occurrence revocation** (registered under CC 3.1.7 R2(a), on the planes-as-rows pattern of `key:record` / `key:revocation`): a framed `SignedIdentityOccurrenceRevocation` signed outside the two-path roster door ([CC 3.3.6.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)) is kept as a local projection that still revokes but has no attestation row and so could never replicate — a node added later would see the revoked occurrence as live. Persist mints this row from the signed object it verified: attester = the revoker, the framed object under one envelope member, the row's signature columns the object's own hybrid signature, id = sha256(domain ‖ JCS(object)); a receiver re-derives the row and refuses any byte difference (domain `ciris:identity:occurrence_revocation:v1:`). **The admission condition is structural, never an instant** (a framed object's instant is signer-chosen, so "signed before the door" is unverifiable and a thief holding one device could mint a backdated revocation of the owner's other device): the carrier is admitted **(A)** for an occurrence of a `node` or `agent` identity, which is single-signer in any case, and **(B)** for an occurrence of a user self only when cosigned by a **strict majority of the self's current live roster** ([CC 3.3.6.3](part_3_the_namespace.md), the quorum path), so a framed revocation rides the door it would otherwise bypass; a user-self carrier without that quorum is refused; the substrate reports the refusal to the owner, who re-proposes through the quorum path. A new revocation goes through the two paths, so the family confers no authority that did not exist. Wire `identity:occurrence_revocation:v1`. **Never withdrawn.** | -1 only | **Yes:** the revoker, judged as the framed object's signer; for a user self, cosigned by a strict majority of the live roster |
| `transport:canonical_rebind` | **The holders' re-bind of a seated serve node's transport destination and transit pair** (registered under CC 3.1.7 R2(a); the quorum act of [CC 3.3.6.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) and [CC 5.3.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md)): an attestation row about a seated serve node, authored by an accord holder and cosigned in `additional_scrubs[]`, admitted at the accord charter's threshold, carrying the node's new `SignedTransportDestination` under `transport_destination`. It lands Rooted on every node that holds the root and **supersedes the bundle-seeded binding**, so a transit-pair rotation is a quorum act and not a re-mint; a re-bind below the threshold is refused `transport_rebind_without_holder_quorum`, and a seated node's unilateral rotation that would retire the pinned pair before a re-bind has landed is refused at its own rotation door, `serve_transit_pair_rotation_before_rebind`. Wire `transport:canonical_rebind:v1`. | positive-only | **Yes — the accord holders, cosigned at the charter's M**; never a single holder, never the node itself |

#### Key wraps (registry row)

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `key_grant:{axis}` | One wrap of one data-encryption key to one recipient ([CC 3.3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)): `{axis}` ∈ `content` \| `epoch` \| `stream` — closed; wire `key_grant:{axis}:v1`. `attested_key_id` is the recipient occurrence; the envelope carries the key's identity on that axis and the wrap (`wrap_algorithm` v2, `wrapped_dek`). Union by identity, never withdrawn, placed at the content's scope. | positive-only | **Yes — sealer-only:** `attesting_key_id` speaks for the content's or stream's owner (`content`, `stream`) or is the epoch's minter, an active member occurrence at `asserted_at` (`epoch`); a `withdraws` naming the row is refused |

#### Retired delegation labels (registry rows — closed)

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `self:delegates_to` | **Closed label, no job.** A dimension value carried on `delegates_to` rows of the Self-at-login composition ([CC 4.4.3.4.3](part_4_composition_governance.md)) only so the row carried a version tail. It claims **no job**: a reader treats a row carrying it as unlabeled and infers nothing from it. Registered so stored and in-flight rows resolve; no new leaf is ever added beneath it. | positive-only | **Yes — closed:** only on an owner's own `delegates_to` row; confers nothing |
| `self:delegates_to:agent_occurrence` | **Closed label, no job.** The same, on the row delegating to the person's agent occurrence. | positive-only | **Yes — closed:** as above |

A delegation that claims a job rides `trust:{job}` ([CC 3.1.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)): a scope-carrying grant is `trust:confers:v1`. These two labels are not a `self:` family — `self` is a `cohort_scope`, and a claim's namespace should not read as one. The `self:` stem is therefore **closed to these two**: any other dimension beginning `self:` is refused `namespace_family_unregistered`, never admitted as open vocabulary (a device's name is `device:label`, [CC 3.1.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)).

#### Lineage-witness consent (registry row)

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lineage_witness:{stage}` | The ceremony by which a node becomes a witness of a trust-root lineage ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) T6), one attestation-plane row per step: `{stage}` ∈ `proposal` \| `acceptance` \| `decline` — closed. A **proposal** is a founder's invitation naming the lineage and exactly one node key in `subject_key_ids`, with a required `expires_at`. An **acceptance** or **decline** is the answer of that node's **owner**, naming the proposal by `references_attestation_id` and `proposal_hash`. The shape follows the membership ceremony ([CC 3.1.3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)) and is a different family on purpose: a witness is not a member, holds no seat and gains no access. The row confers nothing by itself — it is the precondition for the charter re-scrub that lists the key. | positive-only | **Yes — per-leaf emitter:** a proposal is an active founder's of the lineage named; an acceptance or decline is signed by the owner of the node named (or a key that acts for the owner), never by the node and never by a founder |

#### The mesh census and the secret poll (registry rows; semantics at [CC 6.1.9](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md#6.1.9))

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `census:share:{class}` | **A node's census return, one share of two** ([CC 6.1.9](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md#6.1.9)): an **addressed** row whose signed `for_key_id` is one of the period's two serve seats, carrying `period`, `return_token`, `share_index` ∈ {0, 1}, `expires_at` = the period's end, and an opaque body (the one-hot share and its proof share). Held by the author and the recipient only; any other node refuses it `federation_addressed_not_recipient`. Refusals: `census_return_recipient_not_seat` (the recipient is not a serve seat), `census_return_expired` (offered after the period's end), `census_share_index_invalid`, `census_share_duplicate_token` (a second share with the same `(return_token, share_index)` in a period at the same seat). `{class}` is the scope class the return describes. | signed | **Yes — addressed:** the returning node's key; reach is the named seat only |
| `census:partial:{class}` | **One aggregator's field-wise sum of the shares it accepted** for a period: `period`, `share_index`, `share_count`, `member_commitment_hex` over the accepted tokens, `mass_total`, `aggregation_algorithm_id`, and a body of field elements. Signed by a serve seat (`census_partial_signer_not_seat`); refused after the period's composite (`census_partial_period_closed`); a token with one share of two at period close is dropped and reported `census_share_pair_incomplete`. Federation scope. | signed | **Yes — seat:** a serve seat of the period |
| `census:composite:{class}` | **The mesh census for a class and period**: a `scores` row naming both partials (`partial_a`, `partial_b`) and carrying the [CC 6.1.2.1](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md#6.1.2.1) inputs — `version = 3`, `source_count`, `n_eff`, `max_source_multiplicity`, `mass_commitment_hex`, `member_commitment_hex`, `aggregation_algorithm_id`, optional `noise_floor_descriptor` — with `mass_total` and the summed histograms. Signed by a serve seat (`census_composite_signer_not_seat`). Refusals: `census_composite_below_k` (`source_count < 10`), `census_composite_partials_mismatch` (the partials differ in commitment or share count, or `mass_total` is not their sum), `census_composite_mass_total_mismatch` (the cells do not sum to `mass_total`), `census_composite_duplicate_period` (a second composite for the class and period, from any seat), `aggregation_meta_dominated`, `aggregation_meta_multiplicity`. Federation scope. | signed | **Yes — seat:** a serve seat of the period; one per class and period |
| `census:member:share` | **A member's community cell, one share of two**: addressed to one of the period's two tally members of the room (`ballot_share_recipient_not_tally_member`), emitted every period at a fixed size whether or not a poll is open; the body is sealed under the community's epoch key and carries the active bit, a one-hot over an open poll's options plus *none*, and the padding; `poll_id` lives only in the body. `ballot_share_duplicate_token` and `ballot_share_pair_incomplete` as for the census share. Community scope. | signed | **Yes — addressed:** a member of the room; reach is the named tally member only |
| `census:member:partial` | **A tally member's sum** over the community cells it accepted, published into the room with the members of `census:partial`; `ballot_partial_period_closed` after the period's tally. Community scope. | signed | **Yes — tally member:** one of the period's two, drawn from the roster |
| `census:expected_active` | **The community's expected-active count**, the base of [CC 4.5.13](part_4_composition_governance.md#4.5.13): `period`, `window_periods = 4`, `active_count` (owners active in at least one of the last four periods), `floor_applied`, `member_commitment_hex`; published into the room by the period's share-index-0 tally member and recomputed by every member — a row that differs from a member's own recomputation over the two partials is refused `census_expected_active_mismatch`. Community scope. | signed | **Yes — tally member:** the period's share-index-0 tally member |
| `ballot:tally:{poll_id}` | **A community's secret poll result**: the composite of the members' poll cells for a period, with the members of `census:composite`, named by the poll it closes; refusals `ballot_tally_partials_mismatch`, `ballot_tally_mass_total_mismatch`, `ballot_tally_duplicate_period`, `aggregation_meta_dominated`. A tally carries no standing under Part 4; governance acts stay identified rows. Community scope. | signed | **Yes — tally member:** the period's share-index-0 tally member |

#### 3.1.3.1 `session-claim` — `session:*` — which occurrence of a self is handling an exchange (normative)

Distinct from the substrate self-reports above: these are **occurrence** self-reports, not substrate ones. Persist is the steward — it owns the rows, the projection, the merge rule and the read — and it enforces both rules below; this section is the row those validators read from, so that two of them cannot apply different predicates to one artifact.

**The problem it solves.** A *self* is one federated identity plus the N nodes it stewards — its **occurrences**. An attestation addressed to a fed id fans out to every one of them, because a fed id has no transport path of its own; delivery is therefore N-to-M and **exactly one occurrence may act**. The claim is keyed by `(community, session)`: *"which node is my self?"* has no answer — a self is legitimately doing several things at once, an agent scouting in one community while the person is on video in another — but *"which node is handling session z in community B?"* does. Canonical leaf `session:claim:v1`; the family governs **any addressed, stateful exchange** — a session, a claim flow, a moderation duty, a request expecting one reply — and is not chat-specific.

**Registry row (CC 3.1.7 R1 — this is the catalogue row the validators read).**

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `session:{kind}` | Which occurrence of a self is handling an addressed, stateful exchange. Canonical leaf `session:claim:v1`; `{kind}` open vocabulary per [CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md). Keyed by `(community, session)` — a routing table, never a leader election, and not chat-specific. Emitter: **self-report** (`attesting_key_id` = `attested_key_id` = the claiming occurrence). Composition: **convergent merge** — earliest `claimed_at`, ties on the lowest occurrence `key_id`. Projection ceiling **`Cohort`** at every commons tier, no `authority` branch. | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md) self-report** |

**This is a routing table, not a leader election.** Nothing here elects a node, confers standing, or ranks occurrences. Election does not belong on this plane at all.

**Emitter — self-report (normative).** `attesting_key_id` MUST equal `attested_key_id` and MUST be the claiming occurrence. The registry carries this as the family's own reserved rule (`occurrence-self-report`, CC 3.1.3.1) ahead of the substrate-wide self-report rule of [CC 3.4.3](part_3_the_namespace.md), which does not apply here: a claim is an *occurrence's* statement, not the substrate's. A row about an occurrence authored by anyone else is not that occurrence's claim and MUST NOT be folded as one — the same discipline [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md) states for `config:{scope}`, and for the same reason: a third-party assertion of where you are attending is a rumour.

**Composition — convergent merge (normative).** A claim is keyed by **`(community_id, session_id)`** and timestamped by **`claimed_at`** — three signed envelope members ([CC 2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md): `community_id` is the existing member; `session_id` and `claimed_at` are this family's, REQUIRED on every `session:*` row, `claimed_at` in the [CC 2.6.2](part_2_the_grammar.md) canonical form). A row missing any of the three is not a claim and MUST NOT be folded as one; no consumer substitutes `asserted_at`, `context` or a payload field, so identical rows yield identical winners everywhere. There is no compare-and-swap across a mesh, so concurrent claims are **expected rather than prevented** and MUST settle on **earliest `claimed_at`, ties broken on the lowest occurrence `key_id`**. The tie-break is load-bearing, not decoration: a fan-out arrives at once and clocks are coarse, so without a total order two nodes would disagree about the survivor forever, each correctly applying "earliest wins". Because the rule is convergent from either arrival order, every occurrence computes the same holder with **no coordination round-trip**. Consumers MUST still make handling idempotent per attestation id — determinism only bites once views agree, and replication lag means they transiently do not. A claim goes **stale** and becomes claimable again so a dead node cannot hold a session forever. **The horizon is the row's own `valid_until`** ([CC 2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)), signed by the claimant and moved only by a renewing row that `supersedes` it — never a consumer-local timeout, because two occurrences applying different horizons would expire the same earliest claim at different instants and the convergence above would come undone (one acting on a later claim while the other still holds the earliest as holder). The *cadence* of renewal is the claimant's to choose; the *instant* of staleness is the same for every reader. A row without `valid_until` is malformed on this family. **The lease is bounded, and the bound reads the claim alone (normative).** A signature proves who supplied `claimed_at`, not that it is honest, and "earliest wins" would otherwise let one occurrence hold a session indefinitely by back-dating `claimed_at` and far-dating `valid_until`. The one bound this Part imposes is therefore **self-contained**: `claimed_at ≤ valid_until` and `valid_until − claimed_at` MUST NOT exceed **86 400 seconds** — a lease runs forward from its claim, and for at most a day — read from the claim's own signed members and nothing else — no community record, no opening row, no receiver clock — so every substrate reaches the same verdict for the same row at any arrival time and in any replication order. A claim over the bound is refused (`session_claim_out_of_window`) and never enters the fold; a claimant that wants to hold longer renews with a `supersedes` row, each renewal bounded the same way. Peers MUST NOT re-apply any arrival-clock check to a replicated claim: the [CC 2.6.7](part_2_the_grammar.md) clock rule (±5 minutes; a future-dated row is malformed) is applied once, at the emitter's own admitting substrate, and a row it admitted replicates as admitted. **Residual, stated:** a claimant can still back-date `claimed_at`, so earliest-wins can be won against honest occurrences by at most one lease (24 h); that is the accepted cost of ordering on signer-stamped instants with no global clock. Tighter bounds — a per-community lease, or anchoring `claimed_at` to the exchange's opening row — need members the envelope does not carry (a pinned opening-row id, a pinned policy-record id); a community that needs more witnesses its session claims on the [CC 5.3.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md) plane.

**The invariant beneath both rules (normative).** **An unclaimed exchange is never acted on** — not by a quorum, not by the lowest id, and **not by a single-node self**. Being the only occurrence is not authority to act; it means there is one place where nobody is home. Attendance is encoded as **presence in the table**: an unattended occurrence is *absent*, never present with a weak claim. A weak-claim value is one a careless comparison can promote into a right to act, so no such variant may exist.

**Projection ceiling: `Cohort` at every commons tier, with no `authority` branch (normative — not a default).** A trust root is not a party to someone else's session. The ceiling is not to be lifted: at `Global` the family would publish an **attendance map of a person's devices** — which node they are chatting from, which is running an agent, which is on video — to the whole mesh. That is the [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) structural-invisibility promise inverted.

**What the substrate does not own.** Attendance itself. *"A human is present here"* / *"an agent is running here"* is not a storage fact, and no substrate can know it; when to claim, renew, and release is the consumer's decision.


#### 3.1.3.2 `membership` — `membership:*` — nobody joins a family or community without their own consent (normative)

**The rule.** Admission to a `family` or `community` is two consents, never one: the group's, under its `consensus_protocol`, and the joiner's own. The admission predicates of [CC 4.4.3.2.3](part_4_composition_governance.md) and [CC 4.4.3.4.2](part_4_composition_governance.md) read this consent as well as the existing members' signatures: without it a record growing the roster by K would be admissible with no act by K, and a founder could enrol any registered key. The joiner's consent is the joiner's; no quorum, however large, stands in for it — so the rule binds **every** `consensus_protocol`, `founder_only` included. Persist is the steward and enforces it at admission, on the local door and the replicated apply alike, so no host can skip it.

**Registry row (CC 3.1.7 R1).**

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `membership:{stage}` | The membership ceremony, one attestation-plane row per step: `{stage}` ∈ `proposal` \| `acceptance` \| `decline` \| `widening` \| `removal` \| `resignation` — closed. The first three are single-signer (below); the last three are the roster **events** as cosigned rows ([CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md)): a widening or removal is cosigned under the group's `consensus_protocol`, a resignation is signed by the leaving member, and each is covered by the next `group:roster` version ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) T6). A **proposal** is an invitation to one key K, targeted at the group (`family_key_id` / `community_key_id`), carrying `group_kind`, the offered `role` (nullable), `subject_key_ids = [K]` (exactly one) and a required `expires_at` — or, on a `public` community that declares `admission: open` ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)), the founder's **standing** proposal to any requester, whose `subject_key_ids` is resolved at acceptance. An **acceptance** or **decline** is K's answer to exactly one proposal, targeted at the same group, naming it by `references_attestation_id` and `proposal_hash` (the proposal's `original_content_hash`); an acceptance also restates the `role` offered. | positive-only | **Yes — per-leaf emitter:** a proposal is an inviter's (under `founder_only`, an active founder; otherwise any active member at the proposal's `asserted_at`); an acceptance or decline is the invitee's only — the signer resolves (`signer_acts_for`, [CC 3.4.7.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)) to the proposal's `subject_key_ids[0]` |

**Why K is named in `subject_key_ids`.** A targeted row's `attested_key_id` is its own producer, so the invitee cannot be named there; `subject_key_ids` is the member that already carries consent authority over a Contribution ([CC 2.3](part_2_the_grammar.md)) and is the key the substrate delivers on — a proposal is readable by, and applied on, any node whose self-collective contains that entry, without that node holding the group's roster.

**The proposal is an invitation, not the group's decision.** It carries no quorum. The group decides on the **growth record** — a membership **widening**, the only door that adds anyone — whose primary signer and cosigners are judged under `consensus_protocol` exactly as before. One quorum check, not two; and under a quorum protocol K can accept and still not be admitted ("accepted, awaiting the group").

**Admission (normative).** A widening that seats a key K not active in the group is admissible only if all hold: (1) an admitted `membership:acceptance:v1` by K references a proposal P for the same group (and, on a widening that assigns one, the same `role`); (2) P was neither declined nor withdrawn; (3) the acceptance's `asserted_at` ≤ P's `expires_at`; (4) the widening's own signed instant — on a cosigned widening, the primary signer's `asserted_at`; cosignatures may land after `expires_at`, bounded by the hold window of [CC 3.3.4](part_3_the_namespace.md) — ≤ P's `expires_at`; **(5) P has not already seated K** (an acceptance is spent by the widening that seats it, so a member who resigns is not re-seated on the old acceptance). Expiry is judged on the instants the parties signed, never on a receiver's clock, so every node gives the same verdict for the same rows. A role change for an already-active member is not growth and needs no acceptance. **Two readings the membership model (pinned by the register) fixes:** a proposal seats at most once — a widening that cites an acceptance an admitted widening has already consumed is not admissible, so one invitation never admits twice; and under `unanimous` a removal is signed by every other active member *and* is a strict majority of the roster, which coincide above two members and diverge at two, where the one other member is not a majority — a two-member roster removes nobody by this door, and resignation is the exit. Geographic's `location_proof` ([CC 4.4.3.2.3](part_4_composition_governance.md)) is required **in addition**, never instead.

**A supersede never adds a member (normative).** A new version of a `family` or `community` record may change its metadata, keep, re-list or drop members, and never seats anyone; a roster-growing supersede is refused. Every addition goes through the widening plane, which carries the signed instant expiry is judged on. One exception, which consent already covers: a trust-root-grade community's founders' amendment ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md), where founder seats move only through the record) may seat a key that **signed** that version.

**Founding (normative).** A founding record seats a listed member iff that member **signed** the record, as its authority or a co-signer (a family record carries `additional_scrubs` as a community record does); signing is consent. A listed member who did not sign is refused and joins only by proposal and acceptance. This is what makes a multi-founder `quorum:M/N` community — and the `ciris-canonical` birth row, whose three founders, the accord holders, each co-sign it once — possible to found at all. The T7 recovery widening complies for the same reason: the conferred key signs it.

**A node gives no acceptance (normative).** The consent this section requires is a person's. A key whose `identity_type` includes `node` has no agency ([CC 3.4.7.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) and is never asked for an acceptance; a node member of an `infrastructure` community is seated by the founders' quorum on the widening alone — for `ciris-canonical`, by the accord's existing blessing of a canonical node, by the same strict majority. **What a verifier checks:** the seat is valid when the founders' quorum signed the record **and the node's own key record carries the conferring family's m-of-n scrub** (for `ciris-canonical`, the accord's, already in the genesis bundle). The node's owner claim ([CC 6.1.5.3](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md)) is enforced **where the node operates**, not where the roster is verified: the owner-binding sits at `self` scope on the node and no other node can read it, and carrying it in the bundle instead would publish a person-to-node custody edge to the whole federation. The same holds at founding: a node listed in an `infrastructure` community's founding record is seated by the founders' signatures on that record and never signs it, so the founding rule above (every listed member signed) counts only the persons it lists ([CC 6.1.5.3](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md)). Its standing is still serve, store and replicate, never a vote (CC 3.2, *Infrastructure does not vote*).

**Refusals.** Surfaced as the existing `hard_case:community_consensus_protocol_violation:{community_key_id}` (and the family twin) with a named rule: RETRYABLE `membership_acceptance_unresolved` (no acceptance held yet — rows arrive out of order) and `membership_proposal_unresolved` (the answer's proposal not held); terminal `membership_declined`, `membership_proposal_expired` (a withdrawn proposal counts as expired), `membership_acceptance_mismatch` (group, hash or role), `membership_reply_conflict` (both an acceptance and a decline by K to one proposal), `membership_founding_member_unsigned`, `membership_supersede_cannot_add`.

**Decline, withdrawal, expiry.** `expires_at` is REQUIRED on a proposal, after its `asserted_at` and at most 30 days later. A decline is terminal for its proposal; the second, opposite reply by the same K to the same proposal is refused; asking again is a **new** proposal. The inviter withdraws a proposal with the ordinary `withdraws`. A growth already admitted is never re-judged, and leaving stays the member's own forward-only `withdraws`.

**Not a membership rule: reverse quorum.** The reverse-quorum brake of [CC 4.5.13](part_4_composition_governance.md) governs objection to commons acts. It admits no one and confers no consent on anyone's behalf.


#### 3.1.3.3 `custody` — `custody:*` — which of a cohort's own devices hold a blob, and which hold none (normative)

**The rule.** A family must be able to see how many copies of its content exist — "only one copy, back it up" before a phone dies — without weakening encryption or outsider invisibility. (1) Within-cohort custody discovery is consistent with [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md): structural invisibility, and the "wire discovery: none" of the `self` / `family` tiers ([CC 4.4.3.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)), mean invisibility to those **outside** the cohort. (2) The report is its own leaf, `custody:ack:v1`, and it is the **one** holding claim at every tier, placed at the content's own scope, so no row shape exists that could advertise a bounded cohort's holdings to outsiders. (3) **"No copy here is a receipt (node responsive, no copy)"**: a device's signed `none` is a custody fact in its own right, the counterpart of a [CC 5.3.3.6](part_5_transport_substrate.md) delivery receipt.

**Registry row (CC 3.1.7 R1).**

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `custody:{kind}` | Within-cohort custody reports. `{kind}` ∈ `ack` — closed; wire `custody:ack:v1`. One report per device per blob: the blob's address digest, `custody_state` `here` \| `none`, and the signer-stamped instant; `here` also carries the blob's `size`. Placed at the blob's own `cohort_scope` and replicated only within that cohort. | positive-only | **Yes — holder self-report:** `attesting_key_id` is the device the report is about; nobody reports custody for another device |

**Scope and invisibility (normative).** A custody report is placed at the content's own `cohort_scope` — `self` for a person's own devices, `family`, or `community` — and replicated only to that cohort's members. It is never delivered to a non-member, and carries nothing about the blob beyond its address, size and the device; the blob's name and format stay in the sealed descriptor ([CC 3.3.13](part_3_the_namespace.md)). An outsider learns nothing it did not learn before.

**One holding claim at every tier (normative).** `custody:ack:v1` is the holding claim of the mesh, at every tier. A row typed with the retired `holds_bytes` token ([CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md)) is refused: the row-type slot admits only the five. Five rules. **(1) Scope is the content's.** A report is placed at the `cohort_scope` of the blob it is about — `self`, `family`, `community` or `affiliations` for a bounded cohort, **`federation` for Commons and `public` content** — and the substrate refuses a report whose scope is not its content's; an emitter never chooses a scope constant. **(2) Audience follows from the one rule.** Who receives a report is [CC 6.1.5.3](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md): the cohort's roster, its members' claimed nodes under each owner's allow list. At federation scope that audience is everyone, and the `here` rows **are** the holder directory a consumer reads to find holders ([CC 5.3.2.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md)); at a bounded scope they are the within-cohort custody view and nothing else. **(3) A holding claim is never a content binding.** It says where bytes are, not that they may stay: a withdrawn or tombstoned blob is not live because a device still reports holding it ([CC 2.3](part_2_the_grammar.md) bytes plane), and the bytes-plane fold MUST read liveness from the content's own rows only. **(4) Retracted by its signer alone.** A device ends its own report by re-acknowledging `none` or by `withdraws`; nobody withdraws another device's report, and a takedown is the [CC 2.3](part_2_the_grammar.md) tombstone path, not a withdrawal of holders. **(5) Freshness is the row's.** The 72-hour window below is the claim's `expires_at`; no second TTL exists. With these, "holding claims at every tier, bounded to the cohort" ([CC 6.1.5.3](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md)) is true at every tier with no exception clause.

**Freshness (normative).** A report is **live for 72 hours** from its instant, and a responsive device re-acknowledges about **daily**. A report older than 72 hours reads as *unknown*: a lost device stops counting within three days, while a phone that is off for a weekend still counts. A device that drops a blob re-acknowledges `none`.

**What a cohort member sees, per device.** **here** — a live `here` report; **received** — a [CC 5.3.3.6](part_5_transport_substrate.md) delivery receipt with no later live report; **none** — a live `none` report; **unknown** — no live report. The count of *here* is the number of known copies; a UI MUST NOT present *unknown* as *none*, nor a lapsed `here` as a copy.

**No possession challenge yet (stated).** A `here` report is trusted as signed by the device: nothing on the wire yet asks the device to prove it still holds the bytes. Possession challenges for custody reports are not yet defined. The residual is named: a device that lies, or a device whose storage has silently rotted, is counted as a copy until its report lapses or it is challenged once challenges exist. CC 6.1.5's **N6** is unchanged and still governs a holding claim counted toward an item's rarity: until a challenger exists a holding claim weighs by what backs it (possession verified 2, signature only 1, unverified 0), and the challenger is staged for the durability tier's first release; a custody report is a separate, within-cohort count and is not thereby a possession-bound claim.

#### 3.1.3.4 `files` — `file:*` and `collection:*` — files, and directories of them (normative)

**The rule.** A file is one row per file placed in a cohort, with name and type sealed ([CC 3.3.13](part_3_the_namespace.md)). Directories are objects in their own right, so a directory can be promoted, moved, renamed, withdrawn and shared as one thing.

**Registry rows (CC 3.1.7 R1).**

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file:{version}` | A file: one row per file, placed at a `cohort_scope`, its bytes addressed by digest and described by the [CC 3.3.13](part_3_the_namespace.md) Source struct, name and format inside the sealed descriptor. Wire `file:v1`. | positive-only | **Yes — author-emitted:** the file's author (or a device that author acts through) |
| `collection:{kind}` | A collection of files that is an object in its own right. Canonical kind `directory` (wire `collection:directory:v1`); `{kind}` open per [CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md), leaving room for albums or playlists. Its name and its own `parent` live in its sealed descriptor, exactly as a file's do. | positive-only | **Yes — author-emitted:** the collection's creator; an amendment (rename, move) is its author's, or the cohort's under its `consensus_protocol` where the cohort's rules say so |

**Identity and membership (normative).** A directory's identity is the attestation id of its creating row, carried forward by `supersedes` under the [CC 3.3.9](part_3_the_namespace.md) stable-id grouping; renaming or moving it is a `supersedes` of that row. A file's or a directory's membership in a directory is **`parent`, carried inside its own sealed descriptor** — the containing directory's stable id, sealed with the name. There is no edge row: the tree's shape is sealed at every scope, including `community`, so no holder learns how many items a directory holds, and moving a file is one write — the child's row re-sealed with a new `parent`. Placing one's own file in a directory needs no one's leave but that file's author.

**Promotion is one intent, executed as N crossings (normative).** Every widening is an act on specific bytes: [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) emits on the new Contribution, promotion is the federation-emit moment ([CC 5.3.2.4.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md)), and the perceptual-hash tripwire fires at every scope-widening promotion ([CC 1.13.3.4](part_1_foundation.md)). A collection-level crossing would be the one way to widen bytes without that tripwire, so none exists and no folder key is ever minted. Promoting a directory is a `supersedes` of the directory row at the wider scope — the intent — which its author's node carries out as one ordinary promotion per file that author owns, each with its own emission and tripwire, reporting any it did not widen and why.

**A directory's scope is a default, never a floor (normative).** A file placed in a directory is placed at the directory's scope at that instant, by its author's own act; a file its author pinned narrower stays narrower, and widening the directory never drags it. Narrowing a directory is forward-only: it changes the default for later placements and MAY withdraw the author's own wider copies; it un-publishes nothing.

**No consent for anyone else's files (normative).** A directory promotion widens **only the promoting author's own files**. Each author holds revocation authority over their own rows ([CC 5.3.2.4.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md)), and no collection act stands in for it — the principle [CC 3.1.3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) states for membership. Another author's files in a shared directory receive a widening **request**, which that author's node acts on only on that author's acceptance. Every per-file gate still binds: a non-author subject's consent ([CC 2.3](part_2_the_grammar.md), [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) and the minor protections of [CC 3.4.13](part_3_the_namespace.md). A directory act inherits every gate and bypasses none.

**Custody at the directory tier** is a query over the per-file custody receipts of CC 3.1.3.3, filtered by `parent`; it needs no row of its own.

### 3.1.4 `transport-delivery` — CIRISEdge — transport, delivery, reachability

**Steward**: CIRISEdge. Substrate-self-reports per [CC 3.4.3](part_3_the_namespace.md).

Canonical leaves: `transport:{kind}`, `delivery:{class}`, `peer_reachability:{network}`, `key_boundary:{scope}`. Polarity: signed. See [CC 8.1](part_8_appendices.md) Edge leaf glossary.

Edge also emits on the one `system:*` family registered at [CC 3.1.3](part_3_the_namespace.md), under its own `identity_type` ([CC 3.4.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)).

`delivery_receipt:{stream_id}` is Edge's too, and is registered here rather than left to [CC 3.4.6](part_3_the_namespace.md) prose: subscriber-emitted, membership-gated, **not** a substrate-self-report. Polarity: signed. Emitter rule and the validated-not-adjudicated discipline: [CC 3.4.6](part_3_the_namespace.md).

#### Transport-capacity leaf (registry row)

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `capacity:relay_delivery` | A consuming node's **measurement** of a relay's delivered-versus-claimed A/V throughput over a window, keyed to the relay's own `SignedRelayCapacity` claim `(advertiser_key_id, stream_id, epoch)`: the envelope `score` is the fulfilment ratio, never a verdict. Subject = the relay (`attested_key_id`), attester = the consumer (`attesting_key_id`), `attestation_type: scores`, wire `capacity:relay_delivery:v1`. Edge names the leaf and owns its semantics; the honesty score proper is LensCore's, composed fleet-wide with multi-observer corroboration. | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)**: the `capacity:` stem's two gates, no-self-emit and consent-before-scoring |

**A `capacity:` leaf owned outside LensCore (normative).** Three things this row settles. *Leaf ownership and family policy are different things.* The component that owns the measured mechanism names the leaf — edge owns the A/V delivery vocabulary — and both [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md) gates bind it regardless of owner: `attesting_key_id ≠ attested_key_id`, and admission only over a subject holding a live `consent:scope:analyze` grant. *It is not a factor of the capacity score.* `capacity:relay_delivery` is a transport-plane leaf about a node; it is never one of the five factors of [CC 3.1.8.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) and MUST NOT enter `capacity:composite`, whose `min` runs over exactly `C, I_int, R, I_inc, S`. *The consent addressee at a pair.* CC 3.4.5 addresses the `analyze` grant to the scoring community; a relay and its consumer are a pair, and here the grant covers the attesting consumer — the relay's `consent:state:granted` with scope `analyze` resolving `Granted` for that consumer through the substrate's one scoped-consent fold, which is the same predicate admission re-checks and the emitter refuses on (a named withhold, never a silent drop). This is not the accountability case: because the measurement is taken against a claim the relay itself signed, it could be argued to be *accountability*, which [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md) says consent never gates — but the row is filed as a `capacity:*` score about the relay and feeds LensCore's fleet-wide honesty composition, which is consensual reputation, and one leaf carved out of the family's gate would be the ungated transmission principle the reservation exists to close. A mesh whose relays have not yet minted their `analyze` grant on latch therefore emits nothing on this leaf, by design (the plane opens when subjects open it).

#### Rendezvous route leaf (registry row)

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rendezvous:route` | **A receiver's published route for targeted delivery through the Anonymous Tier ([CC 5.4.6](part_5_transport_substrate.md#5.4.6)).** A federation-scope row whose attester and subject are one **rendezvous key**: a fresh hybrid key pair for each rotation period, unlinkable to the receiver's identity because it is fresh (ML-DSA has no blinding). Its `key:record:v1` row travels inline in the payload, so a sender holding only this row can verify it; the key's `identity_type` is `rendezvous`, and it has no occurrence and no standing: a key plane refuses a rendezvous key offered as an identity with `federation_rendezvous_key_not_an_identity`. Payload: `format` (`"outfox-xwing-v1"`), `period` (`floor(unix_seconds / 3600)`), `valid_from` (the period's start), `valid_until` (the period's end plus one grace period), `rendezvous_node_key_id` (the layer-3 serve seat whose mailbox holds the receiver's packets; it MUST be a seat), `route_blob_base64` (the reply header for the receiver's three hops, sealed per hop to that period's hop keys), `rendezvous_key_record` (the inline key record). **Reach:** everyone, as on the bootstrap planes, because no sender is Rooted with a key that lives for one period. **Retention:** the current period plus one grace period; each period's row `supersedes` the previous one in the same lineage, and a row is purgeable after `valid_until`; never a `hard_case`, never a durable item. **One route per period, used many times:** each relay can count the packets addressed to one rendezvous at its layer, which the rendezvous node sees anyway; the `format` member leaves room for a single-use variant. | signed | **Yes — self:** attester = subject = the period's rendezvous key; reach is federation-wide |

### 3.1.5 `accord-agent` — CIRISAgent — Accord principles + DMA + conscience + apophatic bounds

**Steward**: CIRISAgent. This is where M-1 enters the namespace directly: the six principles, the four decision-making algorithms, the four consciences, and the apophatic bounds all become attestable dimensions.

#### Autonomy + explainability wire families (registry rows)

`consent:*` is `autonomy:` at the wire — the subject's own grant, scope and revocation over a Contribution about them; it **governs routing**, so leaving it unreserved would leave the transmission principle open. `trace:*` is `fidelity:explainability_sla:` at the wire — the artifact an `l2_reasoning_trace` / `l3_full_dma_chain` / `l4_attested_chain` commitment actually delivers.

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Reserved? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `consent:{kind}` | Subject-side consent authority over Contributions naming the subject in `subject_key_ids`, plus the producer-side and substrate-side leaves that close its lifecycle. Leaf catalogue, per-leaf polarity and the emitter class of each leaf: [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md); the directed node→peer grant `consent:replication:{version}`: [CC 3.3.7](part_3_the_namespace.md). **Open in its parameters, closed in its leaves** — the `{kind}` positions *within* a catalogued leaf are open vocabulary per [CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md); a new leaf is admitted only once [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) catalogues it with an emitter class. | per-leaf ([CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `consent:state:{stance}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); the subject's stance on a Contribution, semantics at [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). `{stance}` ∈ `granted` \| `revoked` \| `expired` — closed; `expired` is the one substrate-emitted value. | enumerated | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:stream:{kind}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); pre-packaged stream bundle, canonical kinds at [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | enumerated | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:deletion_sla:{days}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); the producer's time-to-delete-after-revoke commitment, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:deletion_complete` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); producer's eviction attestation, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | positive-only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:decay:{stage}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); a decay-protocol stage, emitted by the party running the protocol, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | enumerated | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:partnership_grant` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); subject side of a bilateral grant, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) / [CC 4.4.3.5.3](part_4_composition_governance.md). | positive-only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:partnership_accept` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); producer side of a bilateral grant, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). | positive-only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:scope:{kind}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); a descriptive scope row — the scope of a grant is the `scope` envelope member of `consent:state:granted` ([CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)). | enumerated | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:replication:{version}` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); directed node→peer replication grant, [CC 3.3.7](part_3_the_namespace.md). | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `consent:community_trust` | Catalogue row (CC 3.1.7 R1); a node owner's standing grant that the node's sealed reasoning traces may be captured for the community-trust plane, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md). Wire `consent:community_trust:v1`. | positive-only | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** per-leaf emitter class, [CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) |
| `trace:{form}:{version}` | An agent's own reasoning trace carried envelope-native. Canonical leaf `trace:complete:v1` — the post-scrub `CompleteTrace`. The envelope carries non-empty `trace_id` + `agent_id_hash` and **exactly one** of an inline `trace` object (canonical bytes within the [CC 2.6.1.3](part_2_the_grammar.md) envelope size bound) or a `manifest` object bearing the `trace_manifest:v1` schema below. Future members are `trace:{form}:{version}` and need no per-member ratification; a new `{form}` lands via [CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md). | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `identity_amendment` | **The identity change a Wise Authority authorised, as applied** (promise 5: nobody edits an agent's identity record without a Wise Authority; [CC 4.3](part_4_composition_governance.md#4.3)). Attester = the agent's occurrence key, subject = the agent's identity key. Members: `amendment_digest` (lowercase hex SHA-256 of the applied amendment) and `authorized_by` (the `attestation_id` of the `deferral:response:approved:v1` row); `evidence_refs[]` → that response. The build the agent runs is read from its occurrence row ([CC 3.3.6](#3.3.6)) through the roster, never asserted here. **Admission (normative):** the row is admitted only when the cited response is admitted, its outcome is `approved`, its subject is this agent, and its attester held a Wise Authority seat at the response's `asserted_at`; otherwise it is refused `identity_amendment_unauthorized`. An amendment applied without an admitted authorising response is a defect the register shows, not a row. | signed | **Yes — occurrence:** the agent's occurrence key, attester of the applied amendment |
| `trace_summary:{kind}` | Post-scrub digest of a trace for cross-node consumption when the trace itself is out of scope or over cap. Same self-emission rule; carries no claim the full trace does not. | signed | **Yes — [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md)** |
| `retirement:{state}` | **The agent's own retirement row** ([CC 7.5.5](part_7_lifecycle_stewardship.md), the Retirement Standard). `{state}` is the closed lifecycle of the `retirement_record` workflow (`decided` \| `frozen` \| `secrets_destroyed` \| `handed_over` \| `notified` \| `custodian_minted` \| `retired`, the Agent's artifact). Self-emitted up to `retired` (attester = the agent key); the `retired` row **names the custodian key** that signs every later custody row.
