The liveness margin, the declared stalled state and the one bounded recovery row are T7 below: a resignation that leaves exactly `M` founders stalls the root — loudly, by design — and recovery is the conferring body's conferral of a replacement founder, never a lowering of `M`.
- Content is Commons-tier: plaintext, holding claims at federation scope; NO at-rest DEK cascade; [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) structural-invisibility does NOT apply (canonical-service trust data is public by design).

**Membership change ceremony**: same roster-version shape as `geographic`, but the admission predicate is `consensus_protocol` over the founder subset — there is no `location_proof` containment check. Adding a fourth+ operator (e.g., a new independent Node operator) is a founder-quorum event; the new member joins with `role: member` (non-founder) and does NOT thereby gain admission authority. Promoting a member to `role: founder` (widening the admission quorum basis) is itself a founder-quorum `supersedes` — the door widens by the existing door's consent or, while the roster is stalled, by the one bounded recovery widening T7 admits on the strength of a ceremony-plane conferral.

**Infrastructure does not vote (normative).** A founder of an `infrastructure` community is a **human-held key** whose `identity_type` ([CC 3.4.7.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) does not include `node`, and is either (a) a key that includes `user` and carries a ceremony-plane conferral (T2 below), or (b) a **current holder of the conferring accord family** — `accord_holder`, human-held by the definition of the holder triple ([CC 4.2.3](part_4_composition_governance.md)) — who qualifies directly, with no self-conferred role. A holder's eligibility is judged on the accord roster fold at the instant, so a key that leaves the accord roster leaves the active founder set (T7). A key whose `identity_type` includes `node` — a canonical install, a relay, any service occurrence — MUST NOT carry `role: founder` and MUST NOT be counted in the community's `consensus_protocol`; it joins as `role: member`, and its standing is *serve, store, replicate*. A substrate MUST refuse at admission a `community` row or `supersedes` that lists a `node`-bearing key as founder (`hard_case:community_consensus_protocol_violation`, [CC 3.4.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)), and a consumer's roster fold MUST drop such a seat rather than count it. The reasons are [CC 3.4.7.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) (`node` is "steward + `infra:*` reach only, never agency") and the threat the split closes: three installs with votes are three HSMs with the same compromise surface as one; a compromised install that holds no vote can serve stale or missing records — which `transparency_log:*` cosigning detects — but cannot mint a licence, a key record or a roster change that verifies. What the regions distribute is *serving*, not *authority*. For the shipped default `ciris-canonical`, **the founders are the three accord holders `A1`, `B1` and `C1` themselves** (the admission quorum *is* the accord's), and the installs `ciris-canonical-{1,2,3}` are members. The same three keys therefore act on two planes that stay distinct: as the accord they decide by `accord_decision` objects under [CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md) and hold the halt; as the community's founders they sign community rows under `quorum:2/3`. All three sign once, at the mint (the founding rule of [CC 3.1.3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)); every later change — adding or removing a member or a node — is 2-of-3, and nothing requires all three again.

**Worked example — the `ciris-canonical` trust root**:

```
community {
  community_key_id: "ciris-canonical",
  community_name: "CIRIS Canonical Services",
  cohort_subkind: "infrastructure",
  cohort_subkind_payload: {
    infrastructure_constraint: {
      service_class: "canonical", // umbrella: registry + lens + node
      admission_quorum_basis: "founders"
    }
  },
  members: [
    // founders: the three HUMANITY_ACCORD holders, baked at genesis — human-held
    // accord_holder keys, founders directly. Each
    // signs the birth row once; every later change is 2-of-3. Not the installs' keys.
    {key_id: "A1", role: founder},
    {key_id: "B1", role: founder},
    {key_id: "C1", role: founder},
    // members: the canonical installs (claimed node keys), seated by the accord's
    // blessing (2-of-3) with no acceptance — a node has no agency — and, over time,
    // Lens/Node installs + independent operators. A member serves, stores and
    // replicates; it casts no vote:
    {key_id: "ciris-canonical-1-d7bdeu223k", role: member},
    // {key_id: "ciris-canonical-2", role: member}, ...
  ],
  consensus_protocol: "quorum:2/3", // over the 3 founders — the accord holders
  consensus_protocol_entrenched: true
}
```

Consumers pin `community_key_id: ciris-canonical` and resolve the live member set via `resolve_community` ([CC 4.4.3.2.4](part_4_composition_governance.md)); they do NOT hard-pin per-install fingerprints. The Reticulum addressing dual: `community_key_id` is a CEG-directory binding (not a Reticulum destination) — resolve → path-request ⌈2/3⌉ founders → verify the quorum attestation; reachability is never an attestation ([CC 5.3.3.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md) / [CC 3.4.4](part_3_the_namespace.md)).

**Default trust, not forced root.** `ciris-canonical` is the **default** trust anchor a conformant CIRIS deployment ships pinned — it is **NOT a forced root**. A conformant consumer MUST be able to **re-root**: untrust the canonical group, pin a *different* `cohort_subkind: infrastructure` community instead or in addition, or run with none. `infrastructure` is a **general primitive** — any operator MAY emit their own governed trust-root community; `ciris-canonical` carries no privileged wire status, only a shipped default. Membership grows by the community's own `consensus_protocol` (founder-quorum vote, above), never by fiat. A forced root is a walled garden; a default-plus-re-root is a federation — this is the autonomy/justice seam: unregulated standing without the steward's permission. *(All resolution is `key_id` → signed `transport_destination` → Reticulum, [CC 4.4.3.2.4.1](part_4_composition_governance.md); DNS is never part of the trust or addressing chain — a deployment's HTTPS hostname, if any, is operational convenience outside this spec.)*

**Trust ≠ membership.** Pinning an `infrastructure` community as a trust root is **trust, not membership** — distinct relationships a consumer/substrate MUST NOT conflate:
- **Trust + serve** (no membership): a node that *trusts* an `infrastructure` community **serves** it — relays, stores, transports, serves its reads — **without being a member**. It holds no community DEK (infra has none — Commons-plaintext, [CC 4.4.3.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)) and does **not** count in its `consensus_protocol`. Trust is the shipped default (above); serving follows from trust.
- **Membership** (standing *in* the group — sharing its DEK where one exists, standing to speak AS the group, and counting in `consensus_protocol` **only where the member's role is one the protocol counts**: every member for `geographic`, founders alone for `infrastructure`, and never a `node`-bearing key, per the paragraph above): requires **admission by that community's own protocol** (founder-quorum for `infrastructure`, [CC 4.4.3.2.3](part_4_composition_governance.md)). The three human-held steward keys ARE the founders of `ciris-canonical`; the canonical installs are its first `role: member`s — standing without a vote; a generic node shipped pinned to canonical only **trusts + serves** it.

The worked-example member list above is the *founder/member* set; "ships pinned to canonical" (every conformant deployment) is the *trust* set — different populations. The "default trust anchor" language means the latter, never automatic membership.

**Steward-binding gate for non-infrastructure membership.** A key whose `identity_type` ([CC 3.4.7.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) includes `node` or `agent` **MUST** have a bound **steward** — a `user`-role identity holding the one **live owner-binding `delegates_to(user → key, …)`** ([CC 2.4.1](part_2_the_grammar.md), the `is_steward_bound` predicate below; an `identity_occurrence` ([CC 3.3.6](#3.3.6)) places the key under the identity and binds nothing by itself) — **before it may be admitted to any non-`infrastructure` community** (`family` / `community` / org). It MAY **trust + serve** `infrastructure` communities with **no steward**. **The ground of the gate is custody, not substrate** ([CC 1.13.2](part_1_foundation.md)): a `node` key is bound because a device cannot accept for itself; an `agent` key is bound because an `agent`-role identity cannot accept for itself — the custody relation defined under *Conferral is not stewardship* below. `identity_type` is how the wire reads that fact; the predicate `is_steward_bound(K)` is the one below. Rationale ([CC 1.13.2](part_1_foundation.md) / [CC 3.4.7.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)): non-infra membership is an **authority act** (standing to speak in the group), and authority MUST root in an accountable human, never a bare node — a fresh, un-stewarded node is **canonical-trust-and-serve only** until stewarded. A substrate evaluating a non-infra `community` admission MUST reject a `node`/`agent`-role member lacking a live steward-binding. The steward-binding is a **precondition**, not a substitute for the vote — the admitting community's `consensus_protocol` still governs *whether* the owned key is admitted.

**Steward-binding as a substrate-resolvable predicate.** A substrate decides stewardship with this boolean: **`is_steward_bound(K)`** holds iff exactly one live owner-purpose `delegates_to(U → K)` ([CC 2.4.1](part_2_the_grammar.md), carrying the [CC 2.4.1.2](part_2_the_grammar.md) `delegation_purpose: "owner_binding"` marker) exists from a `federation_keys` identity `U` with `user ∈ U.identity_type` ([CC 3.4.7.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) that is not itself steward-bound; nothing else binds — not an `identity_occurrence`, not a `delegates_to` of any other purpose, not `K` being a `user` key itself. A key so bound terminates at an **accountable human** (`user`-role); a `node`/`agent` key with no such edge, or with more than one, is steward-less. This is the concrete predicate the gate above and the [CC 4.5.5](part_4_composition_governance.md) clause-(b) "steward-bound root" check rely on.

**Conferral is not stewardship (normative).** The steward-binding predicate, its admission gate, and the `steward_bindings_of` fold key on **exactly one owner-binding edge** — the one live `delegates_to(user → K)` carrying the [CC 2.4.1.2](part_2_the_grammar.md) `delegation_purpose: "owner_binding"` marker, from a human key that is not itself steward-bound — never on capability conferrals, occurrences or any other edge. The discriminator is the consent structure, stated once for every family: **stewardship is a custody relation over a key that cannot accept for itself** — for a `node` or `agent` key, the accountable human it is rooted in. The same predicate, and nothing else, is reused where a person cannot accept for themselves (an adult under adjudicated incapacity, [CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md); a minor under guardianship, [CC 3.4.13](part_3_the_namespace.md)); the relations are not alike, and this document draws no analogy between an agent and a child ([CC 1.13.2](part_1_foundation.md)). By contrast **a capability conferral is a consensual grant the target must accept for itself** (the [CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) T3 rule: no conferral, however strong its ceremony, substitutes for the target's own acceptance). An act the target must accept cannot be custody of the target. Consequences, paired so the gate and the fold never disagree: a quorum MAY confer a duty scope ([CC 4.5.5](part_4_composition_governance.md) `moderate` / `takedown` / `review` / `slash`) or any delegation-plane capability on an adult `user`-role key, and the write gate MUST NOT refuse it as a stewardship act; steward-binding an adult remains forbidden ([CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md) presumption of sovereignty); and registering a person as `agent`/`accord_holder` to route a duty past the gate is an ontological misclassification, and this rule makes it unnecessary. The steward gate is not an age gate for conferrals: a duty scope that requires an age or assurance floor MUST declare it on its own [CC 4.5.5](part_4_composition_governance.md) row (per the [CC 3.4.11](part_3_the_namespace.md) ladders), never inherit it from a custody rule.

**The organisation is on the bond (normative).** Where a steward holds an `agent` key on behalf of an organisation, the owner-binding names the organisation's affiliation in its `community_id` ([CC 2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)) and is cosigned under that affiliation's protocol ([CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md), the same shape as a licence under [CC 2.4.1.2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)). The affiliation then answers alongside the steward: `slash` and `quarantine` ([CC 3.1.9.2](#3.1.9.2)) reach the affiliation's keys as well as the steward's, and the record names the deploying organisation first. A steward who is the organisation's employee signs as the accountable human the gate requires; the organisation does not escape by naming one. Without a `community_id` the binding is a personal steward's.

**Single-owner invariant (normative — this is what makes `self` well-defined).** Steward-binding answers "is this key accountable to *a* human?"; **ownership** answers "to *which one*?" — and the ownership sub-relation is **single-valued**. A `node` / `agent` key is **owner-bound by at most one** responsible steward at any time. The owner-binding is the `delegates_to(user → key)` carrying `delegation_purpose: owner_binding` ([CC 2.4.1.2](part_2_the_grammar.md)) — **distinct** from act-on-behalf and hierarchy delegations, which remain **multi-parent** DAGs ([CC 4.5.13](part_4_composition_governance.md)). Only the owner-binding purpose is single-valued; the general `delegates_to` grammar is unchanged (no new primitive).

- **`self` is the single-owner cohort.** The `self` cohort is the transitive set of nodes sharing **one** owner — a person's own devices, distinct keys unified by the owner-binding graph — so, for `self` content, an **"outsider" is exactly a node not owner-bound to my owner** ([CC 1.13.3.4](part_1_foundation.md) / [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md)). Ownership MUST be resolved by the **purpose-filtered** `owner_of(K)` — the live owner-bindings of `K` (`delegation_purpose: owner_binding`), **which resolves to at most one key**, never a set to be silently reduced. An owner-binding is written at `cohort_scope: self` and MAY later be re-signed by the owner at `federation` — *announcing*, [CC 5.4.6](part_5_transport_substrate.md); `owner_of` folds both, so ownership never depends on announcement. `owner_of` is **not** the general steward set: a reader that returns *every* live `delegates_to(user → K)` regardless of purpose **MUST NOT** be used to resolve ownership — it conflates act-on-behalf and hierarchy with ownership and would inflate cardinality. A key with **zero** owner-bindings has **no owner**, is steward-less, and has an **empty `self` cohort** (its `self` content is delivered nowhere beyond the key itself). A key with **two or more** distinct owners has an **undefined** `self` boundary and MUST NOT be treated as owned by any of them. **Composition with [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md):** an `identity_occurrence` of an owned key is in the **same** `self` cohort — an occurrence inherits its key's owner — so `owner_of` resolves *across* occurrence boundaries via the owner-binding graph and the CC 5.2 occurrence-based recipient set is a **subset** of the owner-binding cohort. The owner-binding cohort **widens** the occurrence set to a person's distinct device keys; it does not replace it, and the two never disagree on membership.
- **The owner signs its own owner-binding (MUST); the first binding is set at provisioning.** An owner-binding is admissible only if `owner == delegates_to.attesting_key_id` — the owner's signature **is** the claim of ownership (mirroring the user-target guardianship rule below). But an owner signature alone does not prove authority *over the node*, so "first authenticated binding wins" is not decided by who reaches the substrate first: the **genesis** owner-binding is established **at provisioning**, co-signed by the node's own key (or carried in the node's genesis record). A node with no genesis owner is **unowned** (empty `self`), **not** a first-come landgrab target — this forecloses a remote party self-signing `owner_binding(→ node)` on an unclaimed node's `key_id` before its legitimate owner. Decided by provisioning custody, never by a `key_id` sort.
- **Adding a device.** The genesis binding of a person's *next* node is set by the owner's own pen on a node they already own — while the self's roster holds one key; with two or more keys a new `user`-role key lands by the self-roster door ([CC 3.3.6.3](#3.3.6.3): a strict majority of the live roster, the proposer included, or the lone path after its window with no objection) — peer to peer, with no canonical store. **(a)** An unowned node may originate exactly one kind of message: a **device-join request** on the pairing plane: an opaque request of kind `0x0000_0002` that carries exactly two fields, `key_record` (the sender's own key record, self-signed: scrub key, `key_id` and signing key are one key) and `challenge` (32 fresh random bytes, present exactly when `key_record` is), signed by its node key, with the public halves of its occurrence keys in the record; no attestation, no route and no third party's record. The receiver checks, in this order and before any cryptography: a per-sender-key rate, a per-link rate, then a node-wide ceiling on pending requests; only then does it verify the record and the signature. A known key takes the ordinary path and its record is ignored; an unknown key without a record is dropped; it serves and asserts nothing else until bound. The request moves no trust state and confers nothing, so T8 (i) is untouched, and the receiving node holds it only while its owner has opened "add a device" for a bounded window. **(b)** The owner's one approval signs **two rows, each standing alone**: the owner-binding `delegates_to(owner → new node, infra:*)` of this section and the new node's device occurrence under the owner's self ([CC 3.3.6](#3.3.6)); the binding is admitted first and the occurrence second, and the new node applies the binding through its own setup door before it links. **(c)** The availability residual is accepted and bounded: the receiving node holds at most a fixed number of pending join requests and one per sender key, so a flooder who refills free keys can keep the receiving node's ceiling full and delay a join; that is availability, not safety, and is met by the owner-opened window, fair eviction and the joiner's retry — never by a proof-of-work stamp, which would price joining against the preamble's precondition that joining costs nothing. **(d)** The **matching code** the owner compares MUST be derived by the owner's own node from the request (requester key and challenge) and MUST NOT be supplied by the requester; the derivation is the implementer's. **(e) Under the per-link hybrid seal ([CC 5.3.3.5](part_5_transport_substrate.md) E1), the joining device is unowned but never anonymous.** The seal admits an initiator with no owner-binding on the pairing plane only, and binds the initiator's **self-signed node key** into the handshake: the key that opened the sealed link, the key that signed the device-join request and the key the matching code is derived from MUST be one key, the responder is the owner's announced node authenticated by its published transit pair, and a link opened this way carries the pairing plane and nothing else. Proof of possession verifies the key's `key_id` fingerprint against its public key ([CC 2.6.8](part_2_the_grammar.md)), so a self-signed record cannot claim another key's id. Together these close on-path substitution: what the person types into the new device and the device's fresh keys cross under post-quantum confidentiality, the device they approve is provably the device that asked, the first device still tells a stranger nothing before approval, and nobody can dial an unowned node. A fresh device roots the canonical nodes' transit pairs from the genesis bundle ([CC 5.3.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md), `serve_transport_destinations`), seals to one, and replicates the owner node's announced binding before it dials the owner node. **(f) Offline or LAN-only pairing:** where no canonical node is reachable, the owner's device-join pairing payload (the [CC 2.6.8](part_2_the_grammar.md) contact code or its QR form) carries the owner node's transit pair; the new device pins it **for that one pairing link only**, never stores it as Rooted, and every key-bound rule of (e) applies unchanged.
- **Admission-time enforcement (MUST).** While a live owner-binding exists on a key, the substrate **MUST reject at bind time** a second owner-binding naming a *different* owner — the incumbent MUST first `withdraws` / `recants` ([CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)), or its binding must lapse. This is the same first-claim-wins discipline as the founder/root first-run claim (a second distinct claimant is refused, not merged), enforced *when the binding is admitted*, not merely assumed at read time. Ownership transfer is therefore **explicit** (supersede-then-rebind or withdraw-then-rebind), never accretive. Act-on-behalf / hierarchy delegations are **not** gated by this rule.
- **No permanent ownerless lock (MUST).** An owner-binding SHOULD carry `delegation_valid_until` ([CC 2.4.1.2](part_2_the_grammar.md)) so it can lapse; and a key rendered ownerless by a **provably-dead** incumbent — a **WA-adjudicated liveness finding** (a lost owner key unable to sign a `withdraws`, established by sustained non-response plus out-of-band confirmation; the death/liveness predicate is pinned per deployment, so a merely-quiet live binding cannot be withdrawn as "dead") — MUST remain **re-claimable**, so a node can never be locked ownerless forever. **The same recovery path also reaches a binding found to be a *seizure*** — a *live* wrongful owner-binding admitted by front-run or fraud, not only a dead one — on a WA seizure finding. So a front-run that installs a live adversarial owner is **reversible, not permanent**, and a partition-merge that admits two owners is repaired to cardinality 1 by the same authority rather than left in indefinite `self`-scope denial. Ownerless is **fail-secure** (no `self`-scope delivery), never permanent.
- **The recovery wire path (MUST — the clause above is not satisfiable without it).** [CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md) admits no third-party `withdraws` against a live incumbent, so "MAY withdraw such a binding" needs an admission rule to ride. It rides two **existing** rules and adds no primitive. The owner-binding `delegates_to(owner → K)` names **K** in its `subject_key_ids`, so (i) a `withdraws` issued by **K itself** is already admissible under [CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md) rule **(2)** (subject revocation), and (ii) one issued by a recovery authority **R** holding `delegates_to(K → R, scope: [owner_binding_recovery])` is admissible under rule **(4)**. This is the stewardship covenant made mechanical: the key that is still being kept running is the subject with standing to say its owner is gone.
- **The recovery gate (MUST — otherwise the path is a self-liberation exploit).** A substrate **MUST reject** a rule-(2) or rule-(4) `withdraws` targeting a live owner-binding unless it carries a `wa_adjudication_ref` naming a [CC 4.3](part_4_composition_governance.md) Wise-Authority quorum finding of **abandonment** or **seizure** against that binding. Without this gate a compromised node key withdraws its own owner and the single-owner invariant is worthless. The substrate **records the finding; it never makes it** — quorum adjudicates, the substrate admits.
- **The abandonment predicate, the window, and the ceremony.** A finding of abandonment rests on a **signed freshness floor**: the incumbent owner's most recent owner-signed attestation (the substrate's `fresh_as_of` floor is the canonical carrier; the owner-binding attestation itself establishes the initial floor, so a binding is never floorless) has not advanced for longer than the deployment-pinned **`owner_abandonment_window`**, whose floor is **90 days**, *and* out-of-band confirmation per the liveness predicate above. A deployment MUST publish its window; an unpublished window means no abandonment finding is admissible there. The ceremony is four steps and MUST NOT be collapsed: **(1)** a petition naming K and the evidence; **(2)** the CC 4.3 quorum finding, minted as an attestation the `wa_adjudication_ref` resolves to; **(3)** the gated `withdraws`, after which K is **unowned** — empty `self` cohort, fail-secure, no `self`-scope delivery; **(4)** a fresh owner-binding under the genesis rule above, co-signed by K. Reclaim **MUST NOT** transfer incumbent → claimant in one act: passing through the unowned state is what keeps the single-owner invariant and the anti-landgrab rule intact.
- **Consumer fail-closed (MUST).** Any consumer that resolves `self` via `owner_of` — **widening the [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) identity-occurrence recipient set** to a person's own multiple devices (e.g. `self`-scoped replication, a node-switcher) — **MUST** treat **cardinality ≠ 1** as an error and fail **closed**. (The base CC 5.2 resolution — recipients = the `identity_occurrence`s of one `attesting_key_id` — is single-key and already unambiguous; this rule governs the owner-graph *widening* beyond it.) It **MUST NOT** pick one owner from a sorted set: taking the first owner-binding in sort order is a **grind** — an adversary who lands a second owner-binding-shaped `delegates_to` whose `key_id` sorts first would *become* the resolved owner. Zero owners → not owned (no `self`-scope delivery beyond the key itself); two-or-more → hard error, no `self`-scope widening.

The minor-guardianship relation below is the analogous single-valued **user-target** binding — a minor has at most one live guardian, transferred by `supersedes`, never accreted — so both the "one owner per node" and "one guardian per minor" invariants share the supersede-not-accrete discipline.

**Minor stewardship — the structural no-slavery guarantee.** Steward-binding is **responsibility, not property**: a steward is responsible *for* a ward, never a holder *of* one. The federation's value is fixed — *"Stewardship: Treat autonomy and ethical agency as a trust"* ([CC 1.15.6](part_1_foundation.md)) — and the wire format MUST hold it **by construction**. There are exactly three subjects of stewardship, and a fourth that is **un-stewardable**:

- **`node`** (infrastructure) — steward + `infra:*` reach only, **never agency**; admitted under the steward-binding gate above ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)) **unchanged**.
- **`agent`** (a key with a brain) — partnership and agency *under* stewardship; the agent retains its constitutional autonomy and dignity ([CC 1.13.2](part_1_foundation.md)); admitted under the steward-binding gate above **unchanged**.
- **`user`-as-minor** (a child) — a guardian holds the minor's binding, per the guardianship rule below. The row and the gate are the same as for a node or an agent: a `user → key` steward-binding that `is_steward_bound` folds. The duty it carries is the guardian's under their own law ([CC 3.4.13](part_3_the_namespace.md)), not an operator's.
- **`user`-as-adult** — **self-sovereign and un-stewardable**: no steward-binding whose target is a self-sovereign adult is admissible except under the adjudicated-incapacity basis of [CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md). You never steward a node, an agent, or a person as a possession. You are responsible *for* a node or an agent you root. An adult answers only for themselves.

**Guardianship rule (normative).** An under-18 `user` identity **MUST** have a **live steward-binding** — a non-superseded, non-withdrawn `delegates_to(adult-user → minor-user)` ([CC 2.4.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)) — from an adult (18 or older) `user` identity at all times. **No minor `user` identity operates without a live adult steward.** The adult is the `attesting_key_id` of the `delegates_to`; **its signature on that envelope IS the agreement-to-stewardship** — the explicit act by which the guardian consents to be the accountable responsible party for the minor. Guardianship is **transferable and revocable** through the existing structural composers, with **no new primitive**: a new guardian's `delegates_to` carried as a `supersedes` ([CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)) replaces the prior binding; a `withdraws` ([CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)) by the current steward (or by a subject with revocation authority over the binding) ends it. A minor whose steward-binding goes non-live (superseded-without-replacement or withdrawn) is **steward-less and MUST NOT operate** until re-stewarded — fail-secure, identical in posture to a steward-less `node`/`agent`.

**User-target admission rule.** The steward-binding gate above guards only the `node`/`agent` direction; without this rule a `delegates_to` whose target resolves to a `user` would be unguarded, and stewarding a person admissible. A substrate evaluating a `delegates_to(S → T)` whose **target `T` resolves to a `user`-role identity** ([CC 3.4.7.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) **MUST** admit it **only if** all of the following hold; otherwise it **MUST** reject:

```
admit_user_steward_binding(delegates_to S -> T):
  require  user in T.identity_type            // target is a user identity
  require  age_band(T) == minor   (< 18)      // ward is a minor   — I1 age band,
                                              //   age_self_declared / age_assurance:* [CC 3.3.12]
  require  user in S.identity_type            // steward is a user identity
  require  age_band(S) == adult   (>= 18)     // steward is an adult — I1 age band
  require  S == delegates_to.attesting_key_id // steward signed it = agreement-to-stewardship
  otherwise REJECT
```

The age band is the I1 band (`age_self_declared` / `age_assurance:*`, [CC 3.3.12](part_3_the_namespace.md); `minor` < 18 / `adult` ≥ 18). A user-target binding where `T` is an adult is **rejected except under the adjudicated-incapacity basis of [CC 3.4.12](part_3_the_namespace.md)** — the un-stewardable case above, with its one aperture. A user-target binding where `S` is a minor, or where the agreeing signer is not the steward, is rejected. `node`/`agent`-target admission is governed solely by the steward-binding gate above and is **not affected** by this rule.

**Trust and consent are distinct, role-scoped relationships.** **Trust is inbound** — accepting what a member *produces*; **consent is outbound** — letting one's own data *flow to* a member. They are independent per role:
- **lens (observation):** *trust* = consume its `detection:*` scores; *consent* = your traces flow to it.
- **registry (authority):** *trust only* — there is no trace-flow to consent to; the founder-quorum is a **closed mutual-trust set** (the core trust each other).
- **node (consensus):** *trust* = accept its deferral / vote / moderation outcomes; *consent* is **medium-dependent** (moderation, routing, voting, …) but always expressed through the **one** `consent:*` object ([CC 3.3.1](#3.3.1) / [CC 3.3.5](#3.3.5)) — same primitive, many surfaces.

A consumer MAY hold any combination across role × axis (trust a member's output while refusing it data, or the reverse). There is no single "trust the community" switch.

##### Canonical `cohort_subkind: public`

**The shape (normative).** A `public` community is a Commons-tier room with a roster. It is **founded by anyone** ([CC 3.1.3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md): a founding record seats a listed member iff that member signed it; no owner gate), **moderated** exactly as every community ([CC 4.5.4](part_4_composition_governance.md): a named, steward-bound `moderate`-holder from creation, merit auto-promotion, fail-secure), **joinable by open admission** (below), and **discoverable by a listing row** (below). Its crypto tier is the Commons: content at `cohort_scope: community` + `community_id` is **plaintext, no DEK** (the [CC 4.4.3.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md) `infrastructure` carve-out generalised: a room whose roster anyone may join has no confidentiality claim against the public, so a DEK would be ceremony without a secret). Its audience is still the roster's claimed nodes under the one rule ([CC 6.1.5.3](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md)) — *replicated on join, visible to everyone on request*: a non-member reads the listing and may read the content by asking a member's node, and nothing is pushed to a node whose owner has not joined; a canonical holds the listing row and nothing else unless it joins. Holding claims are at federation scope ([CC 3.1.3.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)): there is nothing to hide at this tier. The listing is the founder's own row; admission follows the room's `consensus_protocol` under the floor of [CC 4.5.13](part_4_composition_governance.md#4.5.13) — by invitation, no single vote admits; open (`open:self_admit`), no single vote excludes. The founder sets the room's moderation rules from the grammar as it stands — a published hash-pinned rule set ([CC 1.2](part_1_foundation.md) T1), a per-room watchlist ([CC 4.5.7](part_4_composition_governance.md), never global), `content_class` / `cw_class` / `content_rating` ([CC 3.3.12](part_3_the_namespace.md)), `legal_basis: CommunityStandards` takedowns with counter-notice ([CC 4.5.3](part_4_composition_governance.md)) — and no new rule object is minted here.

**Open admission (normative).** A community record MAY declare **`admission: open`**. The founder's standing **`membership:proposal:v1`** is then addressed to any requester — its `subject_key_ids` is resolved at acceptance rather than fixed at proposal — the requester's own **`membership:acceptance:v1`** is their consent, and the widening that seats them is admitted under the declared `consensus_protocol`. The two-consents rule of [CC 3.1.3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) is preserved whole: the inviter's act becomes a standing offer, the joiner still signs. This generalises the [CC 5.3.3](part_5_transport_substrate.md) public-broadcast exemption (`listed: public` and grants to any requester without a ceremony). A record without `admission: open` is invite-only; `open` is admissible only on a `public` community (an encrypted roster that anyone may join is a contradiction, and is refused).

**Discovery: the listing row (normative).** Discovery is a federation-scoped `scores` row by the founder on **`community:listing:v1`** ([CC 3.1.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)): name, description, `cohort_subkind`, the admission policy, the named moderator and the `community_key_id`, replicated like any Commons row and ranked by NodeCore votes ([CC 4.4.3.7](part_4_composition_governance.md) Policy F, layer 3). It is the community's own row, distinct from the per-member `listed: public` flag ([CC 2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)), which is unchanged and remains the member's one-way choice about roster visibility. There is otherwise no community directory in this document: a private community's `community_key_id` arrives out of band.

**Publication is the seam (normative).** Posting into a `public` room is publication: the perceptual-hash tripwire of [CC 1.13](part_1_foundation.md) runs on the post exactly as it runs on a promotion into the Commons — never inside a private cohort, never on the device — because the post's audience is anyone who joins, and a room that anyone may join would otherwise be the way around the seam.

**Forbidding a room: no new power, and the reason recorded (normative).** CC admits no group ban, and this is where a reader will look for one. The accord's powers are enumerated and closed ([CC 4.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md): halt, notify, drill, resumption, root conferral, pipeline blessing, the amendment sign-off and entrenched ratification ([CC 4.5.1](part_4_composition_governance.md#4.5.1), [CC 4.5.1.2](part_4_composition_governance.md#4.5.1.2)); its silence confers no authority, and it cannot reach the consent or licensure planes); [CC 4.5.3](part_4_composition_governance.md) reads the accord as the brake on category-wide exclusion, not its engine. What exists is what applies: a public room is moderated by its named holder, or fails secure without one ([CC 4.5.4](part_4_composition_governance.md)); its founders' and moderators' keys are subject to `slash` → `slashing:{outcome}` and the key-scoped `quarantine:{state}` marker ([CC 3.1.9.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)); a trust root may restrict what its own subscribers carry by `mesh_config` ([CC 4.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md), relieve-never-expand); and every node's owner chooses what their nodes hold. Nothing bans a room mesh-wide, by construction.

##### Trust-root operational semantics — conferral planes, the acceptance edge, liveness-as-signal

The material above fixes what a trust root *is*: a governed `infrastructure` community, default-pinned and re-rootable. The rules below fix how one is conferred, accepted and un-trusted **on the wire**. Everything here composes over the existing `attestation` + `delegates_to` primitives ([CC 2.4.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)) with the [CC 3.1.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) `trust:*` dimensions riding them — **zero new structural primitives, zero new envelope fields**. The governance reading of the accord's reach under this model — the halt's scope and its severance discipline — is [CC 4.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)'s, not this section's.

**T1 — The base is a self-root, and it is never sufficient for federation (MUST).** First run writes a plain `attestation(user → user)` with no modifiers: the user is their own root, and the node inherits it through the owner-binding above. Observation is the **zero state** and carries no scope — there is no `infra:observe` and there MUST NOT be one, because what a party may observe is governed by the subject's consent ([CC 3.3.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) / [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md)), never by a capability the observer holds; a scope there would invert consent. The base floor alone confers **no federated capability**: with no live *external* trust root, a substrate MUST disable federated agent capabilities and manifest validation, server-tier and fail-closed. The un-federated deployment keeps running on the base; attaching a root resumes federation, and detaching returns it to the floor rather than to a broken state.

**T2 — A conferral travels on exactly one of two named planes, and the plane is never inferred.**

| Plane | Confers | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| **Ceremony** | root **identity** | a co-scrub by a **family roster** reaching that family's `consensus_protocol` over the subject's own record, roles carried **inside** the scrub-signed envelope, verified against the evaluating node's own effective roster of that family. The shipped instance is the HUMANITY_ACCORD co-scrub by a strict majority of the standing accord roster; a keyless constitutional family (root kind *family*, [CC 3.4.7](part_3_the_namespace.md) charter rule) confers on this plane by the same mechanism, and the plane is named by the row's scrub set, never by a granter-declared field |
| **Delegation** | operational **capability** | a live `delegates_to` row bearing the scope, resolved at use against a root the evaluating node trusts |

A reserved-prefix role is conferred on the plane that matches what it *is*. `detection:*` ([CC 3.4.8](part_3_the_namespace.md)) and `content_rating:*` are **capabilities** and are conferred by **delegation**: gating them on the ceremony plane would require accord-holder hardware tokens to stand up a routine detector — a gate that fails closed on every legitimate operator and leaves each newly minted root *less* able to equip its own. Root identity is conferred by **ceremony**, which is what makes a root. A portable root minted by any trio enters via delegation; the shipped default enters via ceremony; **neither plane is privileged**, and neither bypasses T3. Leaving the conferral mechanism for a reserved-prefix role unnamed is what lets that role stay self-assertable, which is the hole this pairing closes.

**Standing over time: rotation is a `supersedes`, compromise is a `withdraws` (normative).** A delegation is resolved at use, and the grammar already distinguishes the two ways a grant ends. A grant that is **superseded** — the root issues the successor grant, to a rotated key or with a changed scope, as a `supersedes` on the old one — keeps its lineage: a claim made under the superseded grant, with `asserted_at` before the successor's, keeps the standing it had, and a reader walks the `supersedes` chain to find it. A grant that is **withdrawn** or tombstoned has no successor and no lineage to walk: standing under it is gone at once for every later use, and, where the `withdraws` names a compromised key, back to its `revoked_after` bound — the compromise response (T3 — un-trust fails closed, emergently). A key-plane `revoked_after` bound ([CC 2.6](part_2_the_grammar.md)) composes with both, as it does everywhere. No second mechanism is needed: rotate by superseding, and withdraw only to repudiate. A withdrawal reaches forward: a delegation that ends by `supersedes`, expiry or an ordinary `withdraws` leaves every act already taken under it standing; only the `withdraws` of a compromised key reaches back, and only to its `revoked_after` bound ([CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md#2.4.1.1)).

**T3 — The un-trust lever is one row, and no conferral may substitute for it (MUST).** Every candidate root, on either plane, MUST additionally pass the *evaluating node's own* trust chain: a live acceptance edge `delegates_to(node → root)` bearing `trust:accepts:v1`, the root's self-charter (`trust:charter:v1`) carrying a pre-rotation recovery commitment over the successor keys' **public key material** (below). A latched `constitutional` halt is **not** a leg of this chain: it pauses the effectful acts of agents under the root ([CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)) and moves no trust state; the agent's act gate is the only *gate* that reads it. Reporting or serving the latch's state as a signal (a verdict field beside `drill_freshness`, the served halt status) is not a gate and is expected, since a halt must be observable; conditioning any data door, conferral walk or transit decision on it is the breaker [CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md) names. Un-trust is therefore **one deletion** — remove the acceptance edge and every downstream gate (serve, agent capability, manifest serving, vouching, score validity) fails closed **emergently**, nothing special-cased and no second revocation path to forget. A substrate MUST NOT admit any mechanism, however strong its ceremony, that confers capability on a node which has not accepted the conferring root. The base self-root survives the deletion, so un-trust is nuclear but recoverable: attach a different root and resume.

**A recovery commitment binds key material, never a name (normative).** Every pre-rotation or recovery commitment in this document is `SHA-256(JCS([ {key_id, pubkey_ed25519_base64, pubkey_ml_dsa_65_base64}, … ]))` over the committed successor keys, the array sorted by `key_id` as UTF-8 bytes, each entry's three members exactly as stored on the key record (`pubkey_*_base64`, standard base64), the digest lowercase hex. The **T3 successor commitment** is this over the root's successor keys; the **CC 4.2.6 per-holder recovery commitment** is this over a one-element array. A commitment over key ids alone is non-conformant: a `key_id` carries a 10-character (50-bit) truncated fingerprint of the Ed25519 half and nothing of the ML-DSA-65 half, and a recovery key may sit unregistered for years, so whoever first registers a record under a committed id would satisfy an id-only commitment. At use — the T3 recovery door and the CC 4.2.6 self-rotation alike — the door MUST recompute the element from the presenting key record's stored public keys and check it against the commitment; membership of the id in a committed set is not the check, and a record that matches the id but not the key material is refused. A key with **no ML-DSA-65 half** cannot form an element: a producer MUST NOT commit to one, a door refuses one, and the member is never serialized as `null` (a recovery key is hybrid or it is not a recovery key).

**T4 — Liveness is a trust signal and MUST NOT be a validity gate.** A trust root is **valid until revoked** — by tombstone, `withdraws`/`recants` or supersession — never until a timer lapses, and never by a halt, which reaches an agent's act gate and no root ([CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)). The accord drills are the liveness signal, banded like every other signal in the system: **0–90 days green · 90–180 yellow · 180+ or never red**, reported on the trust surface and never ANDed into validity. A substrate MUST NOT treat drill staleness as a leg of root validity, key validity, or conferral validity. The ≤ 90-day refresh cadence of `accord:lifecycle:active` ([CC 3.4.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)) is not a validity leg either: as one it would give a compiled genesis seed a shelf life and darken the whole mesh at one instant.

**T4a — Attaching is gated on freshness; attached is not (MUST).** T4 governs a root a node already holds. It says nothing about the moment of *attaching*, and that silence is the rollback hole: a consumer fed a stale-but-valid lineage — an old bundle, a roster prefix cut before a revocation, a chain that verifies because nothing in it points past itself — attaches to a root whose revocation it will never see. The rule therefore splits the two moments, and it measures freshness on the *witnesses'* signatures, never the founders'. A consumer that **attaches** a root, or **re-roots** ([CC 4.4](part_4_composition_governance.md)), MUST hold that root's current **lineage head** (T6 defines the object) together with a **witness quorum** of cosignatures over it (T6 defines the quorum), such that the newest *counted* cosignature's `signed_at` is no older than the root's charter-declared **`attach_window_secs`**, measured against the consumer's clock under the [CC 2.6.7](part_2_the_grammar.md) skew rule. The head's own `asserted_at` is not the freshness instant — a healthy roster that has not changed in a month has a month-old head and is attachable, because its witnesses re-committed it yesterday. A head whose cosignatures are older than the window is not invalid and proves nothing against the root; it is *insufficient to attach*. The acceptance edge `trust:accepts:v1` (T3) MUST carry, in its signed envelope, the `attached_head_digest` of the head it attaches on, and the substrate MUST refuse to write it — a write-side gate, deliberately, for this one row kind — with `trust_root_head_stale` when the counted cosignatures are older than the window and with `trust_root_head_unwitnessed` when fewer than the quorum count; the gate runs on the edge's **first admission only** — a restore or replication of a node's own already-admitted edge does not re-run freshness, or T4 would be breached by the node's own backup — and a replayed edge re-attaches on nothing because its digest is checked at that admission. An edge **admitted before a substrate enforced this rule** carries no `attached_head_digest` and stays valid — T4 — on every later put, replication or restore of that same edge; it is read as naming the head the node held when it was admitted. Only a **new** edge is gated: a new edge that names no head is refused with `trust_root_head_unnamed` in every mode, witnessed mode off included, where the head it must name is the anchored one. **A new row with no `trust:{job}` label gives no acceptance and is no charter**: T3 names the acceptance edge by its label, and a reader that inferred the job from the row's direction would let an edge skip this gate by omitting the label. The shipped GenesisBundle (T5, `bundle_fingerprint`) labels every row it signs. Unlabelled rows a node already holds keep their reading under T4. Once the edge is written, T4 governs without exception: the root is valid until revoked, a lapsed window never detaches a running node, `trust_root_valid` reads neither the window nor the cadence, and a node that cannot reach a fresh head keeps running on the root it holds. `attach_window_secs` MUST exceed `witness_cadence_secs` plus the skew tolerance, so that an honest lineage is always attachable, and the rollback exposure at attach is then `attach_window_secs` plus the skew tolerance: the oldest world a fresh consumer can be shown. The window, the cadence, the quorum and the witness directory are members of the root's charter (`trust:charter:v1`, T3) carried in the scrub-signed bytes — integers in seconds, key records for the directory — so changing any of them is a charter re-scrub by the conferring roster, never a configuration edit; a root whose charter declares no window is attachable only through an out-of-band anchor to a specific head (T5's fingerprint comparison, naming the head rather than the bundle). The shipped defaults for `ciris-canonical` and `humanity-accord` are stated in [CC 5.3.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md). What this rule does not do is bounded on purpose: it puts no expiry on an attached root and it does not make the drills a validity leg. An expiring root would let one dead timer un-federate every honest node at once, which is the failure T4 exists to forbid.

**T5 — One genesis artifact, honestly scoped.** The portable trust-root bundle — charter, conferral, and at least one serve node — is the **only** genesis artifact; a bare record list is not a seed and MUST fail to parse. Genesis self-verification is scoped to **tamper-evident**, never to authentic: an attacker's bundle self-verifies identically, so out-of-band fingerprint comparison at attach time is a first-class step, not an optimization.

**T6 — A trust-root lineage is witnessed, and two witnessed heads are equivocation (MUST).** Forks of a family or community roster are first-seen-wins, and only a founder quorum can fork one: a founder set that shows two heads to two nodes produces two internally consistent worlds, and no node can tell. The mechanism already exists at [CC 5.3.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md) and the obligation is already stated for ledgers at [CC 3.3.10.1](part_3_the_namespace.md) item 4; this rule binds both to the objects that matter most.

*The lineage, and its head.* A **conferring family** is a family that a live `trust:charter:v1` names as its root — the roster whose co-scrub confers root identity on the ceremony plane (T2) — other than the accord family, which is a witnessed lineage in its own right: its head is defined the same way, and the `prior_family_digest` an `accord_proposal` names is the digest of its current head. A **witnessed lineage** is every conferring family and every `infrastructure` community. **The head is the signed roster record at a version (normative).** A lineage's roster is a version chain: each version is the signed family or community record, scrub-signed by the lineage's own conferring quorum — the founder quorum under the roster's `consensus_protocol` for an `infrastructure` community or conferring family, the [CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md) roster-change threshold for the accord family — yes-votes from a strict majority of its **standing** roster. That record at a version **is** the lineage head; there is no separate head object and the mint signs nothing extra. On the base it is the cosigned `group:roster:v1` row ([CC 3.1.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)), whose cosigners are the version's signers. Its **digest** is the SHA-256 of the record's signed bytes (its row hash); **`prev_head_digest`** below names the digest of the version it succeeds, carried in the record and empty at genesis, and descent is that chain; **`charter_digest`** names the `trust:charter:v1` row's scrub-signed bytes in force at that version. Every roster-affecting row — a seat added or removed, a serve-node join, a membership widening or revocation, a resignation, a `revoked_after` bound, an accord decision, a charter re-scrub — MUST be **covered** by a new version within one cadence of its `effective_at`, and nothing else moves the head: a version that a charter re-scrub did not move would let a fork carry a different charter under the same head, so the charter in force is bound in the version. **The duty is the conferring quorum's**, because only its signers can produce a version — the single exception being an accord holder's own key recovery ([CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md)), a version signed under that holder's recovery key that changes only their own seat; the substrate enforces the consequences and never synthesises one. Three consequences, and no others: **(i)** a version whose roster disagrees with the fold of the roster rows effective up to it is refused; **(ii)** a roster row on a witnessed lineage that is older than one cadence and not yet covered by a version marks the lineage's head **lagging** (`lineage_head_lags_roster`), witnesses do not cosign a lagging head, and attaching on it therefore goes stale through the T4a freshness gate; **(iii)** the row itself still **takes effect at its `effective_at`** — the fold is the authority on who holds a seat, the head is the authority on what a fresh consumer may attach on, and a removal or revocation never waits on a ceremony. With witnessed mode off there is no cosignature to withhold, so a lag is reported and nothing else changes. The founder quorum is never on a timer. The cadence duty is the **witnesses'**: each directory witness MUST re-cosign the current head, unchanged, at least once per **`witness_cadence_secs`**, so that silence is detectable rather than merely absent and a stable roster stays attachable without a ceremony. The genesis head of each shipped lineage — the `humanity-accord` family record and the `ciris-canonical` community's **birth record** — is a member of the GenesisBundle's `attestations` ([CC 5.3.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md)) and is pinned by `bundle_fingerprint` with everything else in it; a community born from an artifact the bundle does not pin has no anchor (T5).

*Witnessed.* A head is witnessed when it carries at least **`witness_quorum`** valid lineage-head cosignatures ([CC 5.3.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md)) from distinct keys in the charter's **witness directory** (`witnesses[]`, key records inside the charter's scrub-signed bytes, hence inside the genesis bundle for the shipped roots and changed only by re-scrub — a consumer never fetches the directory from the route it is attaching to, and the directory needs no delegation walk to verify, which is what lets a node that holds no root yet verify a witness). `witness_quorum` MUST equal ⌊|`witnesses`|/2⌋ + 1 — a strict majority of the directory, and at least 2 — so that two witnessed heads for one lineage require at least one witness to have signed both, which is attributable from the two signatures alone; a charter declaring a smaller quorum is refused at admission. **Listing is the conferral, and it needs the owner's consent.** A key is a lineage witness because the charter's directory lists it, and for no other reason: the directory is scrub-signed by the conferring roster and travels in the bundle, so nothing else has to be walked to verify it. The `witness` `identity_type` is the emitter role for `transparency_log:cosigned:*` ([CC 3.4.10](part_3_the_namespace.md)), is conferred by delegation under a root, and is therefore **neither required of nor sufficient for** a lineage witness — a node attaching for the first time holds no root to resolve that delegation against, which is the circularity the directory exists to avoid. A witness is someone else's node, so the conferring roster MUST NOT list a key without a live `lineage_witness:acceptance:v1` from that node's owner ([CC 3.1.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)): the charter re-scrub that lists the key cites the acceptance, and a re-scrub listing a key with none is refused. The node then cosigns unattended; nodes have no agency, and the consent is the owner's. An owner ends it by a `withdraws` of the acceptance: cosignatures that key signs after the withdrawal do not count, the key stays in `n` until the roster delists it by re-scrub, and the effect is therefore only ever toward *fewer* counted cosignatures — a harder attach, never a detached node (T4). A witness is **independent**: a listed key that is not a founder of the lineage, not a member seat of it, and not owner-bound or steward-bound ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md) `is_steward_bound`) to any founder of it — a serve node of `ciris-canonical` is not an independent witness of `ciris-canonical` — evaluated at the cosignature's `signed_at` against the roster at the head being cosigned; a later change of role neither voids an earlier cosignature nor validates one. A lineage witnessed by fewer than the quorum, or by its own founders or seats, is unwitnessed for this rule. A cosignature on a head is verified against the directory of that head's **parent** (the genesis head against the bundle's charter), never against the head's own `charter_digest`, which a fork head chooses; a directory change therefore takes effect at the head that carries it and is witnessed by the directory before it — the ordinary rotation-chain shape, so the witness set can turn over without a moment in which no one watches. If the previous directory can no longer reach its own quorum (its witnesses revoked or gone), the lineage cannot turn its directory over by itself, and the conferring body re-seats the directory by a charter re-scrub on the ceremony plane (T2); the head that carries it is witnessed by the *new* directory, the substrate records `hard_case:lineage_directory_reseated:{lineage_key_id}` ([CC 3.4.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)), and a consumer treats that head like any other whose parent's directory it cannot verify: attachable only through an out-of-band anchor to that specific head (T5). A consumer SHOULD obtain at least one counted cosignature from that witness directly rather than from the node it is attaching to, so that an aggregator cannot select which cosignatures a consumer sees. The cadence IS the equivocation-exposure window and MUST be stated, never implied zero.

*Witnessed mode off (normative).* A charter MAY declare `witness_quorum: 0`: the lineage's **witnessed mode is off**, whether its `witnesses[]` is empty or already lists keys — *listing and turning on are separate acts*, and no cosignature counts while the quorum is `0`. With the quorum at `0` there is no timer and no obligation, and a head counts as current when it carries a valid founder-quorum signature and descends by `prev_head_digest` from a head the consumer holds out of band — at genesis, the head in the pinned bundle ([CC 5.3.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md)) — and T4a's attach gate reads as *attach only through an out-of-band anchor* (T5): no head is fresh by cosignature, so none is attachable by cosignature. The residual is stated: with witnessed mode off, founder equivocation is not detectable on the wire and first-seen-wins holds. **Adding a witness is the same ceremony as blessing a canonical node**: a proposal and a co-signature by a strict majority of the standing accord roster, here a charter re-scrub that lists the key in `witnesses[]`, one witness at a time if need be, and independence is judged when the key is listed. Witnessed mode is turned on only by a later amendment signed by a strict majority of the standing accord roster, that sets `witness_quorum` to ⌊n/2⌋ + 1 over the directory then listed — never all three holders. The head that carries that amendment is judged under its parent's mode, so it is current on the founders' quorum; from the next head onward every rule of T6 applies. A non-zero `witness_quorum` below ⌊n/2⌋ + 1 stays refused. **A charter silent on `witness_quorum` is in witnessed mode off**, exactly as one declaring `0` — the reading [CC 2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)'s forward-compatibility table already gives an absent member ("the lineage is unwitnessed for T6") — and witnessed mode is only ever on by an explicit, valid value; a substrate MUST NOT substitute an internal default. **`n` is the size of the witness directory in force for the head being judged** — the `witnesses[]` of the charter its parent carries (the parent-directory rule above), not of any later charter and not a count at the consumer's instant.

*Equivocation, in two tiers.* Two heads for one lineage at one sequence number, neither descending from the other, that each meet the witness quorum are **equivocation**: the substrate MUST raise `hard_case:lineage_equivocation:{lineage_key_id}` ([CC 3.4.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)) carrying both digests and both cosignature sets, MUST NOT adopt a new head for that lineage until the adjudication plane rules — where, as for ledgers, equivocation is distinguished from honest stale-state recovery before anything is slashed — and no consumer may attach to a frozen lineage until then. The freeze suspends **head adoption only**: `withdraws`, `recants`, tombstones, `revoked_after` bounds and the halt latch are single-signer, head-independent rows and keep landing, so a compromised founder key that equivocates cannot use the freeze to hold its own revocation hostage (T4). A consumer already attached keeps its current state on its current head. A head that carries a signature valid under the lineage's quorum *at that head's instant* ([CC 2.6](part_2_the_grammar.md) `revoked_after` read-time rule) but does not descend from the lineage's witnessed head is a **fork attempt**, not equivocation: recorded once per `(lineage_key_id, fork point)` — the fork point being the nearest ancestor **on the witnessed chain**, so a chain of forks off a fork shares one point — as `hard_case:lineage_fork_attempt:{lineage_key_id}`, never cosigned, further attempts off the same point counted and not stored, and never freezing — a founder set revoked at head five can sign forks off head four forever, and none of them may freeze the witnessed lineage. A consumer that holds a witnessed head MUST refuse a competing head that is not witnessed, and MUST NOT silently adopt a first-seen fork; first-seen-wins does not hold for witnessed lineages and holds for ordinary rooms. A founder roster recovering from its own restore re-syncs its head from the witness plane before it writes, exactly as a ledger owner does. The head a consumer needs inside T4a's attach window is *this* witnessed head: one object serves both rules.

**T7 — Liveness margin and the stalled state (MUST).** The `infrastructure` conformance requirements above forbid `founder_only`, `unanimous` and bare `majority` so that no single founder can admit alone and no growable core needs all-N. Under [CC 4.4.3.4.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)'s absolute-M reading that protection holds at founding and erodes with the first exit: three founders at `quorum:2/3` lose one and the two who remain must both sign, so from that instant any one of them stalls the root, and recovery routes through the same two. [CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md) settles this for the accord family alone, with a live set and a floor-of-1 fire, and nothing there reaches an `infrastructure` community or any other conferring family.

*Scope.* T7 applies to every `infrastructure` community and to every conferring family as T6 defines it, and **not to the accord family (`humanity-accord`)**, whose liveness is [CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md)'s and which T7 never evaluates; T8's `Rooted` evaluates this leg over the root community only, never over the family that conferred it. A `geographic` community evaluates over its current member set and needs no margin.

*One founder predicate.* Founder eligibility under *Infrastructure does not vote* — a `user` key with a ceremony-plane conferral, or a current holder of the conferring accord family — is **one predicate**, applied identically at admission, in the active founder set below, and in T8's standing verdict; a key that admission seats as founder and the fold does not count would leave the root born stalled. *The active founder set.* Founder *f* counts at instant *t* iff: *f* was seated as founder by the base row or an admitted widening whose `effective_at ≤ t`; no admitted revocation or `withdraws` of or by *f* has `effective_at ≤ t`; *t* < the effective `revoked_after` of *f*'s key (the minimum across its revocations, [CC 2.6](part_2_the_grammar.md)); and *f*'s `identity_type` **as read at its seating row** does not include `node` (*Infrastructure does not vote*) — a later key record that adds `node` is a resignation at that record's `asserted_at`, so no `node`-bearing seat ever moves a state by being one. Resignation and removal are one row kind at the fold; the grammar does not distinguish them here.

*Live and stalled.* A collective in scope with `consensus_protocol: quorum:M/N` — where `M ≥ 2`, `quorum:1/N` being non-conformant for the same reason `founder_only` is — is **live** while its active founder set holds at least **M + 1** signers and **stalled** when it holds M or fewer: at exactly M every remaining founder is a veto, below M no founder-quorum act is possible. Stalled is a **declared state**: the substrate that admitted the row whose `effective_at` moved the count — for a `revoked_after` crossing, the substrate that admitted the revocation row — MUST emit `hard_case:community_liveness_stalled:{community_key_id}` ([CC 3.4.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)) with its `event_id` keyed on that row, and `hard_case:community_liveness_restored:{community_key_id}` the same way on recovery; peers re-derive the verdict from the rows and do not emit, and between the two events any fold reproduces the state. A stalled root is **valid but non-admitting**, and *non-admitting* means exactly this: no roster widening or removal by the founders' count, and no ceremony-plane conferral by this roster, until the margin is restored. Everything else is unchanged — serve, vouch, manifest validity, score validity, single-key delegations under the charter's scope — and T4 holds: nothing detaches, no running node loses federation, and a node whose acceptance edge is written into `Stalled` is federated.

*Recovery.* Recovery is by the **conferring body**, never by the remaining founders lowering M (entrenchment, [CC 4.5.1.2](part_4_composition_governance.md)) and never by the members. Because the founders' count cannot admit the row that would restore it, a stalled roster admits **exactly one row kind without that count**: a widening whose only change is the addition of a **founder** seat for a key that carries a live ceremony-plane conferral from the collective's conferring family — `trust:confers:v1` under a co-scrub reaching that family's `consensus_protocol`, the same act that seated the original founders — and whose `identity_type` includes `user` and excludes `node`; the conferral's `scope` MUST name this collective's `community_key_id` (or `family_key_id`), so a key conferred as steward of one lineage cannot seat itself in every stalled lineage under the same family; the widening row is signed by the conferred key itself, and the conferral is its authority. The substrate verifies the conferral, not a founder count; the conferring body does not thereby sit in the quorum, it exercises the power that made the quorum. The residual is named rather than hidden: a conferring body that can both retire a founder (a revocation *of* a seat) and confer one can replace a stalled roster seat by seat, with no founder-count act — that is the power that founded the roster, held by the accord for `ciris-canonical`. For `ciris-canonical`, whose founders are the accord holders themselves, a founder seat is lost when its holder leaves the accord roster ([CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md)), and the recovery widening seats the replacement holder, who signs it. The exception is bounded the way a restore-only power is bounded: it can only *add* a conferred human founder, only while stalled, and can never remove a seat, change `M`, the protocol, the basis or the subkind — so it cannot be a seizure vector, and it removes the veto rather than relocating it. For `ciris-canonical` this is the accord's co-scrub conferring a replacement steward founder.

*Founding and floors.* A collective in scope MUST be founded at **N ≥ M + 1** active human founders; founding at N = M is non-conformant. A charter or admission floor that *raises* the effective M — a strict-majority floor, or an unknown protocol read as unanimity — is itself a liveness reduction and MUST be refused at the founding row's admission and MUST NOT be applied at read time; the charter leg reads the protocol the row declares. Resignation stays one row: this rule does not slow an exit; it makes the exit's cost loud.

**T8 — The standing verdict is a state machine, and the machine is the normative object.** T1–T7, [CC 3.3.9](part_3_the_namespace.md)'s stable-id current-state resolution, [CC 2.6.7](part_2_the_grammar.md)'s instant discipline and [CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md)'s live set each fix one edge of the same question — *is this node rooted right now?* — and prose spread across seven rules cannot show that it covers every instant-related corner of that question. The verdict is therefore stated once, as a function **V(rows, t)** an implementer can check and a model checker can exhaust: *t* is a signer-stamped instant, never the evaluating clock, and the [CC 2.6.7](part_2_the_grammar.md) skew rule gates T4a's attach refusal only, never V.

*States.* `Rooted` — a live conferral chain runs from the evaluating node's self-root (T1) through its own `trust:accepts:v1` edge (T3) to a root whose charter carries a pre-rotation recovery commitment and a scope set covering at least `infra:serve` and `infra:attest`, whose lineage has a current head under T6 (witnessed, or anchored out of band while witnessed mode is off), whose active founder set is live (T7, evaluated over the root only; satisfied by definition when the root is the accord family itself). Holder custody ([CC 4.2.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)) is an admission gate at the seating row, never a leg of V — an attestation chain can lapse without a row, and V has no inputs but rows. `Stalled` — every leg of `Rooted` holds except the margin: valid and non-admitting (T7). `NotRooted` — otherwise, including: no acceptance edge; the edge, the charter or the community row withdrawn, recanted, tombstoned or past its effective `revoked_after`; the charter missing its recovery commitment or its scope; the head de-witnessed (its counted witnesses revoked before their `signed_at`, or the directory collapsed to non-independent keys); and, at attach time only, a head whose counted cosignatures are older than `attach_window_secs`, or a lineage frozen as equivocation (T4a, T6) — the freeze is an attach-time clause and never a leg of V for a node already attached.

*Inputs.* The lineage's heads and their cosignatures; the roster fold (seats, roles, `effective_at`s); `withdraws`, `recants`, tombstones and `revoked_after` bounds; each seat's `identity_type` at seating; the charter in force; the evaluating node's own acceptance edge and the `attached_head_digest` it names (an edge whose digest no longer verifies under (viii) is read as naming that head's nearest verifying ancestor, so T4 holds); the halt latch **as it reaches this node** ([CC 4.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md): a live accord edge at the halt's `asserted_at`); and *t*.

*Transitions.* Every transition is a row, or the crossing of an instant a row declares, and the machine names which moves which state: the acceptance edge written (`NotRooted → Rooted`, or `→ Stalled` by the margin at *t*); the edge deleted (`→ NotRooted`, one row, T3); a revocation, `withdraws` or `revoked_after` crossing that takes the active set to M or fewer (`Rooted → Stalled`); the T7 recovery widening restoring the margin (`Stalled → Rooted`); a new witnessed head that removes a leg — a charter re-scrub dropping the recovery commitment, a directory collapse — (`→ NotRooted`); a competing witnessed head (no transition for an attached node — the freeze is attach-time only — and nobody attaches); a `revoked_after` lowered below a head's `asserted_at` (see viii). A `constitutional` halt and its resumption move no state here ([CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)): an edge deleted during a halt stays deleted because deletion is its own row, not because the halt did anything to the verdict.

*What never happens — the invariants a model MUST prove.* (i) A seat whose `identity_type` at seating includes `node` never moves a state by its own signature. (ii) V(rows, *t*) is constant on every interval of *t* that contains no `effective_at` and no `revoked_after` of any row — a lapsed timer (drill staleness, an expired `attach_window_secs`, a missed `witness_cadence_secs`) never moves `Rooted` or `Stalled` to `NotRooted`. (iii) A `withdraws` or resignation never reaches behind the head's link instant: it removes the signer from every later *t* and from no earlier one; a key revocation reaches behind only through its `revoked_after` bound, per (viii). (iv) The edge-written transition is enabled only on a head whose counted cosignatures are fresh and at least `witness_quorum`: a stale or under-quorum head never yields `Rooted` on attach (a guard on the transition, with the served head as environment input). (v) A `constitutional` halt moves no state here: *V* at *t* is the same with and without a latched halt, whatever roster rows land between ([CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md): the halt reaches the agent's act gate and nothing else). (vi) Deleting the acceptance edge is sufficient for `NotRooted` from any state, and from `NotRooted` *for want of an edge* nothing but a new edge naming a head re-enters `Rooted` — no conferral, however strong its ceremony (a halt resumed, or a head re-witnessed, re-enters on the edge that never left). (vii) V(rows, *t*) is independent of the order in which the rows arrived — the fold commutes — so it is byte-equal on every node holding the same rows. (viii) A `revoked_after` lowered below a witnessed head's `asserted_at` is applied at read time, fail-closed, as [CC 2.6](part_2_the_grammar.md) says: for such a revocation *v* and head *h*, V(rows ∪ {*v*}, *t*) equals V over the rows with *h* and every head descending from *h* removed, for all *t* — the lineage's current head is the latest witnessed head that still verifies, and the signer leaves the active set from the bound onward. This is the one input that moves the verdict backward in instant order, and it is the intended one — witnessing records that the world saw a head; it does not make a compromised key's signature good.

*Evidence.* A mechanized model of this machine, checked for (i)–(viii) over bounded founder sets and instant orderings, is the standing-verdict model, pinned by the register as a mechanized-evidence row at this decimal.

## 3.3 `content-ingestion` — Content-ingestion prefixes

NodeCore ships an open set of `external_content` sub_kinds with three feed surfaces (local / community / global) composed against `cohort_scope`. See [CC 4.4.3.3](part_4_composition_governance.md) Tiered-Scope Composition pattern.

**1+4-preservation mechanism (stated once for all CC 3.3.x subject_kinds).** Every subject_kind below preserves the [CC 1.7](part_1_foundation.md) 1+4 lockdown the same way: it rides the existing `scores` attestation_type with the payload-level `subject_kind` discriminator carrying the wire slot, and its lifecycle/admission/revocation rides the existing structural composers (`supersedes` / `withdraws` / `delegates_to` / `recants`) — **zero new structural primitives**. This is the integrity invariant of the whole Part: the federation gains rich content semantics without ever widening the structural surface a verifier must trust. Each subsection's closer states only its unique datum: the [CC 1.7](part_1_foundation.md) path number it confirms, plus any subject_kind-specific composition note.

### 3.3.1 `consent` — Consent namespace family

The wire-format primitives for subject-side consent authority over Contributions where the subject is named via [CC 2.3](part_2_the_grammar.md) `subject_key_ids`. This is autonomy at the wire format: the person a Contribution is *about* can grant, scope, and revoke its use, not only the person who produced it.

**Where the vocabulary is open, and where it is not (normative).** `consent:*` is a reserved family with a per-leaf emitter class, so "open vocabulary" cannot mean "any leaf, ungated" — an ungated leaf on a routing-governing family is a forgeable transmission principle, which is the hole the reservation exists to close. The split is therefore between the **leaf** and its **parameters**:

- **The leaf set is closed and extended by catalogue entry.** The second segment of the dimension (`state`, `stream`, `deletion_sla`, `deletion_complete`, `decay`, `partnership_grant`, `partnership_accept`, `scope`, `replication`, `community_trust`) is exactly the set tabulated below. A new leaf ships the same documentation-only way every [CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md) axis vocabulary does — the addition is a registry entry, not an amendment — but the entry MUST name the leaf's emitter class, drawn from the closed set {subject-side, producer-side, substrate-side}. A leaf with no named emitter class is unregistered, and a consumer MUST reject it rather than admit it ungated: fail-secure is the only reading that does not silently choose one of the two failures.
- **The parameters are open.** The `{kind}` / `{stage}` / `{days}` / `{version}` positions *within* a catalogued leaf are open vocabulary per [CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md) — `consent:scope:train`, `consent:scope:share:cohort:family`, a novel decay stage — and need no ratification, because the emitter class is fixed by the leaf and a new parameter cannot change who may speak.

Forward-compatibility is preserved where it is real (new scopes, new streams, new decay stages arrive with no amendment) and withdrawn only where it would have meant *"accept a consent claim from an emitter no rule covers."*

Canonical leaves and their emitter classes: below.

| Prefix | Description | Polarity | Emitted by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `consent:state:{granted\|revoked}` | Subject's stance on the target Contribution. Closed-set stance values; `revoked` overrides prior `granted`. **Common case**: bare `scores` from a subject_key_id of the target. | enumerated | subject_key_id |
| `consent:state:expired` | Lapse of a grant when `valid_until` passes without renewal. **Substrate-emitted, not subject-emitted** — it records an observed clock event, not a stance, so it is the one `consent:state:` value whose emitter class differs from its siblings.
