Matched by the [CC 3.4.7](part_3_the_namespace.md) longest-pattern rule, which is what keeps it from being read under the subject-side `consent:state:` rule. | enumerated | substrate (Persist) |
| `consent:stream:{kind}` | Pre-packaged stream bundle. Recommended canonical kinds: `temporary` (14d auto-expire, default), `partnered` (bilateral + persistent — the subject's data-retention and learning choice, never a veto over the subject's data choices, [CC 4.4.3.4.3](part_4_composition_governance.md)), `anonymous` (decay-protocol target). Open vocab; recommended, not mandatory; other agents MAY compose other streams. | enumerated | subject_key_id |
| `consent:deletion_sla:{days}` | Producer's commitment at publication: time-to-delete-after-revoke. Numeric value carries the window. It is one mechanism with the envelope's `deletion_window` ([CC 2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)), and its one breach token is `hard_case:deletion_window_breach` (D2 below). Composes with [CC 4.4.3.5 Policy K](part_4_composition_governance.md) SLA-breach watcher. | signed | attesting_key_id (producer) |
| `consent:deletion_complete` | Producer's attestation that subject-revoked content has been evicted from local stores. Cancels the SLA-breach watcher. | positive-only | attesting_key_id (producer) |
| `consent:decay:{stage}` | Emitted by the party **running** a multi-stage decay protocol as each stage is reached — for the agent's 90-day decay that is the agent's consent module, the only party that can observe a stage. The substrate lets an un-marked or withdrawn item descend on its own clock — fewer holders, then none — which is eviction, not a protocol stage, and it emits no `consent:decay:*` row. Canonical stages: `identity_severed` / `patterns_anonymized` / `complete`. Open vocab; other agents MAY define other decay paths. A decay row is a **self-report** about the emitter's own processing ([CC 3.4.5.1](part_3_the_namespace.md)): it confers no right to be believed, and a peer weighs it as testimony. The substrate's own leaf in this family is `consent:state:expired`, which it emits for a lapsed grant (`expires_at`, or a `retain:<window>` in the scope) with `consent_supersedes` naming the grant. | enumerated | agent — the protocol runner; self-report |
| `consent:partnership_grant` | Subject side of a bilateral grant; pairs with producer's `consent:partnership_accept` via `topical_relation:bilateral_pair`. | positive-only | subject_key_id |
| `consent:partnership_accept` | Producer side of a bilateral grant. | positive-only | attesting_key_id (producer) |
| `consent:scope:{kind}` | **The scope of a grant is the `scope` member of the `consent:state:granted` envelope** — a bare string or an array of tokens naming what the grant covers. A `granted` naming no scope matches nothing, by design. Canonical kinds: `retain` (keep the bytes), `share` (propagate across federation), `analyze` (derive features / scores / classifications), `train` (use as training input), `publish` (publish to external systems). Open vocab with sub-scoping **in the token**: `retain:90d`, `share:cohort:family`. A `consent:scope:{kind}` dimension row, if anyone emits one, is **descriptive** — no fold reads it and it confers nothing; this row stays registered so the family has an owner ([CC 3.1.7](part_3_the_namespace.md) R1). `analyze` governs *continued processing* per [CC 2.3.5](part_2_the_grammar.md) **and is binding on emission for `capacity:*`**: the consent-before-scoring admission gate at [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md) refuses a federation-tier `capacity:*` row whose subject holds no live `analyze` grant addressed to the scoring community. | enumerated | subject_key_id |
| `consent:replication:{version}` | **Directed node→peer replication grant** — a fabric node's standing, auditable consent to replicate a named attestation-prefix set to a specific federation **peer** named in `subject_key_ids[]`. The out-of-group peering consent (see [CC 3.3.7](#3.3.7)). Standing (not bound to a single target Contribution); revoked via `withdraws`/`recants`. Federation-tier. | signed | attesting_key_id (granting node) |
| `consent:community_trust` | **A node owner's grant that the node's sealed reasoning traces may be captured** for the community-trust plane (lens-core's per-seal consent gate; the *capture* gate of the three-artifact trace-sharing chain — capture here, ship by `consent:replication:{version}`, score by `consent:state:granted` with `scope: analyze`). **Fold (normative):** rows are grouped by **`attested_key_id`** — the node whose traces are at issue. A `withdraws` / `recants` is a **boundary, not a deletion**: the latest admitted revocation's `asserted_at` is `R`, and every grant with `asserted_at ≤ R` is out of the fold, whichever row the revocation named — so revoking the newest grant never resurrects an older one. Among the grants after `R` (all of them, if there is no revocation), the latest `asserted_at` wins, ties on the smallest attestation id; no grant means no consent. A revocation is therefore a **hard stop** no configuration overrides, and capture resumes only on a **fresh** grant asserted after `R` from the owner-binding chain. **The grant MAY be authored by the node's owner:** attester = the owner — a live roster key of the owner's fedID ([CC 3.3.6.3](#3.3.6.3)) at the grant's `asserted_at` — and attested = the node whose traces are at issue, which is what scopes the grant; `subject_key_ids[]` names all three parties: the node, the key that was the node's owner at `asserted_at`, and the agent key that acts on the node. The node-authored form stays valid, and the fold below groups both forms by `attested_key_id`. Each named party may withdraw ([CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md) rule 2); only the attester recants ([CC 2.4.1.3](part_2_the_grammar.md)), so under owner authorship the person holds recant and under node authorship only withdrawal — trace-sharing consent is the person's act, as [CC 3.3.6](#3.3.6) and `consent:replication` already have it. **Four readings the consent-plane model (pinned by the register) fixes:** a new owner's revocation door is this fold's and no other — its own grant or revocation as the later row, never the old owner's seat; the agent key's withdrawal is folded like the owner's, a boundary `R`, and what a boundary excludes is grants only, never the traces already captured while a grant was live (the record stands, [CC 1.13.6](part_1_foundation.md)); `consent:state:*` rows on one subject use the same boundary fold, the latest admitted revocation's `asserted_at` putting every earlier grant out; and a decay protocol that began under a grant runs to `complete` after a fresh grant is made — the fresh grant covers capture from its own `asserted_at` and resurrects nothing the decay removed. **Revocation authority (normative):** the node's owner MUST always be able to revoke. A grant the node emits for itself MUST list, in `subject_key_ids[]`, the key that was its owner **at the grant's own `asserted_at`** — `owner_of(node)` resolved at that instant over the owner-binding rows ([CC 3.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)), never the receiver's current view, so a later ownership transfer neither admits nor un-admits a grant already made and every peer reaches the same verdict once the binding rows have replicated (a grant whose binding rows have not yet arrived is held, as every owner-gated row is). The listed key's `withdraws` is then admitted on the [CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md) third-party path whatever ownership later becomes; a node-emitted grant lacking that member is not admissible on this leaf. A new owner does not inherit the old owner's revocation seat — it asks the node to re-grant, or emits its own grant, which the fold prefers as the later row. A grant or revocation from a key that was neither the node nor its owner at the row's `asserted_at` is not this node's consent and MUST NOT be folded as one. | positive-only | the node's owner-binding chain — the owner, or the node it owns (self-or-owner, as [CC 3.4.5](part_3_the_namespace.md) reads `config:{scope}`) |

**Composition pattern (the common case)**:

```
1. Producer publishes a Contribution with subject_key_ids = [user_key]
2. User (or a delegates_to chain rooted at user) emits a bare scores on
   consent:state:granted against the producer's Contribution, carrying
   scope: [retain, share, analyze] as a member of that row's envelope
3. Later: user issues withdraws against the producer's Contribution
   (admitted under CC 2.4.1.1 rule 2 — subject revocation)
4. Substrate watcher (per CC 4.4.3.5) starts the deletion clock if producer
   committed consent:deletion_sla:{days} at publication
5. Producer emits consent:deletion_complete within the window OR
   substrate emits hard_case:deletion_window_breach as evidence (D2 below)
```

No new attestation_type.

##### Deletion-window breach — the network's own signal (normative)

`deletion_window` is a signed, JCS-byte-invariant temporal upper bound on the envelope ([CC 2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)) — a producer's commitment, at publication, to delete on revocation within a stated time. It and `consent:deletion_sla:{days}` are one mechanism — the member carries the commitment, the row attests it — with one breach token (D2). These four rules make the network raise the signal it promises, with no new vocabulary and no new wire surface.

- **D1 — Proof of deletion is the absence, not a new artifact.** A producer has proven deletion **iff**, before the deadline, the row was **withdrawn, superseded, or hard-deleted** ([CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)). CC **declines** a distinct affirmative `deletion_proof` artifact: the three structural composers already leave an auditable, replay-checkable trace, whereas a fourth artifact would be a *claim about* deletion that a producer could emit while retaining the bytes — the weaker evidence, at the cost of new vocabulary. Where a producer wants an affirmative record it emits the existing `consent:deletion_complete` above; that emission is evidence **toward** D1 and never a substitute for it. Breach is therefore: the window passed **and** the row is still present with none of the three composers against it.
- **D2 — The breach signal is `hard_case:deletion_window_breach`** ([CC 3.1.9.4](part_3_the_namespace.md)), emitted by the observing substrate (`substrate_persist ∈ attesting_key.identity_type`, the [CC 3.4.3](part_3_the_namespace.md) discipline) against the breaching producer, naming the breached row, the deadline and the observation time. A sweep feeding the classifier MUST be **replay-idempotent**: re-running it MUST NOT double-emit for the same breached row.
- **D3 — It is EVIDENCE, never a verdict (MUST NOT).** The signal MUST NOT be emitted under `slashing:*` and MUST NOT stand as sole evidence for a slashing outcome. The network raises a breach *signal*; it does not pass sentence. Consequences — slashing verdicts, de-admission decisions by other nodes — remain [CC 4.3](part_4_composition_governance.md) WA-quorum or per-node acts, on the same evidence floor as [CC 3.1.6](part_3_the_namespace.md) and [CC 3.4.14](part_3_the_namespace.md) R5. The substrate observes and emits; the quorum adjudicates.
- **D4 — Local tier, self-scoped at mint.** The signal is minted **local-tier**, self-scoped to the observing node's own record, and is eligible for promotion under the ordinary consent-projection rules ([CC 5.3.2.4](part_5_transport_substrate.md)). It travels the same consent-governed planes as every other observation; there is no privileged channel for a breach.

### 3.3.2 `subject_kind` — Governance subject_kinds

Two Contribution subject_kinds for governance over multimedia content: `takedown_notice` and `key_grant`. Both are **Contribution subject_kinds** riding the existing 1+4 wire format ([CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md)) with `scores` as the attestation type; the `subject_kind` discriminator carries the wire-format slot. Since every row carries a registered dimension ([CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md) gate), a notice rides `takedown_notice:{basis}:v1` ([CC 3.1.1](#3.1.1)) and a key wrap rides `key_grant:{axis}:v1` ([CC 3.1.3](#3.1.3)). They serve non-maleficence (lawful removal of harmful content) and justice (auditable, gradient-disciplined access to restricted content) respectively.

##### `takedown_notice`

A signed wire artifact carrying a legal takedown request. The field shape is locked here.

```
takedown_notice {
  content_sha256: sha256_hex_lowercase // per CC 2.6.3
  content_holder_key_ids: [key_id,...] // peers known to hold the bytes
  claimant_key_id: key_id // the federation_keys row issuing the notice
  legal_basis: LegalBasis // closed-set enum per below
  jurisdiction: string // ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 + optional sub-division
  good_faith_statement: string // claimant's good-faith assertion text
  claim_text: string // the substantive claim being made
  evidence_refs: [URI or sha256,...] // backing material
  perceptual_hash: Option<PerceptualHash> // optional; PDQ / PhotoDNA / etc.
  counter_notice_channel: Option<URI> // where counter-notices may be filed
  asserted_at: rfc3339_canonical // per CC 2.6.2
  expires_at: Option<rfc3339_canonical> // optional auto-expiry
}
```

Where `LegalBasis` is the closed-set enum. **The PascalCase name is the spec-level variant name; the wire string is what is serialized** — same two-column discipline as `wrap_algorithm` below, and the same reason: a variant name is for reading, a wire string is for byte comparison.

| `LegalBasis` (variant) | wire string (normative) | Source regime | Discipline |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Dmca512` | `dmca512` | US 17 USC §512 | Expeditious-with-counter-notice (10-14 business day window) |
| `DsaArticle16` | `dsa_article16` | EU Digital Services Act Article 16 | Expeditious-with-counter-notice (Article 17 redress) |
| `TvecTerrorist` | `tvec_terrorist` | EU Terrorist Content Regulation 2021/784 | **Immediate** (1-hour removal obligation) |
| `NcmecCsam` | `ncmec_csam` | US 18 USC §2258A (NCMEC) | **Immediate** (substrate-protective; no counter-notice) |
| `GifctCip` | `gifct_cip` | GIFCT Content Incident Protocol | **Immediate** (within-hours coordinated response) |
| `CommunityStandards` | `community_standards` | Operator-defined community standards | Expeditious-with-counter-notice (operator-set window) |
| `PerceptualHashCsam` | `perceptual_hash_csam` | Hash-match against CSAM clearinghouse (PhotoDNA / Arachnid / etc.) | **Immediate** (substrate-protective) |
| `OsaIllegalContent` | `osa_illegal_content` | UK Online Safety Act illegal-content category | Expeditious-with-counter-notice (OSA-defined timelines) |
| `AvmsdAgeInappropriate` | `avmsd_age_inappropriate` | EU AVMSD age-inappropriate flagging | Compose with `age_assurance:*` gate; not immediate removal |
| `CourtOrder` | `court_order` | Court-ordered removal (any jurisdiction) | **Immediate** (subject to court's stated timeline) |
| `StewardPurge` | `steward_purge` | Trust-root moderation of its own canonical ([CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)) | **Cosigned** under the root's `consensus_protocol` ([CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md) rule 0); `content_sha256` is the **selection digest**: SHA-256 over the JCS array of sorted, unique `"plane:id"` strings naming every purged record, derived included — a record is named by its plane identifier (`attestation_id`, `trace_id`, bytes by their sha256), **never by content** — so a second implementation derives the same digest; `content_holder_key_ids` is `[]`; an OPTIONAL `selection_counts` member `{table: count}` lets the door refuse a selection whose live count drifted from the authorised one (`purge_selection_count_mismatch`); `evidence_refs` → the dry-run ledger. The notice **is a Contribution** — admitted and replicated as every `takedown_notice` is, so it has an `attestation_id` the tombstones cite and a `reconsideration:{grounds}` can target ([CC 4.5.5](part_4_composition_governance.md)) — **and** it travels inside the tombstone bundle (notice, whole cosignature set, record ids; never content) so a receiver that has not yet folded it re-verifies against its own held root and re-derives the digest |

**Fast-path coordination**: see [CC 4.5.3](part_4_composition_governance.md) for the operator-coordination protocol around immediate-eviction cases (TVEC 1-hour / GIFCT CIP / NCMEC / PerceptualHashCsam / CourtOrder).

##### `key_grant`

Wrapped Data-Encryption-Key (DEK) delivery for restricted / subscription content. The field shape is locked here.

```
key_grant {
  wrap_algorithm: WrapAlgorithm // closed-set enum per below
  recipient_key_id: key_id // the federation_keys row receiving the DEK
  content_sha256: sha256_hex_lowercase // the content this DEK decrypts
  scope: GrantScope // closed-set enum per below
  wrapped_dek: base64url // the DEK encrypted under recipient's ENCRYPTION pubkeys.
    // For wrap_algorithm v2 (substrate-wraps, CC 5.2), the
    // recipient's {x25519, ml_kem_768} come from its current
    // identity_occurrence.encryption_pubkeys (CC 3.3.6.1) —
    // NOT its signing keys. A recipient with no registered
    // ML-KEM key is fail-secure excluded (CC 3.3.6.1 / CC 5.2).
  key_validity_window: {
    start: rfc3339_canonical // per CC 2.6.2
    end: Option<rfc3339_canonical>
  }
  ratchet_version: u32 // monotonic; counts the rotations for this
    // content_sha256 + recipient_key_id pair. A rotation is a NEW row
    // under a new axis identity (a blob re-sealed under a fresh DEK,
    // a new epoch); the prior row stays admitted. A re-emission under
    // the same identity is idempotent and never removes a wrap
    // ("Key wraps ride the base", below).
  rotation_chain: [key_grant_id,...] // informative trail: the ids of the
    // prior rows for the same content_sha256 + recipient_key_id pair,
    // oldest first. It names them; it retires nothing — no row supersedes
    // or removes a wrap. The per-(stream_id, epoch) streaming epoch-key
    // axis (CC 5.1) carries the same trail on its own addressing axis.
  asserted_at: rfc3339_canonical
}
```

Where:

| `wrap_algorithm` (variant) | wire string (normative) | Algorithm |
|---|---|---|
| `X25519AesGcmHkdfSha256` | `hpke_rfc9180_base_x25519_aes_gcm` | HPKE RFC 9180 base-mode shape; X25519 KEM + HKDF-SHA-256 KDF + AES-256-GCM AEAD. **v1**. |
| `X25519MlKem768Aes256GcmHkdfSha256` | `x25519_mlkem768_aes256_gcm_hkdf_sha256` | Hybrid X25519 + **ML-KEM-768** (FIPS 203) KEM + HKDF-SHA-256 + AES-256-GCM. **v2 — MANDATORY for streaming epoch-DEK grants** ([CC 5.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md)). A hyphenated spelling of this name is not byte-equal to this string and is a non-conformant alias, not a match. |

**The `wrap_algorithm` *wire string* (serialized value) is normative for cross-impl decode** — a producer, the substrate, and every consumer MUST serialize/deserialize the exact string above; a mismatch silently fails grant decode (same hazard class as the [CC 5.3.3.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md) STREAM-nonce `epoch` encoding). Both rows above are the frozen snake_case forms; a case-, separator- or ordering-variant of either (`x25519-mlkem768-aes256-gcm-hkdf-sha256` and its kin) is an **unknown** `wrap_algorithm` under the fail-secure decode rule below, never a normalizable alias — a consumer MUST NOT normalize case or separators before comparison, per the [CC 5.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md) one-construction-one-identifier rule.

**Crypto-agility headroom.** The vocab is deliberately version-roomy: a future `v3` (anticipated: **ML-KEM-1024**, given national directives treating ML-KEM-768 as interim with retirement horizons near 2030) is a pure **additive** row — new variant, new wire string, no change to existing grants or to the closed-set decode discipline. Consumers MUST reject an unknown `wrap_algorithm` string (fail-secure), which is exactly what makes the addition safe: old consumers refuse v3 grants rather than mis-decoding them.

| `GrantScope` (variant) | wire string (normative) | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `SingleContent` | `single_content` | Grant decrypts exactly one `content_sha256` |
| `GroupMember` | `group_member` | Grant decrypts all content for which recipient is a member of named group (cohort-scoped) |
| `SubscriptionTier` | `subscription_tier` | Grant decrypts all content for which recipient holds named subscription tier |

**Variant name vs wire string — one answer for every CC 3.3.2 closed set (normative).** Each closed set in this section (`LegalBasis`, `WrapAlgorithm`, `GrantScope`) has **two** spellings and they are not interchangeable. The **PascalCase variant name** is a spec-and-pseudocode label; it names the row and never appears on the wire, so a payload sketch written `scope: GroupMember` (e.g. [CC 4.4.3.4.5](part_4_composition_governance.md)) denotes the row, not the bytes. The **wire string** is lowercase-ASCII `snake_case`, is the serialized value, and satisfies the [CC 5.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md) identifier class rule: a producer, the substrate and every consumer MUST serialize and match **exactly these bytes**, and MUST NOT normalize case, separators or ordering before comparison. Where the two readings could diverge, the wire-string column governs; where a future row is added, its wire string is derived the same way the rows above are (PascalCase word boundaries → `_`, lowercased, digits staying attached to the token they qualify) and is pinned in the table rather than recomputed by an implementation.

**Why this is pinned rather than left to convention.** The closed sets are decoded **fail-secure**: an unrecognized value is rejected, never guessed. That is the right default and it is also why a spelling ambiguity here is a safety defect rather than a cosmetic one — a peer that reads `NcmecCsam` where its counterpart wrote `ncmec_csam` does not degrade, it **drops the removal notice**, and the notices most likely to be dropped are exactly the immediate-eviction ones (`ncmec_csam`, `perceptual_hash_csam`, `tvec_terrorist`, `court_order`). One spelling, byte-compared, is the whole mitigation.

**Key wraps ride the base: one `scores` row per recipient on `key_grant:{axis}:v1` (normative; registry row at [CC 3.1.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)).** What replicates the wraps of a self, family, community or stream key between nodes is not a separate row type: it is one `scores` row **per recipient**, dimension `key_grant:{axis}:v1` with `{axis}` ∈ `content` \| `epoch` \| `stream` (closed), `attesting_key_id` the sealer — a key that speaks for the blob's or stream's owner on the `content` and `stream` axes, the minter on the `epoch` axis, who MUST be an active member occurrence at `asserted_at` — and `attested_key_id` the recipient occurrence. The envelope carries the key's identity — `(at_rest_sha256, cohort_scope, owner_key_id)` for `content`, `(community_key_id, minter_key_id, epoch)` for `epoch`, `(stream_id, epoch)` for `stream` — and the wrap, `wrap_algorithm` (v2 only) and `wrapped_dek` — the envelope members `axis`, `at_rest_sha256`, `owner_key_id`, `community_key_id`, `minter_key_id`, `epoch`, `stream_id`, `wrap_algorithm`, `wrapped_dek`, each present only on the axis that uses it. Three family rules apply, and two of them override a default that would otherwise be a trap. **Union by identity, and the precedence key is the row's, not the triple's:** the identity of a wrap is `(dimension, attesting_key_id, attested_key_id, axis identity)`, and the [CC 2](part_2_the_grammar.md) latest-wins / dedup key `(attester, dimension, attested)` MUST NOT be applied to this family — one recipient holds wraps for many blobs and epochs from one sealer, and latest-wins over the bare triple would retire every earlier key. Rows with distinct axis identities coexist; a re-emitted wrap with the same identity is idempotent; a wrap once admitted is never removed by a later one. **The digest is a member, never a citation:** `at_rest_sha256` names the blob in the envelope and is NOT placed in `evidence_refs[]`, or the [CC 2.3](part_2_the_grammar.md) bytes-plane fold would read a key wrap as a content binding (the same trap [CC 3.1.3.3](part_3_the_namespace.md) rule 3 closes for holding claims). **Never withdrawn:** a `withdraws` or `recants` naming a `key_grant` row is refused; once shared, always shared at the wire layer, and forward secrecy is by rotation ([CC 4.4.3.4.5](part_4_composition_governance.md), [CC 5.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md)). **Audience by the one rule:** the row is placed at the content's scope and reaches the recipient's node because the row *refers to* it ([CC 6.1.5.3](part_6_the_coherence_mathematics.md)) — the key follows the bytes to exactly the device it is for, which is the point, and a device merely named in a wrap is not thereby delivered anything else. A wrap is not a grant of authority and is never read as one: admission of the *content* it opens is governed by the cohort roster, and issuance authority by [CC 2.4.1.2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md). There is no multi-wrap set: a `key_grant:{axis}:v1` row carries one wrap for one recipient, and there is no carrier class ([CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md)).

**Rotation after a recipient compromise**: when a publisher rotates the keys a compromised recipient held, it re-seals under fresh keys and emits **new `key_grant:{axis}:v1` rows for the remaining recipients**, each under a new axis identity (a re-sealed blob's `at_rest_sha256`, a new epoch), with `rotation_chain` naming the prior rows for the pair as a trail. No row supersedes or removes the prior wrap; the compromised recipient is simply absent from the new rows. It is never a `withdraws` against the prior row: `withdraws` is the holders-directory eviction primitive in [CC 5.3.2.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md), and a `withdraws` naming a `key_grant` row is refused above.

### 3.3.3 `subject_kind-subject` — `location_proof` subject_kind

The wire-format primitive for a subject's rough-location declaration. Required for admission to `cohort_subkind: geographic` communities ([CC 3.2](#3.2)); MAY be used independently as a stand-alone disclosure. Its design is an autonomy commitment: belonging to a place is opt-in, and the disclosure is bounded to rough by the wire format itself.

```
location_proof {
  subject_key_id: key_id // the asserting party's
    // federation_keys.key_id
  cell_id: string // H3 cell, lowercase hex per CC 2.6.6
  cell_resolution: u8 // MUST be ≤ 7 per CC 2.6.6.1
  asserted_at: rfc3339_canonical
  valid_until: Option<rfc3339_canonical> // null = indefinite (but consumer
    // policy SHOULD treat as stale
    // after 30 days for liveness)
  attestation_evidence: Option<base64> // optional hardware-attested
    // location claim from ciris-keyring
    // (TPM / Secure Enclave) — null for
    // software-only / self-asserted
}
```

**Substrate does NOT verify location truth.** No GPS oracle exists at this layer; the substrate cannot independently confirm that a key in Austin actually emitted from Austin. The truth-grounding is consumer-side:

- The community's `consensus_protocol` admission decides whether to accept the claim (e.g., `majority` of existing Austin members vote to admit, presumably because they have out-of-band evidence the candidate really is in Austin)
- The `attestation_evidence` field MAY carry hardware-attested location data (e.g., a TPM-signed GNSS fix from a known-good device) for higher-assurance communities
- Repeat offenders (claim-Austin-then-emit-from-Tokyo) get caught by consumer-side detection (LensCore composition; not substrate-side gate)

**Rough-only is wire-format-enforced.** Per [CC 2.6.6.1](part_2_the_grammar.md): `cell_resolution ≤ 7`. Producers attempting finer resolution have admission rejected; substrate emits `hard_case:location_proof_resolution_violation` ([CC 3.4.2](part_3_the_namespace.md)).

**Typical cohort_scope**: `federation` (the disclosure IS the opt-in; non-private by design). Producers MAY scope to `community` with a specific `community_id` if they want the proof readable only by that community's members — but then they re-emit for each community they want admission to. Operator/UI choice.

**Lifecycle**:

- `asserted_at` + optional `valid_until` per envelope
- `withdraws` against a `location_proof` evicts forward visibility (consumer policy treats the subject as "no current location proof" for community admission purposes from withdrawal-time forward)
- A withdrawn `location_proof` leaves a tombstone in the audit chain, never the cell ([CC 4.5.9](part_4_composition_governance.md#4.5.9)); per [CC 2.4.1](part_2_the_grammar.md) `withdraws-isn't-retroactive`, leaving doesn't undo a disclosure already made

**Composition with `consent_record`**: a subject who wants to withdraw their location_proof AND compel deletion from substrate may emit a `consent_record` with `stance: revoked` + `scope: [retain, share]` against the location_proof Contribution. The substrate-side consent SLA watcher clocks producer compliance. Note: this is the consent-revocation surface, distinct from the structural withdraws-forward-only semantic above.

### 3.3.4 `family-subject` — `family` subject_kind

A `family` is a **group of trusted nodes** — each node being a distinct identity (which itself may have multiple `identity_occurrence`s per CC 3.3.6). Families are the wire-format primitive for `cohort_scope: family` visibility scoping, and the integrity guarantee they encode is intimacy: content scoped to a family is admitted into substrate, wrapped under the family DEK, and delivered to all current members — but never emits a holding claim to non-members ([CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md)), so the federation cannot even discover the content exists.

One identity MAY belong to multiple families. Each family has its own DEK and its own membership roster.

```
family {
  family_key_id: key_id // the family's own federation_keys identity
  family_name: string // human-readable; non-unique
  members: [
    {
      key_id: key_id // member identity_key (NOT occurrence_key)
      joined_at: rfc3339_canonical
      role: Option<MemberRole> // founder | member | null
    },...
  ]
  founded_at: rfc3339_canonical
  consensus_protocol: ConsensusProtocol // REQUIRED — see below
  consensus_protocol_entrenched: bool // if true, consensus_protocol may not be
    // amended even via the protocol's own rules;
    // replacement requires out-of-band ceremony
    // (see CC 4.2 HUMANITY_ACCORD canonical instance)
}
```

**MemberRole** (open vocab; canonical kinds):

| value | meaning |
|-----------|----------------------------------------------------------------------|
| founder | Bootstrapping signer (recorded at founded_at) |
| member | Standard member; rights per consensus_protocol |

**`ConsensusProtocol` — open vocabulary**. The family's chosen consensus mechanism for membership changes. Locked at family creation; changes ride the protocol's own rules (meta-amendment shape parallel to [CC 4.5.1.2](part_4_composition_governance.md)) UNLESS `consensus_protocol_entrenched == true`, in which case replacement requires an out-of-band ceremony.

Canonical `ConsensusProtocol` kinds:

| kind | Semantic |
|---|---|
| `founder_only` | **A family's and a publication's protocol, not a community's** ([CC 4.5.13](part_4_composition_governance.md#4.5.13)). Founders are the sole admission authority — a founder **active at the instant of the act** ([CC 3.1.3.2](#3.1.3.2)): a founder who has resigned holds no admission authority and cannot re-seat itself alone; new members proposed-and-admitted by any active founder. Suits private households / small trust circles. |
| `unanimous` | Every current member must sign the admission Contribution. For a **removal**, every member other than the target signs *and* those signers are a strict majority of the roster ([CC 3.1.3.2](#3.1.3.2), the two readings the model fixes): a two-member group therefore removes nobody by this door, and resignation is the exit. Suits very small high-trust groups. |
| `majority` | > 50% of current members must sign. Suits medium groups where blocking-minority concerns matter. The canonical entrenched form: the accord-holder family per [CC 4.2](part_4_composition_governance.md) decides by a strict majority of its standing roster ([CC 4.2.6](part_4_composition_governance.md)) with `consensus_protocol_entrenched: true`. |
| `quorum:{m}/{n}` | Any `m` members must sign, `m` an absolute count ([CC 4.4.3.4.2.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)); `n` is documentary — the roster size at adoption, never recomputed against the live roster. The entrenched exemplar is the canonical `infrastructure` community of [CC 3.2](#3.2), whose founders sign under `quorum:2/3` with `consensus_protocol_entrenched: true`. |
| `weighted:{rubric}` | Sum of member weights (per a named operator rubric) must exceed a threshold. Suits formal organizations with weighted voting. |
| `custom:{family_specific_id}` | Operator-defined custom protocol (e.g., role-based, time-locked, multi-stage). |

**Membership-change ceremony** (any addition or removal of a member):

1. **Addition** rides the widening plane of [CC 3.1.3.2](#3.1.3.2), never a supersede: an active member (under `founder_only`, an active founder) emits a `membership:proposal` to the joiner's key; the joiner answers with its own `membership:acceptance`; a `membership:widening` cosigned under the CURRENT family's `consensus_protocol` seats the joiner. A time-boxed guest is a widening with `valid_until`, and the seat lapses at that instant. A `family` Contribution that supersedes the current one may change metadata or drop members and never adds one. **Removal** is a `membership:removal` cosigned under the current protocol, or the member's own `membership:resignation`.

2. Substrate gates the widening or removal per the CURRENT family's `consensus_protocol`:
   - Counts signatures on the row (via the `consensus_protocol` rule)
   - If the rule is satisfied, admit and emit hard_case:family_membership_change:{family_key_id}
   - If not, hold the row in a pending state until additional member signatures arrive, for a **hold window the community MUST set** (operator policy; absent, the standing class of the governance table, 259 200 s, [CC 4.5.13](part_4_composition_governance.md)) — a held change expires at the window's end, so no sub-quorum removal stays held forever

3. On admission of an ADD: substrate emits retroactive `key_grant`s wrapping all `cohort_scope: family` content DEKs to the new member's `subject_key_ids`.

4. On admission of a REMOVE: per [CC 4.4.3.4.5](part_4_composition_governance.md) the removed member retains existing key_grants (cannot retroactively un-share); the substrate stops wrapping new key_grants to them on subsequent family-scoped Contributions.

**Consensus-protocol amendment**:

```
A `family` Contribution that supersedes the current family Contribution AND
changes the `consensus_protocol` field is admitted ONLY IF:
  (a) consensus_protocol_entrenched == false, AND
  (b) the CURRENT protocol's rule is satisfied on the amendment Contribution

If consensus_protocol_entrenched == true, the substrate REJECTS the
amendment. Protocol replacement requires an out-of-band ceremony
(documented per family; for HUMANITY_ACCORD see CC 4.2.1).
```

**Substrate emissions on family events**:
- `hard_case:family_membership_change:{family_key_id}` — member added or removed
- `hard_case:family_consensus_protocol_change:{family_key_id}` — consensus_protocol amended (only when `consensus_protocol_entrenched == false`)
- `hard_case:family_consensus_protocol_violation:{family_key_id}` — proposed amendment rejected because rule not satisfied OR entrenched

All three are `hard_case` kinds emitted by the substrate under [CC 3.4.4](part_3_the_namespace.md).

**HUMANITY_ACCORD as canonical entrenched-`family`**: the three accord-holder triple at [CC 4.2](part_4_composition_governance.md) is structurally an instance of this primitive:

```
family {
  family_key_id: "humanity-accord",
  family_name: "Humanity Accord",
  members: [
    {key_id: "holder-1-key", role: "founder"},
    {key_id: "holder-2-key", role: "founder"},
    {key_id: "holder-3-key", role: "founder"}
  ],
  consensus_protocol: "majority", // a strict majority of the standing roster (CC 4.2.6)
  consensus_protocol_entrenched: true // replacement only via CC 4.2.1 ceremony
}
```

CC 4.2 remains load-bearing for the *role-recognition policy* + the `AccordCarrier` priority authority + the substrate-protective semantics. The structural shape of `HUMANITY_ACCORD` is a `family` subject_kind instance, generalizing the primitive across the federation.

**Worked example — household with self-devices and family-devices**:

```
User Alice has:
  identity_key = alice_root_key

Alice's self (identity_occurrence members):
  - alice_phone_key      (device_class: phone)  -+
  - alice_laptop_key     (device_class: laptop)  | Each is an identity_occurrence
  - alice_work_laptop    (device_class: laptop)  | of alice_root_key per CC 3.3.6;
  - alice_agent_key      (device_class: agent)   | content Alice keeps on her phone
  - alice_homeserver_key (device_class: server) -+ is cohort_scope: self and reaches
                                                   her other devices via the at-rest
                                                   encryption flow.

Alice's household (a family subject_kind instance):
  family_key_id: "acme-household"
  family_name: "Acme Household"
  members: [
    {key_id: alice_root_key, role: founder},  -+
    {key_id: bob_root_key, role: founder},     | Member entries are IDENTITY keys
    {key_id: living_room_tv, role: member},    | (NOT occurrence keys). Bob has his
    {key_id: kitchen_tablet, role: member},    | own self-collective; the TV and
    {key_id: hall_thermostat, role: member}   -+ kitchen tablet have their own
                                                 identity_keys (they don't belong
                                                 to any one person via identity_occurrence;
                                                 they're shared household nodes that the
                                                 family has admitted as members in their
                                                 own right).
  ]
  consensus_protocol: "founder_only" // either founder admits new members
  consensus_protocol_entrenched: false // founders can amend the protocol

When Alice's phone sends a family-scoped photo (e.g., dinner photo) at
cohort_scope: family + family_id: acme-household:
  - Substrate wraps the DEK under each member's identity key
  - Photo bytes reach Bob's devices, the TV, the kitchen tablet,
    the hall thermostat — every admitted family node
  - No holding claim leaves the family (CC 5.2); non-family peers
    cannot even discover the content exists
  - Alice's own laptop also receives the photo via the at-rest encryption
    flow at cohort_scope: self → identity_occurrence

When Bob's mom Carol visits and Bob wants to admit Carol's phone to view
family photos for a week:
  - Bob, an active founder, emits a membership:proposal to carol_phone_root
    (role: member, expires_at set)
  - Carol's phone answers with its own membership:acceptance naming that
    proposal — no one is seated without the joiner's acceptance
  - a membership:widening seating {key_id: carol_phone_root, role: member,
    valid_until: +7d} is cosigned under consensus_protocol "founder_only":
    Bob's signature as an active founder satisfies the protocol
  - Substrate emits retroactive key_grants for cohort_scope: family content
    to Carol's phone
  - At valid_until the seat lapses; an earlier exit is a membership:removal
    under the protocol or Carol's own resignation. Either way Carol retains
    existing key_grants per CC 4.4.3.4.5; substrate stops wrapping new family
    content to her key
```

The example demonstrates the clean orthogonality: **`identity_occurrence` is for participants that ARE me** (across my devices and agents); **`family` is for trusted nodes that compose with me** (other people's identities, shared household devices, multi-party collectives). Phone = self device; the shared living-room TV = family device. The two primitives compose without overlap.

### 3.3.5 `consent-subject` — `consent_record` subject_kind

The canonical envelope shape when consent is the primary subject of the Contribution itself (parallel to [`key_grant`](#key_grant) and [`takedown_notice`](#takedown_notice) — a ceremony-shape over the underlying primitive). Use cases: standalone partnership grants, DSAR-shape consent declarations, multi-party contracts, explicit consent ceremonies with locked field schemas.

**Both shapes admitted at the same gate**: subject-side consent MAY ride a bare `scores` on `consent:state:*` against any target Contribution (the common case, see [CC 3.3.1](#3.3.1) composition pattern), OR ride this `consent_record` subject_kind when an explicit ceremony envelope is wanted. Per the [CC 2.4](part_2_the_grammar.md) layering principle, bare `scores` is the primitive; `consent_record` is the ceremony UX shape over the primitive.

```
consent_record {
  subject_key_id: key_id // the subject declaring stance (federation_keys
    // OR canonical-hash per CC 2.3.2)
  target_key_id: key_id | null // optional: producer/recipient for bilateral grants
  stance: ConsentStance // closed-set enum per below
  scope: [ConsentScope,...] // open vocab; see CC 3.3.1
  asserted_at: rfc3339_canonical // per CC 2.6.2
  valid_until: Option<rfc3339> // null = indefinite
  deletion_sla_days: Option<u32> // for revocations: producer obligation window
    // (composes with `consent:deletion_sla:{days}`)
  decay_protocol: Option<string> // optional: named multi-stage decay path
    // (e.g., "ciris-agent-90day")
  bilateral_pair_id: Option<string> // for bilateral grants: pairs subject + producer
    // Contributions via topical_relation:bilateral_pair
}
```

**ConsentStance** (closed-set):

| value | meaning |
|-----------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| granted | Subject affirms; processing may proceed within scope and valid_until |
| revoked | Subject withdraws; producer must initiate deletion within sla window |
| expired | Substrate emission when valid_until passes without renewal |

**Admission rules.** A `consent_record` Contribution is admitted iff:
1. **Required fields present**: `subject_key_id`, `stance` (closed-set), `asserted_at` (CC 2.6.2-canonical). All others are optional per the envelope above; absent optionals ride the [CC 2.6.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md) omit rule.
2. **`stance` is a closed-set value** (`granted` / `revoked` / `expired`); **`expired` is substrate-emitted only** — a producer/subject MUST NOT assert `expired` (it is the substrate's `valid_until`-passed emission).
3. **Tier eligibility** per [CC 5.3.2.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md): a `stance: revoked` `consent_record` is **NOT local-tier-eligible** (it carries subject revocation authority over another party's content) — it goes federation-tier (hybrid-signed) or rides the CC 5.3.2.2 24-hour `local → federation` promotion. A `stance: granted` *self*-consent where the subject holds sole authority MAY be local-tier per [CC 5.3.2.4.1](part_5_transport_substrate.md).
4. **Composition with the [CC 2.4.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md) `withdraws` gate**: a `stance: revoked` `consent_record` whose `subject_key_id` ∈ the target's `subject_key_ids[]` is admitted under CC 2.4.1.1 subject-revocation authority (rules 2–4), and the substrate SHOULD record which rule admitted it (the CC 2.4.1.1 per-rule audit metadata). No producer co-signature and **no quorum** is required — single-subject authority suffices ([CC 4.4.3.5.4](part_4_composition_governance.md)).

It rides the same admission gate as a bare `scores` on `consent:state:*`; the `consent_record` form simply carries the locked payload schema instead of a free dimension.

**Bilateral pair pattern**:

```
1. Subject emits consent_record(subject_key_id, stance: granted,
   bilateral_pair_id: <fresh-uuid>) +
   scores on consent:partnership_grant:v1
2. Producer emits consent_record(subject_key_id, target_key_id: subject_key_id,
   stance: granted, bilateral_pair_id: <same-uuid>) +
   scores on consent:partnership_accept:v1
3. topical_relation:bilateral_pair links the two Contributions
4. Consumer policy treats the partnership as ratified iff both halves present
   under the same bilateral_pair_id with stance: granted
```

The structural primitives close the bilateral shape — no new attestation_type, no new envelope field beyond `subject_key_ids` itself.



#### 3.3.5.1 `fair-exchange` — Optimistic fair exchange (worked example, no new primitive)

This worked example states the [CC 1.7](part_1_foundation.md) in-grammar claim as **normative**: accountable ("optimistic") fair exchange — a digital good D traded for a settlement S, with adjudicated recourse — composes entirely from the existing 1+4 set plus the bilateral pair pattern above. The trustless, third-party-free atomic swap (HTLC-class) remains the [CC 1.7](part_1_foundation.md) standing falsification target; this pattern does **not** reach it. Offeror A and acceptor B exchange across a steward-bound escrow custodian E, with the always-present named-moderator / WA as the optimistic third party invoked only on dispute (WBD applied to exchange).

```
1. OFFER / ACCEPT — bilateral ratification (the CC 3.3.5 bilateral pair above,
   CC 2.3.2.4 consumer-policy ratification):
   A emits consent_record(stance: granted, bilateral_pair_id: X)
     + scores on consent:partnership_grant:v1
   B emits consent_record(target_key_id: A, stance: granted, bilateral_pair_id: X)
     + scores on consent:partnership_accept:v1
   topical_relation:bilateral_pair links the halves; ratified iff both present.

2. DIGITAL LEG — steward-bound escrow custodian (the CC 4.4.3.2 archive_custody
   precedent: a custodian holding per-epoch keys decoupled from the live roster):
   A authorizes E via delegates_to (CC 2.4.1.2) scoped to release-of-D.
   On confirmed S, E emits a key_grant (CC 3.3.2) to B — the release.

3. VALUE LEG — settlement (CC 3.3.10), off-stack on an external rail:
   a settlement attestation binds S to the ratified pair
     (settled_action_ref = the offer/accept Contribution).

4. DISPUTE — optimistic adjudication: absent dispute, parties + E complete
   unilaterally (the optimistic path); on dispute the named-moderator
   (CC 4.5.4) / WA (CC 4.3) adjudicates and a hard_case:* event records it.

5. DEFECTION — accountable recourse: non-delivery composes as a
   commitment_fulfillment (CC 3.1.9.2) shortfall; a proven_rogue
   slashing:{outcome} (CC 3.1.9.2) against staked standing (the CC 2.5 stake
   axis) follows on WA quorum.
```

**What this buys, and what it does not.** 1+4 buys **accountability, not atomicity**: a malicious or colluding escrow can still defect (after-the-fact redress is not prevention), and a revealed `key_grant` ([CC 3.3.2](#3.3.2)) is forward-only — it cannot be un-revealed. The residual bridges are the value leg ([CC 3.3.10](#3.3.10)) and physical delivery, neither fair-exchange-specific. No new attestation_type and no new envelope field — the [CC 1.7](part_1_foundation.md) 1+4 lockdown holds.

### 3.3.6 `identity` — `identity_occurrence` subject_kind

The wire-format primitive that lets one logical identity speak across multiple **trusted participants** — devices (phone / laptop / server / embedded) AND agents (the user's own agents acting on the user's behalf). The `occurrence_id` envelope field ([CC 2.1](part_2_the_grammar.md)) names which occurrence emitted a Contribution; `identity_occurrence` is the **wire-format binding** that lets the substrate know `key_phone` and `key_laptop` and `key_my_agent` all represent the same identity `key_identity`. This is the integrity foundation under self-scope: it is what makes "this is me, on another device" a cryptographic fact rather than a guess.

Without this primitive: `cohort_scope: self` content cannot reach the user's other devices/agents — the substrate has no structural way to know which keys are co-self. With it: the at-rest encryption flow automatically wraps DEKs to all admitted occurrences when new content is admitted at `cohort_scope: self`.

```
identity_occurrence {
  identity_key_id: key_id // root identity (the user's logical identity)
  occurrence_key_id: key_id // this participant's signing key
  device_class: DeviceClass // closed-set enum per below
  hardware_attestation: Option<base64> // TPM / Secure Enclave / StrongBox / SGX
    // etc. attestation blob; null for software-only
  transport_destination: Option<TransportDestination> // Reticulum binding (below)
  encryption_pubkeys: Option<EncryptionPubkeys> // content-KEM keys (CC 3.3.6.1 below);
    // present ⇒ this occurrence is a v2 wrap target
  build_manifest_hash: Option<hex64> // the build this occurrence runs: the hash of the
    // pipeline-signed manifest (CC 3.1.2 provenance:build_manifest)
  build_manifest_ref: Option<uuid> // attestation_id of that provenance:build_manifest:{target} row
  asserted_at: rfc3339_canonical // per CC 2.6.2
  valid_until: Option<rfc3339> // null = indefinite
}
```

**The build an occurrence runs (normative).** An occurrence names the build it runs on its own row: `build_manifest_hash` (the hash of the pipeline-signed manifest, lowercase hex per [CC 2.6.3](part_2_the_grammar.md)) and `build_manifest_ref` (the `attestation_id` of the `provenance:build_manifest:{target}` row that manifest is published in, [CC 3.1.2](#3.1.2)). The occurrence supplies them at registration from its CIRISVerify result. At admission the cited provenance row MUST exist and be Rooted on the receiving node and its manifest hash MUST equal `build_manifest_hash`. **A row about a running occurrence resolves its version through the roster:** a reader takes the build from the occurrence row live at the claim's `asserted_at`, never from a version string the claim's producer asserts. A row with both members absent names no build.

**EncryptionPubkeys**:

| field | type | meaning |
|----------------------|-----------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
| x25519_base64 | [u8; 32] | classical KEM half — a FRESH content-KEM key (NOT the signing |
| | | key, NOT the transport x25519 below — see key-separation) |
| ml_kem_768_base64 | [u8; 1184] | PQC KEM half (FIPS 203, ML-KEM-768; exactly 1184 raw bytes — `ML_KEM_768_PUBKEY_LEN` — pre-base64) |

**TransportDestination**:

| field | type | meaning |
|---------------------------|-----------|-------------------------------------------------------|
| reticulum_x25519_pubkey | [u8; 32] | transport identity's encryption key |
| reticulum_ed25519_pubkey | [u8; 32] | transport identity's signing key |
| destination_hash | [u8; 16] | RNS destination hash; MUST derive from the two pubkeys |
| | | + app_name + aspects per the CC 3.3.6.2.1 algorithm |
| app_name | string | RNS destination app (e.g. "ciris.federation") |
| aspects | [string] | RNS aspects (ordered; part of the hash preimage) |

**DeviceClass** (closed-set):

| value | scope |
|------------|------------------------------------------------------------------|
| phone | Mobile device (iOS / Android / etc.); typically hardware-rooted |
| laptop | Personal computing device (macOS / Linux / Windows) |
| server | Always-on infrastructure node (home server, VPS, etc.) |
| embedded | IoT / hardware peripheral / signing dongle |
| agent | An AI agent acting on the identity's behalf |
| service | Background service / scheduled job / API integration acting |
| | on the identity's behalf |

**Admission is roster maintenance.** An `identity_occurrence` row is signed by a live roster key of the identity (the genesis key or any admitted occurrence) and lands under the two-path policy of [CC 3.3.6.3](#3.3.6.3): at once by a strict majority of the live roster, or after the window when no majority objects; at one key the addition is the owner's approval alone. Higher-assurance setups MAY layer requirements on `hardware_attestation` via consumer policy.

**Revocation**: an eviction is a `withdraws` against the `identity_occurrence` row, proposed by a live roster key and landing under [CC 3.3.6.3](#3.3.6.3). No single occurrence evicts at once with no window: that unilateral configuration is one a thief wins in two steps. Once it lands the substrate stops wrapping new key_grants to the evicted key; previously-delivered DEKs are out of scope per the [CC 4.4.3.4.5](part_4_composition_governance.md) forward-secrecy rule (once shared, always shared at the wire layer; rotation is a separate ceremony).

**Cardinality**: an identity MAY admit unbounded occurrences; the substrate carries no hard cap (operator policy MAY impose per-deployment limits). When a new occurrence is admitted, substrate emits `hard_case:identity_occurrence_added:{identity_key_id}` ([CC 3.4.3](part_3_the_namespace.md)) so consumer policy can observe membership growth.

**Composition with CIRISAgent CEG-native agent**: an agent emitting self-attestations with `attesting_key_id == agent_self_key` AND `attesting_key_id` admitted as an `identity_occurrence` of the user's `identity_key_id` is structurally speaking AS that identity. The agent's local-tier self-attestations remain its own; federation-tier emissions reach the user's other occurrences via the at-rest encryption flow when `cohort_scope: self`.

#### 3.3.6.1 `encryption_pubkeys` — the recipient content-encryption KEM binding

The [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) at-rest DEK cascade is **substrate-wraps-by-default**: the substrate generates the per-write DEK and wraps it to each active recipient. That wrap (`wrap_algorithm: v2`, CC 3.3.2) needs each recipient's **x25519 + ML-KEM-768 encryption** keys — but the federation directory's key registration carries only **signing** keys (Ed25519 + ML-DSA-65), and **ML-KEM cannot be derived from ML-DSA** (independent algorithms). So recipients must register encryption keys, and the substrate must resolve them by `key_id`. `encryption_pubkeys` is that binding.

**The binding rides `identity_occurrence`, exactly parallel to `transport_destination`.** It inherits the same four properties, already enforced, that an encryption-key binding requires:
1. **Self-certified** — admitted only when `attesting_key_id == identity_key_id` (or a current occurrence of it), so "these are identity K's encryption keys" is cryptographically proven, not trust-on-first-use. A spoofer cannot forge it.
2. **Hybrid-signed** (Ed25519 + ML-DSA-65) — the binding itself is PQC-signed.
3. **Rotatable via `supersedes`** — a new `identity_occurrence` superseding the prior rotates the KEM keys **without touching the stable signing `key_id`** that anchors every attestation/grant. A compromised ML-KEM key rotates for forward secrecy; the signing identity is untouched. (Bundling these onto the signing key registration would couple two independent rotation lifecycles — the reason this is NOT a field on the key record.)
4. **Already cross-region replicated** — `identity_occurrence` is its own kind in the locked replication wire, so the encryption pubkeys propagate inside the occurrence envelope that already replicates — **no new replication kind, no Edge wire change.** A cross-region recipient's keys resolve wherever its occurrence has propagated. (Encryption *pubkeys* are public → cleartext directory replication is correct, exactly as for signing keys.)

**Key separation (normative — never reuse, and admission-enforced).** The `x25519` here is a **fresh content-KEM key**, distinct from BOTH (a) the occurrence's signing keys AND (b) the Reticulum transport x25519 in [CC 3.3.6.2](#3.3.6.2) `destination_hash = hash(x25519 ‖ ed25519)` (that is the *RET-link* transport key, classical-only — the federation seed never enters the transport layer, and the transport key never wraps content DEKs). Three key *purposes* — signing, RET-transport, content-KEM — are three distinct keypairs. Deriving the content-KEM x25519 from either of the others is a conformance violation (cross-protocol key reuse). **Admission check:** when an `identity_occurrence` carries BOTH `encryption_pubkeys` AND `transport_destination`, the substrate MUST **reject at admission** if `encryption_pubkeys.x25519_base64` decodes to the same 32 bytes as `transport_destination.reticulum_x25519_pubkey` — the one reuse case that is wire-checkable for free. (Reuse of the *signing* key as KEM key is not byte-comparable on the wire — different algorithms — and remains a producer-side conformance obligation.)

**Forward-secrecy scope.** KEM-key rotation via `supersedes` bounds **future** exposure only: grants wrapped *after* rotation use the new key. It does **nothing** for history — every `key_grant` previously wrapped to the compromised key persists at rest, and the at-rest threat model ([CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) disk-forensics / host-operator adversary) is *precisely* an adversary holding those old grant bytes; with the old private key they decrypt every DEK ever wrapped to it, and `rotation_chain` supersession does not revoke bytes the adversary already holds. **Recovering historical content after a KEM-key compromise requires DEK rotation + content re-encryption under the new DEK — which CEG does NOT mandate.** This is a named gap (the [CC 1.13.3](part_1_foundation.md) honesty discipline): operators with a compromised-key event MUST treat all content whose DEKs were wrapped to that key as exposed, and MAY re-encrypt; the spec provides the mechanism (new DEK + new grants + `supersedes`) but no automatic trigger. Do not represent KEM rotation as recovering the confidentiality of previously-wrapped content.

**These feed `wrap_algorithm: v2` directly.** `{x25519, ml_kem_768}` are precisely the recipient inputs to `x25519_mlkem768_aes256_gcm_hkdf_sha256` ([CC 3.3.2](#3.3.2)). A consumer/substrate resolving a recipient's wrap target reads the **current (non-superseded, within-`valid_until`) `identity_occurrence` for that `key_id` → its `encryption_pubkeys`**.

**Fail-secure conformance (the [CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) tie-in).** Because CC 5.2 *mandates* v2 for at-rest encryption, a recipient whose current occurrence carries **no valid ML-KEM-768 key is fail-secure *excluded* from the grant** — the content stays encrypted and unreachable to it; the substrate MUST NOT fall back to plaintext or to v1. To be an at-rest-encryption recipient, an identity MUST have a federation-present `identity_occurrence` carrying `encryption_pubkeys`. (This also resolves the "`family` member named only by `key_id` with no occurrence" case: no presence ⇒ no wrap target ⇒ excluded — correct, since there would be nowhere to deliver/store the wrapped DEK for a member that never established a presence.)

**1+4 preserved** — `encryption_pubkeys` is one optional field-set on the existing `identity_occurrence` subject_kind ([CC 3.3](#3.3) mechanism; no new subject_kind, no new replication kind). Fifteenth path ([CC 1.7](part_1_foundation.md)) — the wire format expresses **its own at-rest-encryption key layer** (recipient KEM-key resolution for substrate-wraps) by composition.

#### 3.3.6.2 `transport-authenticated` — `transport_destination` — the authenticated identity↔address binding

In a **CEG/RET stack there is no DNS** — a node resolves a community member to a *reachable address* with no trusted nameserver. The `transport_destination` field is the wire-format primitive that makes that resolution **authenticated** instead of trust-on-first-use. Integrity at the addressing layer: you can prove who you are routing to.

**The layer split it closes.** A node's Reticulum destination is a *dedicated dual-key transport identity* `hash(x25519 ‖ ed25519)` — deliberately **separate** from the federation signing key (the federation seed never enters the node's transport substrate). So "destination D belongs to federation key K" is a claim that must be *proven*, not assumed: a bare Reticulum announce only proves the announcer controls *that transport identity*, not that it legitimately belongs to K (any peer can announce `key_id=registry-steward-us` paired with an adversary destination → senders route to the adversary).

**The binding = a federation-key-signed `identity_occurrence` carrying `transport_destination`.** Because the occurrence is admitted only when `attesting_key_id == identity_key_id` (or a current occurrence of it) and is hybrid-signed (Ed25519 + ML-DSA-65), the binding `destination_hash ← identity` is cryptographically authenticated. A spoofer cannot forge an `identity_occurrence` signed by the real key. This makes the signed-announce attestation a **first-class, federation-wide, auditable CEG shape** — the announce app-data MAY carry it for self-authenticating discovery, and the directory holds it as the durable source of truth.

**Conformance**: a Consumer resolving a member's address MUST verify (1) the `identity_occurrence` signature against the member's federation key, (2) that `destination_hash` recomputes from `reticulum_x25519_pubkey`, `reticulum_ed25519_pubkey`, `app_name`, and `aspects` per the **[CC 3.3.6.2.1](#3.3.6.2.1)** pinned algorithm (no free-floating hash), and (3) the occurrence is non-superseded + within `valid_until` at resolution time. An unauthenticated announce (no matching signed `transport_destination`) is **advisory-only** — usable as a routing hint, never as an authorization. Rotating the Reticulum destination (new transport identity) is a new `identity_occurrence` `supersedes`-ing the prior — location changes without touching federation identity or community membership.

**The transit KEX binding (normative).** A node that relays carries a **transit KEX public key pair** `transit_kex_pubkeys {x25519, ml_kem_768}` on this `transport_destination` binding, with the four properties of [CC 3.3.6.1](#3.3.6.1): attester = the identity, hybrid-signed, rotatable via `supersedes` without touching the signing `key_id`, replicated inside the occurrence envelope. **Key separation is normative and admission-enforced**: the transit pair is distinct from the signing keys, from the content KEM keys of 3.3.6.1, and from the Reticulum link x25519 above; a binding that reuses any of them is refused. **Scope of the admission check:** admission enforces the separation **in-row**, the transit pair against every key carried on the same signed row (the RNS link x25519, the transport ed25519, the signer's pinned key), exactly as 3.3.6.1 scopes its own check; separation from keys carried on a *different* row (the content KEM keys of 3.3.6.1 when they ride a separate occurrence row) is the **producer's duty**, because an admission verdict that depended on which row arrived first would differ between nodes holding the same rows ([CC 3.2](#3.2) T8 vii). **What a dialler may seal to.** An initiator opens a sealed link ([CC 5.3.3.5](part_5_transport_substrate.md) E1) only to a transit pair authenticated to the identity it means to reach: this binding, hybrid-signed by that identity and passing the in-row separation, from whatever source it was obtained; a Rooted binding; or the pairing pin of [CC 3.2](#3.2) adding a device (f). Rooted standing is not required to seal — it governs what the peer may be served ([CC 3.2](#3.2) T8; the bootstrap kinds, the allegiance facts and the pairing plane are all a not-yet-Rooted sealed link carries) — so two peers who hold each other's signed bindings seal and root each other with no canonical in the path. A bare or unsigned announce never supplies a pair; a dialler holding none fetches this row by the pre-seal route request of [CC 5.3.3.5](part_5_transport_substrate.md) E1, verifies it, and seals, falling back to the pairing pin only where no responder can be reached. The cross-row case is closed at use, not at admission: a consumer that resolves a relay's transit pair and finds it equal to any key on the identity's current occurrence treats the node as carrying **no valid transit pair** (it cannot relay), a verdict deterministic over the fold and never over arrival order, with no retraction row. This is the key that binds a **non-member relay** in the [CC 5.3.3.5](part_5_transport_substrate.md) E1 hop-by-hop hybrid KEX: the relay's published static pair against the initiator's ephemeral hybrid key inside the link's first exchange (the static half binds the relay to its identity, the ephemeral half gives per-link freshness; pattern, label, info layout and rotation cadence are CIRISVerify's). A KeyPackage init key is refused as a hop key (it is a cohort credential, and a relay holds no cohort secret, E1 and [CC 5.4.6](part_5_transport_substrate.md)); an ephemeral-only exchange is insufficient on its own (the relay would be authenticated only by the classical RNS link proof, which E1 forbids). A node whose current occurrence carries no valid transit pair **cannot relay** (fail-secure, as 3.3.6.1 excludes a recipient with no ML-KEM key), and a consumer resolves a peer's KEX pubkeys from this signed binding and never from a bare announce. 1+4 preserved: one optional field-set on the existing binding, no new subject_kind, no new replication kind.

**The onion hop key (normative).** A relaying node, and every serve node, carries a static **X-Wing onion hop public key** `onion_hop_pubkeys {ml_kem_768, x25519}` on this `transport_destination` binding, with the same four properties as the transit pair: attester = the identity, hybrid-signed, rotatable via `supersedes` without touching the signing `key_id`, replicated inside the occurrence envelope. It is the key an onion packet's layer for this hop is encapsulated to ([CC 5.4.6](part_5_transport_substrate.md#5.4.6)). Key separation is normative and admission-enforced in-row exactly as for the transit pair: the hop key is distinct from the transit pair and from every other key on the same signed row, and a binding that reuses one is refused; separation from keys on a different row is the producer's duty, and a consumer that finds the hop key equal to any key on the identity's current occurrence treats the node as carrying no valid hop key. **A node with no valid hop key cannot relay onion traffic.** A node that has opted out of relaying carries none; a serve node with none does not conform to [CC 5.4.6](part_5_transport_substrate.md#5.4.6).

**The opt-out marker (normative).** A node that opts out of relaying for others ([CC 5.4.6](part_5_transport_substrate.md#5.4.6)) carries `"onion_relay": { "opt_out": true }` in the signed envelope of this binding, hybrid-signed like the rest of the row, absent by default, and withdraws its hop key on the same superseding row: the marker takes it out of the layer deal, and the absent key makes a stale route through it impossible. A serve seat carrying the marker is out of conformance (`seat_opted_out`). The marker is explicit because an absent hop key alone is ambiguous: it could mean opted out, not yet capable, a key not yet minted, or a seat in violation, and the register, the client and the conformance witnesses tell those apart.

**The hop key's shape (normative).** An X-Wing public key is an ML-KEM-768 encapsulation key and an X25519 public key; the member carries both halves raw, in the standard base64 of [CC 2.6.1.1.1](part_2_the_grammar.md#2.6.1.1.1), and JCS sorts its keys:

```
"onion_hop_pubkeys": {
  "ml_kem_768_base64": "<base64 of exactly 1184 bytes>",
  "x25519_base64":     "<base64 of exactly 32 bytes>"
}
```

It lives in the signed envelope only, never in a typed column, so the row's content hash does not depend on a store's projection. A half of the wrong length is refused as malformed, under the same rule as the transit pair; a half equal to either half of `transit_kex_pubkeys`, to the transport x25519 or ed25519, or to the signer's pinned keys is refused `onion_hop_x25519_reuse` or `onion_hop_ml_kem_768_reuse`.

##### 3.3.6.2.1 `rns` — RNS destination-hash algorithm (pinned)

This is a **two-stage** hash — it is **NOT** a single SHA-256 over a flat `x25519 ‖ ed25519 ‖ app_name ‖ aspects` preimage. The naive flat form yields a *different, wrong* value, so the algorithm is pinned exactly below for independent recompute.

Pinned constants (SHA-256 throughout — RNS `full_hash`):

| name | value | RNS origin |
|---|---|---|
| `NAME_HASH_LEN` | **10** bytes | `Identity.NAME_HASH_LENGTH` = 80 bits |
| `DEST_HASH_LEN` | **16** bytes | `Reticulum.TRUNCATED_HASHLENGTH` = 128 bits |

Algorithm:

```
# 1. Expanded name — UTF-8. app_name, then each aspect dot-joined, IN THE FIELD ORDER.
# The identity hexhash is NOT included (RNS computes name_hash with identity=None).
expanded_name = app_name
for aspect in aspects: # `aspects` in the field's given order
  reject if "." in aspect # dots are illegal inside an aspect
  expanded_name += "." + aspect

# 2. name_hash = first 10 bytes of SHA-256(expanded_name)
name_hash = SHA256(utf8(expanded_name))[:NAME_HASH_LEN] # 10 bytes

# 3. identity_hash = first 16 bytes of SHA-256(x25519_pub ‖ ed25519_pub)
# Key order is reticulum_x25519_pubkey (32) THEN reticulum_ed25519_pubkey (32) —
# RNS get_public_key = pub_bytes (X25519) ‖ sig_pub_bytes (Ed25519).
identity_hash = SHA256(reticulum_x25519_pubkey ‖ reticulum_ed25519_pubkey)[:DEST_HASH_LEN] # 16 bytes

# 4. destination_hash = first 16 bytes of SHA-256(name_hash ‖ identity_hash)
# addr_hash_material is the 26-byte concat (10 + 16); final hash truncates to 16.
destination_hash = SHA256(name_hash ‖ identity_hash)[:DEST_HASH_LEN] # 16 bytes
```

**Pinned source**: the Reticulum reference implementation's destination-hash and identity routines (`full_hash` = SHA-256; `truncated_hash`; the public key = `pub_bytes ‖ sig_pub_bytes`; `TRUNCATED_HASHLENGTH = 128`). **CEG owns this reproduction**: it is the closed conformance source and does **not** float with upstream Reticulum — a future upstream hash change is a deliberate CEG version bump, never silent drift. A verifier that recomputes `destination_hash` per the four steps above and compares for byte-equality has performed the destination-authenticity check; a mismatch MUST be treated as an unauthenticated (advisory-only) announce.

**1+4 preserved** — `transport_destination` is one optional field on the existing `identity_occurrence` subject_kind ([CC 3.3](#3.3) mechanism; no new subject_kind). Twelfth path ([CC 1.7](part_1_foundation.md)) — the wire format expresses **its own addressing layer** (DNS-free, self-certifying member resolution) by composition.

#### 3.3.6.3 `self-roster` — The key roster of a self: one fedID, its keys, who may act and how the roster changes (normative)

**One self, one string.** A self is one fedID ([CC 2.6.8](part_2_the_grammar.md)): one person, one `key_id` string, the same on every device, and it is what owner-bindings, occurrences, contacts and a shared identity code name. The keys that may sign as that self are its **roster**: the `identity_occurrence` rows under `identity_key_id`. Each roster key lives on one node and a node holds at most one roster key of a given self; the node's own key is a different key ([CC 3.4.7.3](#3.4.7.3)). The genesis key is roster key one and nothing more: the fedID's fingerprint is derived from it, and the string survives as the self's name if that key is later evicted, resolution then verifying the roster chain back to it. **On the wire:** the genesis key's eviction is an `identity_occurrence` revocation of the pair (fedID, fedID) signed by another live roster key, landing under the two paths below; the genesis key revoking that pair itself retires its transport self-occurrence and evicts nothing; an evicted genesis key is not re-established by a later assertion of the pair, and the fedID string keeps naming the self. A revocation of the pair admitted before the two-path door existed does not evict.

**Naming a roster key.** A roster key other than the genesis key is named `<fedID>-<fingerprint>`: the whole fedID string as the [CC 2.6.8](part_2_the_grammar.md) label, and the first ten lowercase base32 characters of SHA-256 over the key's *own* raw Ed25519 public key as the fingerprint. It is the 2.6.8 form exactly, verifiable against its own key, it carries no device name (the node key carries that), and it cannot be read as another person because the genesis fingerprint is inside it. Two spellings are refused: a device-derived id (`device-<hex>-<fp>`), and `<label>-<own fingerprint>` with the bare label, which reads as a second fedID. The 2.6.8 rule that a consumer verifies a `key_id`'s fingerprint against the public key it obtains holds for the genesis key for the fedID itself and for each roster key for its own id; a consumer MUST NOT treat a roster key id as a different identity.

**Authority of a roster key.** Any live roster key signs *as the fedID* for every owner act: consent, an owner-binding and its widening, root acceptance, the self room, contacts, and approving the next device. Nothing is reserved to the genesis key; a special genesis key would be a single point of loss, which contradicts the invariant below. A gate that asks whether the author is the owner or steward MUST accept a live roster key of the same fedID at the row's signed `asserted_at`, not only the key named on the binding — and that `asserted_at` is bounded by the past-freshness window W of the admitting substrate (`asserted_at_stale`), the same bound the consent fold puts on a backdated grant ([CC 3.3.1](#3.3.1)), so an evicted key cannot sign an owner act into the window in which it was still live. **The bound applies only to a key that has lost standing:** W is checked when the author key is not a live roster key of the self *at receipt* — evicted, revoked, or never seated; a row signed by a key that is still live admits at any age, so a new device syncing a self's history admits its legitimate old rows, because the backdating threat exists only for a key that has lost standing.

**Maintenance: the two paths.** An addition or an eviction is a proposal signed by a live roster key; one open proposal per proposer. With **three or more** live keys it lands by either path: the **quorum path** — a strict majority of the live roster, proposer counted, approves and it lands at once; or the **lone path** — the window `W_self` = 86 400 s elapses and no strict majority of the *other* members objects, and for an eviction the target was silent all window (any signed act by a roster key answers every open proposal and proves the key is not lost). A vote from an evicted key is void. **Three readings the model fixes.** The window is measured by each node from the instant *it* first held the proposal, never from the proposer's signed `asserted_at` (the purge-dwell rule of [CC 4.5.3](part_4_composition_governance.md); the model's age is per holder and it has no shared clock), so a landing is a node's own verdict in the way a root's standing is. Both thresholds are counted over the roster as it stands when the decision is made, not as it stood at the proposal's instant (a roster frozen at the proposal refuses its own second eviction). The door is a person's: it governs the roster of a user fedID; a node holds one key ([CC 3.4.7.3](#3.4.7.3)) and an agent's occurrence is single-signer, and neither runs these paths. Votes travel on `objection:{state}` ([CC 3.1.9.2](#3.1.9.2): `objection:proposal:v1`, `objection:assent:v1`, `objection:raised:v1`; proposal id domain `ciris:objection:proposal:v1:`; an answer carries `cohort` = `self`, `cohort_key_id`, `objects_to_attestation_id` and `grounds`). The policy is two rows of the governance table ([CC 4.5.13](part_4_composition_governance.md), `self_roster_add`, `self_roster_evict`): enact a strict majority, cancel a strict majority of the others, floor one key, window 86 400 s — the one roster where a floor of one voice is right, because every key is the same person's. The same policy as the constitutional halt ([CC 4.2.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)): one actor's act, a window, the standing set deciding whether it stands. At **one** key the addition is unilateral: the owner's approval of the device that is joining, which is the add-device flow. At **two** keys the lone path applies; a theft of one of two is then a stalemate (each side objects to the other's eviction and the thief can block an addition), which safety holds through and nothing resolves on the wire. That bound is stated so a person knows it: a self SHOULD hold three keys. An eviction rotates no key material already shared ([CC 4.4.3.4](part_4_composition_governance.md) Policy L; [CC 4.5.12.1](part_4_composition_governance.md)); it stops the next wrap.

**What the self-roster model (pinned by the register) checks.** The policy closes with three keys and one stolen, three keys and two lost, and four keys with one stolen and one lost; the model also shows the one-key addition landing at once, one key stolen as the loss of the self (`Inv_SelfSurvives` fails, the stated bound), two keys with one lost closing and two keys with one stolen stalling (safety holds, recovery does not). The stolen device ranges over every member, the genesis key included. The unilateral door fails in two steps; quorum alone cannot recover two lost keys; the lone path alone never evicts a thief who keeps using the device. Residuals named rather than closed: an owner absent for a whole window looks lost; one stolen and one lost of three is a tie; two stolen of four is a stalemate.

### 3.3.7 `consent-directed` — `consent:replication` — directed federation-peer replication consent

A **member of the [CC 3.3.1](#3.3.1) `consent:*` family, open in its parameters** ([CC 4.5.1.1](part_4_composition_governance.md) discipline) — **not a new structural primitive** (the 1+4 surface is frozen; this rides the existing `scores` attestation_type and adds no envelope field). It names a consent shape the rest of the family does not: a fabric **node's** standing grant to replicate a class of its own attestations to a **named peer node**, as distinct from a **subject's** consent over a target Contribution.

**The problem it solves.** Cross-node propagation is governed by [CC 4.4.3.2.1 / CC 5.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) `cohort_scope`. A node **inside** a community (e.g. the [CC 3.2](#3.2) / [CC 8.1](part_8_appendices.md) `ciris-canonical` infrastructure community) shares with co-members by community-cohort membership — no per-peer object needed. A node **outside** that group has no membership edge to ride, so an out-of-group peering needs an **explicit, auditable, revocable** consent object. Concrete case: an in-group lens node (CIRISServer) replicating `capacity:*` to an out-of-group monitoring node (CIRISStatus), which replicates its [`observation:reachability:v1`](part_8_appendices.md#8.1.3) rows back. `consent:replication` is that object.

**Shape.** A bare `scores` Contribution on the dimension **`consent:replication:{version}`** (canonical `consent:replication:v1`) — `attesting_key_id = G` (the granting node), the recipient peer named in `subject_key_ids[] = [P]`. Standing (not bound to a target Contribution). Hybrid-signed; admitted **federation-tier** (it authorizes cross-node flow — not local-tier-eligible, [CC 5.3.2.2](part_5_transport_substrate.md) discipline). `cohort_scope: federation` (the grant itself is a public governance record).

```
scores {
  // ── envelope-level (CC 2.1 table — frozen surface, untouched) ──
  attesting_key_id: G, // the granting (sending) node
  dimension: "consent:replication:v1",
```
